# Changelog

All notable changes to **Mørkyn** (formerly Mørkyn) are documented here.

This project follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html), with one documented deviation for hotfix numbering.

### Version Number Guide
- **MAJOR** (`x.0.0`) - Breaking changes to public APIs or saved data formats
- **MINOR** (`0.x.0`) - New features, backward-compatible
- **PATCH** (`0.0.x`) - Bug fixes, backward-compatible
- **HOTFIX** (a digit appended to PATCH) - Appended to the patch number rather than added as a fourth component, so `0.9.1` and `0.9.10` name the same release: `0.9.11` is its first hotfix and `0.9.12` its second. This is the one place the scheme departs from strict SemVer, and it means a later `0.9.2` sorts *below* `0.9.11` in any tool that parses versions. Nothing in the project does - the launchers compare commits against `origin/<branch>` and `APP_VERSION` is only ever displayed - so the ordering is cosmetic. Do not "correct" a hotfix number to the next patch.
- **PRE-RELEASE** (`0.x.0-dev.N`) - Work between stable releases ships as numbered dev builds, so each push can be told apart. New entries always go under `## [Unreleased]` at the top. When a batch is pushed, that section is renamed `## [0.x.0-dev.N] - <date>` with a one-line summary, `APP_VERSION` in `app/main.py` and the README version line are set to match, and the commit is tagged `v0.x.0-dev.N`. N only goes up. SemVer sorts these correctly: `0.10.0-dev.2` < `0.10.0-dev.10` < `0.10.0`. The stable `0.x.0` is cut from the last dev build. The 0.10 work up to 2026-10-07 was split into `dev.1`-`dev.6` by each entry's commit date.

### Entry Format

```text
- [AGENT_ID] Description of change - module/system name included
```

---

## [Unreleased]

> New entries go here. When a batch is pushed this section becomes the next `0.10.0-dev.N`.

### Changed

- [CLAUDE] Job offers are found by the engine and checked by two small asks instead of one parser reading the whole turn (standing rule 2026-10-18: engine first, checkers rather than narrator prompt lines). Over the 141 8B runs the one-shot quest parser proposed a job in 355 of its 360 calls, 112 were created, and a rule was added after it almost every run; the wrong ones came from the player's own words, debt talk and bare narrator marks. Now `app/quest_offers.py` finds the spoken lines that carry job words and who speaks them (the parser's own vocabulary, one speaker's lines in a paragraph read together), drops the known hard shapes in code (a question back about what work is wanted, a demand to prove oneself, the player paying for a service, directions to a person), asks the model to fill one card from that one line, asks it again whether the giver offers that task to the player in the passage, and only then runs the engine's own validation (giver, duplicates, retold offers, reward caps, the player's acceptance). A step of an active job shown done is checked the same way. Narrator QUEST marks only point at a giver's line; a mark with no spoken line behind it makes nothing. Replayed on the 360 archived turns, every wrong job has no candidate or is dropped before the model, and the real offers are found. `AI_RPG_QUEST_PARSER=legacy` keeps the old one-shot parser; `0` turns the asks off. Each ask is traced under `quest_fill`, `quest_check` and `quest_progress_check`. - `app/quest_offers.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] The place the player leaves is not where they go (8B run 146, T49). "I leave the flats before the tide covers them and set off north" moved the player into The Flats; a known place named after an origin word (leave, from, out of) with no going word between is the origin, read the way a place code in the prose already is. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A new place the turn never shows is not stored (8B run 146, T51). LOC_NEW "The Bridge at the Hollow" was made while the prose walked a muddy trail and the move went elsewhere; a new place neither the prose nor the player's line names, that the turn does not travel to, is dropped, as a new person the prose never shows already is. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A name said of someone absent makes no person here (8B run 146, T53, T55). "There is no sign of Rolf Joliffe" minted Rolf at the stream, and "calling out to Rolf Joliffe ... No one answers" then moved him to the clearing; a mention with an absence before it (no sign of, looking for, calling out to, not) or after it (is not here, never answers) does not show the person. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A venue opens only in a settlement (8B run 146, T56). "Walk to the well" in a clearing in the woods opened a venue "The clearing Well" and moved the player into it; a place with no stored size, no venues, no settlement word and not the start is wild, and its well is a thing there. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Looking breaks no bones (8B run 146, T57). A bad miss on a perception search took a hit point as "Botched Perception: you hurt your leg"; a mental skill with no tool in hand has no injury on a mishap, as social skills already have none. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A yes that waits on terms is no yes (8B run 146, T62). "I will hear your terms before I agree to any of them" accepted the open job; the clause after before/until/unless/if/whether/once is cut before the accept readers run. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone else's verb rolls no check (8B run 146, T49). "before the tide covers them" de-inflected to "cover them" and rolled support; a word ending in -s after "the <noun>" or he/she/it/they stays as typed in the de-inflected readings, so only the player's own act triggers. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player having to pay later backs no gain (8B run 146, T34). "The deeper you go, the more you'll have to pay" let a model GOLD +34 through while nothing reached the player; a future or conditional "you'll have to pay" is a promise, not coin moving. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A thing known, said or seen is no thing carried (8B run 146, T43). GRANT "information about the Drowned Hand" entered the pack; a grant whose head noun is information, knowledge, a clue, a secret, a look, a ride or a service is refused before the arrival test. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A card whose task is the player's own words is dropped (8B run 146, T34). The fill took "Take me out to the wreck and show me what is buried" from the player's quoted line and the check said yes; the task or title found in the player's line ends the card before the check. - `app/quest_offers.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sentence tagged as the player's speech is no progress evidence however the prose splits it (8B run 146, T42). - `app/quest_offers.py`
- [CLAUDE] Typed acceptances reach the engine in checked mode (8B run 146, T3 and T14). "I'll take one" and "The captain's ledger, then. I'll find it" left the one open offer standing: the one-shot parser used to propose these accepts, the checked flow proposed none. The engine now proposes the accept of the one offer the line can mean; the player's-own-words check still applies. - `app/quest_offers.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A line that opens on a time of day skips the clock to it (8B run 146, T24). "In the morning I take the ledger down to Emery" at the inn in the evening left the clock where it was. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A bargain is read across the whole speech (8B run 146, T16). '"... I have twelve to my name." I set six gold coins on his counter. "Six for both, or I walk."' rolled nothing because the sum and the dealing word were in different sentences. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sum set down stands as paid only when the other side takes it (8B run 146, T16). Six gold set on the counter and refused ("You'll pay for it") stays the player's; coin whose taking the prose shows (fingers close around it, pockets it, counts it) is paid. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Leaving a thing is not leaving a place (8B run 146, T17). "I leave the six gold on his counter ... I turn for the door" counted as a journey and kept a MOVE to the wreck the prose never showed. A journey leaves a place (town, dock, shop, here) or sets off. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A GRANT whose every word is in an owned item's name is that item, take or no take (8B run 146, T22). "I take the ledger back out of Wulfstan's hands" with GRANT "ledger" put "ledger x2" beside the owned Captain's Ledger. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The noun "bargain" is not the act of bargaining (8B run 146, T24). "Hold him to the Drowned Hand's side of the bargain" rolled appraise and fumbled it; a determiner before the word marks the noun and triggers nothing, while "I haggle over the pelt" still appraises. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A walk inside a line led by another intent still resolves (8B run 145, T45). "We row back to the dock ... I carry the box up through town to the tally house and set it on the desk" was a trade turn by its words and the walk was never resolved, so the player stayed at the wreck while the prose arrived at the tally house and the next turn stood the tallyman at the wreck. A line that names a known place as where it goes resolves movement whatever intent leads. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A departure shows a move to a place made this turn, not to a known place the prose never names (8B run 146, T10). Probing the flats with an oar stood the player back at the known wreck. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A thing striking a thing is no blow of the player's (8B run 146, T11). "Work the hollow with the oar until the blade strikes something solid" rolled melee against the crewman. - `app/skill_checks.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] One speaker is one candidate (8B run 146, T2). The tallyman's two paragraphs cost two fills and two checks; a known speaker's lines across the turn are read together. - `app/quest_offers.py`
- [CLAUDE] A duty kept is no take (8B run 145, T41). "I keep the lookout" put "lookout x1" in the pack as taken-as-asked and let a model GRANT through as asked for. Keeping the lookout, the watch, one's word or the pace asks for nothing. - `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's own words said back are no result (8B run 145, T38). The prose echoed '"... Done," you say' and the progress check finished "Join a team for a deep dive" on it, with 13 XP. The progress finder skips sentences holding the player's quoted words. - `app/quest_offers.py`
- [CLAUDE] Arranging the work is not the work (8B run 145, T36). "Only if you're willing to work for it" was filed as "Salvage Rights Claim" with the steps Confirm / Discuss / Agree. A card whose every step only arranges the work names no task, unless the giver's own line names a job. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Lore nouns read the line as typed (8B run 145, T34). "Feet planted in the mud" de-inflected to "plant" and rolled nature; nature, history, religion and arcana now trigger on their nouns as typed only, as symbol lore already did. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A wait lasts until its hour (8B run 145, T17-T22). "Rest until the evening tide" passed twenty minutes, the clock stayed at noon through the wait, the row out and the dive, and the clock-word repair rewrote the prose's evening to "the sun climbs higher ... the first stars pierce the twilight". A wait, rest, sit or sleep "until / for the <time of day>" in the narrated part of the line now advances the clock to that hour (a time already past is tomorrow's; a tide with no hour is a quarter day). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A fighting verb with a direction is motion, not a blow (8B run 145, T24). "Kick back up to Bennet's boat for air" resolved as an unarmed hit on Bennet and took 4 of his health. Kick, strike, punch, shove, push or shoot followed by a particle (back, up, off, away, out, free, down ...) is no attack for the turn's intent or the energy cost. - `app/world.py`, `app/player_resources.py`
- [CLAUDE] Only a contest has another side (8B run 145, T24). A search of the hull rolled perception "vs Bennet Harrow" because his name was in the line. A person named in the line is the other side of a social roll, stealth or sleight of hand only. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The one person left behind does not answer for someone called on elsewhere (8B run 145, T28). From the inn, "go down to the dock to find Orme and ask ..." rolled persuasion vs Hilde the innkeeper, the only one in the scene record; a line that names anyone known at another place now rolls against nobody here. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's own stated payment is never voided (8B run 145, T21). "I count six gold coins into the bargeman's hand" for a passage, beside a refused GRANT "crate", was read as a purchase with nothing bought and the 6 gold stayed. Only a model GOLD loss tied to refused goods is voided. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A price named to someone is a bargain (8B run 145, T19). '"Six gold, there and back ... Do we have a deal?"' rolled nothing; a sum with a dealing word now rolls persuasion, the full roll. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player needing help is no work offered to the player (8B run 145, T11). "That's a lot to carry. You'll need help getting it up the steps" was filed as the job "Carry the Crate Upstairs"; the fill and check asks both said yes. "You'll need help / a hand" is blanked from a line before its job words are read, like work others ask for and work refused. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A dive is an act (8B run 145, T9). "Go over the side and dive on the wreck to bring up anything of value" was the active job's own work and a risky act, and no die was thrown: dive was no act family and no athletics trigger. Swimming and diving are an act now (athletics), so the dice roll it and the job's step is matched when the job's own title names the act; "The dive. I'll take it." still rolls nothing. - `app/prose_state.py`, `app/quests.py`, `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] "Bring up anything of value" asks for the chest it brings up (8B run 145, T9). The find was refused as taken without the player asking, and the prose about it was cut, because the generic ask was read only as "anything ... worth". Of value, of use, valuable, useful and salvageable count too. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Speech is no doorway (8B run 145, T7). '"Anything missing, Captain, or are we good to go out?"' read as a doorway exit and stood the player in the street while the scene stayed at the table. Both doorway readings now take the narrated part of the line and only the speech that asks to go, through the same helper the destination rules use. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A named move the prose never shows, that the player never asked for, does not happen (8B run 145, T6). "I go aboard, check the dive lines for fraying" at the Harbour Dock came back with MOVE "Salvage Guild's Tally House"; nothing in the line or the prose went there, the move was flagged as a prose mismatch and applied all the same, and the save walked the player back to the tally house. Such a move is now dropped unless the line sets off on a journey, the line names the place, the prose reaches it, or the prose walks indoors. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A list to choose from is no task yet (8B run 143, T2). "The guild has a list of tasks. You'll need to choose one that fits your skills", with the options only in the narration, was filed as the job "Choose a Task" with the steps "Choose dockside salvage", "Choose warehouse inventory"; the check ask said yes. A line that asks the player to choose one is dropped before the fill, and a card whose every step is choosing names no task; the job is the one the player picks, offered when the giver names it. - `app/quest_offers.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] The craft noun alone is not the craft (#102 class, 8B run 142 T5). "I carry the rusted compass and the weathered ledger back up to the tally house" rolled navigation on the word compass, failed on a 2, and hurt the player's leg. The four noun-only craft triggers (navigation, cartography, farming, tinkering) now want the thing used: the skill's own verb, or a using verb on the thing. A compass carried, a map pocketed, a farm walked across or a mechanism looked at rolls nothing; "take a bearing with the compass", "sketch a map", "plow the field", "fix the mechanism" still do, and a trap carried and then set is still set. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The quest gate reads the current step, not only the title (8B run 142 T4). The player worked "Locate the drowned town's wreckage along the coast" while the prose never said "Dredging the Drowned Town", so the gate stayed shut and the progress check never ran. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A bare narrator QUEST mark no longer reaches the engine through the apply step in checked mode (8B run 142 T2). The offer pass had already read the mark as a hint; world.apply_turn merged it again on its own. Both merge points now use one rule. - `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/quest_offers.py`
- [CLAUDE] A check's opponent is someone the engine places in the scene (#89, reopened twice). The stale opponent was patched three times by phrasing (left behind, walked away from, asked past) and came back each run with a new one. The roll's other side is now drawn from the engine's own record of who is here this turn (`conversation.resolve`: present and addressed); a partner left at the gate, someone asked after who is elsewhere, or the companion when the line asks someone else is never the one rolled against. With no scene record the location's roster stands in as before. - `app/skill_checks.py`


## [0.10.0-dev.13] - 2026-10-17

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. The rest of the Saltmarrow fixes (#100-#122), then Saltmarrow replayed with a local model as the narrator, 43 runs on Qwen2.5 7B and 141 on Qwen3 8B, fixing each fault by class: 377 fixes in all. Places reached, passed or only mentioned are told apart. People stand where the prose shows them, keep their names, jobs and sex, and come along when you walk. Jobs are filed only when someone offers a real task, once per offer, from the person who offered it. Gold and items move only on evidence in the scene. Lines and acts the narrator writes for you are removed. The README gains a Features section. Not yet clean: about one state fault per run in the first seven turns, and the narrator still writes your feelings and past.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Work in general is no task (8B run 141, T2). The tallyman restated the player's debt ("But the guild has work.") and then named the job ("You could help recover a shipment from the docks"), and both were filed: the shipment and "Work Off the Winter's Credit", whose one step was "Work for the Guild". The phrase lists for steps that name no task are now one rule: a job whose steps hold only work, employer and debt words (and the giver's own name and job) names no task yet. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A line the prose gives the player is no job offer (8B run 140, T1). The prose voiced the player's walk-in request as 'You say, "I need to speak with the tallyman about work to pay my debt."', the parser quoted it as the tallyman's offer, and "Work to Pay My Debt" was offered before he had said a word. The offer check skipped only lines the player typed; a quoted line the prose tags as said by "you" (the same speaker reading the speech keeper uses) is now the player's too, for the offer line and for the player's-own-words check. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A plain title is no one job (8B run 139, T2). The tallyman offered work sorting the wreck on the docks, the parser filed it as "Salvage Job", and it was refused as a duplicate of the "Salvage Job" a chandler had offered in the opening, for the drowned district. The job was never recorded. From another giver, a near-identical title now needs the first steps to share a word as well before the offer is called a duplicate. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] The one talked to comes along to an unnamed speaker (8B run 138, T1). The player walked into the tally house "to ask the tallyman"; the ops wrote TALK "A" for Leofric, already met in town, and the prose showed only "A man sits behind the counter ... 'You're late,' he says". Leofric was kept in town because the prose never named him, and the next turn sat a new clerk at his desk. When the walk ends with an unnamed speaker, no one known is named there, and the ops talk with someone standing at the place left, that person now stands at the arrival. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place passed on the way in is not the one entered (8B run 137, T1). "You step past the Collapsed Market Stall [[L2]] and into the salvage yard", then on into the tally house, stood the player in the stall: the walked-in venue rule took any place named in a sentence with "into". Places only passed (past, by, beyond, passing) are now blanked before that rule names the venue, through the same helper the named-move checks use. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] An acceptance given to the player goes (8B run 136, T3). After the agent named the job, the prose wrote the player's thoughts ("The offer hangs between you like a weight. You know the Wrecked Reach ...") and then a bare '"I'll take it."', which stayed as the player's: the paragraph did not open on "You ...", and "take" was the one word it shared with the typed "how much does it take off?". A bare line after sentences about the player alone (no he, she or they in them) is now read as the player's, and with typed quotes one shared word never makes a line theirs. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone said not to be there is not there, and a sentence with both sets of pronouns holds two people (8B run 135, T7). At the docks a hooded woman answered, and the prose said "She's not Elias [[A]], but she knows him well"; with the ops' TALK "A", the run-109 rule stood Elias at the docks, and the pronoun keeper, focused on Elias by that sentence, turned her next "she says, her voice low" into "he says, his voice". A code mentioned after not, like, than, of, about, knows (and the like) no longer shows the person here, and a sentence using he and she both neither sets the pronoun focus nor is mended. - `app/world.py`, `app/prose_state.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place named in passing joins the town when first walked to, and someone passed on the way stands in the town (8B run 134, T3-T4). The tallyman named the Northern Trade Docks at T3 and they were made as a place of their own; at T4 the player walked there from the tally house and they stayed outside Saltmarrow, so the walk read as a journey between places. A top-level place with no settlement size reached on foot for the first time now joins the town. And "You pass a water carrier named Tam Radley ... You reach the docks" stood him on the docks; someone the player passes on the way stands in the town. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Speech given to the player is found in a one-segment scene too (8B run 133, T2). "You turn to Percival, your voice firm. 'What do I need to do?'" put a line in the player's mouth that they never typed, and it stayed: the whole scene was one narration segment, and the rule that drops invented player lines reads the player's dialogue paragraph by paragraph. Each segment is now read as its paragraphs. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A replaced move leaves no place behind, and "prove yourself" is no task (8B run 132, T1-T2). MOVE "Talleyman's Office" was rightly replaced by the tally house the player asked for, but the venue rules had already made the office and left it empty on the map; when the prose never shows the model's destination, the player's place is now taken before any venue is made for it. And "You'll have to prove yourself." alone was filed as "Prove Yourself to the Guild" with an invented step; a bare demand to prove oneself sets no task. - `app/world.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A job with no task named is no job yet (8B run 131, T2). The clerk said only "the guild has jobs, and jobs pay debts ... Tell me what you have to offer", and the parser filed "Work Off Winter's Credit" (one step: "Ask for the job details") from "The Salvage Guild's Tally House" as giver, then "Salvage Guild Task" ("Complete the assigned task"). When every step of a proposed job only asks about the work or only says to do "the task", no task has been named and nothing is filed. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Coins lying in the scene move nothing (8B run 130, T2). While the tallyman only listed two jobs, GOLD 15 went through (+10) on "a desk cluttered with ledgers and loose coins, his sharp eyes narrowing as he takes in your words": a coin word and "takes" anywhere in one sentence counted as money changing hands. The verb now has to stand within a few words of the money, and taking in, taking note or taking a look is no take. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A start wished for is no start (8B run 129, T3). "Give me a job I can start today, and tell me what it takes off the ledger" took the job the tallyman offered in answer, as the player starting the work. A start inside the work asked for ("a job I can start", "work I could begin tomorrow?") no longer takes a job; "I start now" still does. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] An echo of the player's line goes, an answer that starts with its words stays (8B run 128, T3). After the engine said the typed lines, the draft's bare "The docks." (its echo of "The docks, then.") stood under them; a short unquoted fragment made only of a line's words now goes with the rest of the draft's retelling. And the rule that drops a piece of the player's line said again took "The docks are under the Reeve." as a near repeat of the three-word "The docks, then."; a near match only counts as a repeat at about the piece's own length. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sentence inside someone's quote is their speech (8B run 127, T2). The tallyman's "There's a job in the docks. You'll need to recover a shipment of salted fish that's been lost to the tide. The guild needs it by midday." lost its middle sentence: the pass that drops the narration acting for the player read it alone, saw no quote marks and a "You ...", and cut the job's own description. A sentence inside an open quotation is now left to its speaker. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The ops' name fits the sex the prose shows (8B run 126, T5). NPC_NEW "Elsabet Hollis" was given to "a figure watching from the edge of the dock ... The man's eyes narrow", storing a man under a woman's name. When the name pools give the name one sex and the prose shows the figure as the other (its noun, or the man / he / she right after it), a name of the shown sex is drawn for that person. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The engine's clock after a dash goes too (8B run 125, T3). "A distant bell rings, marking the hour—08:17." put the game clock in the prose; the clock repair took ", 08:00." and "at 08:30" but not a time after a dash, a colon, "of" or "is". Speech keeps its times. - `app/prose_state.py`
- [CLAUDE] One apostrophe for a job told to you (8B run 124, T2). The tallyman's "You'll work it off by hauling the last load of iron from the docks" was refused as the player's own words: the check that finds a "You ..." line inside someone's quote compared the prose's curly "You’ll" with the parser's straight "You'll" and found nothing. Both are read with one apostrophe now. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sign's possessive is no use of a name (8B run 123, T1). NPC_NEW NAME "Tallyman" ROLE "Salvage Ledger Clerk" went in as a person called Tallyman because the prose's only "Tallyman" was capitalised, on "a sign that reads Tallyman's Ledger". A word whose only uses are a possessive in a thing's name, or that is never used, and that is shaped like a compound job (-man, -keeper, -master, -wright, -smith ...) is a job; a given name like Wren in "Wren's Rest" stays a name. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sign passed is not the door gone in (8B run 122, T1). "You pass a few shuttered shops and a weathered sign that reads The Lame Wheel Smithy ... The salvage guild's tally house is just ahead ... You step inside" opened the smithy (its LOC_NEW, named like a shop) as the place walked into, and the tallyman's desk stood in a smithy. A new place the prose only passes is not the one the entry goes into. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The giver naming or setting the work is the offer (8B run 121, T2). The tallyman said "It's a salvage run. You'll be heading out to the docks and retrieving a shipment", but the parser quoted the player's question, so the telling was refused and the card stayed "Work Against My Debt" from the walk in. "It's a <run, haul, job ...>" and "You'll be <doing it>" now count as the giver's own offer sentence, which also clears a question quoted by a mark, so the open card takes the telling. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A giver asking what work the player wants has set none (8B run 120, T2). The tallyman said "You'll need to prove your worth. What kind of job are you looking for?" and the parser filed "Prove worth through work" as an offer. An offer line that asks the player which job, work or trade they want is no offer yet, unless it also names one ("there's a job on the docks"). - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Taking a job by a word of its title goes by stem (8B run 119, T3). "I will take the wreck" left "Salvage the Wrecked Ship" offered: the title word was "Wrecked" and only exact words matched. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A placeholder is no place, and a role cut before its object loses the dangling words (8B run 118, T3). The tallyman said the player would deal with Captain Veyra; NPC_NEW gave her LOC "Unknown", and a place called Unknown was made for her, with the role "captain of the". A placeholder place (unknown, none, elsewhere, nowhere ...) now files the person in the town the player is in, and a role ending on "of the", "at the" and the like loses those words. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's name is no one else's (8B run 117, T1). NPC_NEW NAME "Isk Tarrow" ROLE "salvage guild tallyman" for the scarred man who steps out of the tally house stored the tallyman under the player's own name. A new person named as the player (full name, or first name with no code of their own) is kept under their job and drawn a name of their own. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A size band is an amount, a grant naming her own thing is that thing, and a place passed is no move (8B run 116, T1). GRANT "tide-reckoner" QTY small DESC "mother's brass tide-reckoner" (for "the one your mother gave you before she vanished") put a second reckoner in the pack: a grant with only a size band passed as a metadata edit and skipped every gain check, and "gave you" read as a hand-over now. A band is now judged as the amount it is, and a grant whose description is the full name of something the player owns is that thing. And MOVE "Saltmarrow Stables" for "You pass the Saltmarrow Stables [[L2]]" on the way to the tally house stood the player in the stables; a place the prose only passes does not show the player there, so the walk ends where the player asked. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A job just taken is not two jobs (8B run 115, T3). In the turn the player took Oswin's salvage job, the parser also proposed "Report to Oswin Bellamy", and it was made and taken as a second job. A new job from a giver whose job is offered or taken and not yet begun is part of that job (unless the words make it more work); only an offered one's card takes the telling, and only the first telling in a turn rewrites it. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A line said is not also retold (8B run 114, T3). The draft told the player's lines in its own words ("You can dive. You square your shoulders and say you'll take the reef job."); the engine said the typed lines at the opening and left that telling under them, so the player said everything twice. When the missing lines are said at the opening, a "you" sentence that is one of them (I to you), or that reports speech with most of its words from the player's line, goes. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A chance to pay moves no coin (8B run 113, T2). While the tallyman only set the reed-cutting work, GOLD 15 went through (+12) because the narration's "each one a chance to pay down what you owe" counted as money changing hands. A purpose or a chance to pay (to pay down / off / back, a chance, way or need to pay) is now a promise like "will pay". - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Of several figures, the one inside is the ops' person (8B run 112, T1). NPC_NEW "Lars" ROLE "tallyman" for "push inside. Inside, ... A man in a soot-stained coat is at the counter", with a rope maker's man passed on the way in, was dropped (two unnamed men, so no one figure to give the name to) and the counter man was seeded with a drawn job, "Hereward, cooper", who then gave out the guild's work. When only one of the figures stands after the player goes in, that one is the ops' person. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A name only spoken of is not the figure, and a word left out of a place's name is that place (8B run 111, T4). The tallyman named Ellen Croft as the one to find; a hooded woman walked in and said "She's not here", and NPC_NEW "Ellen Croft" was stored as her, in a second tally house, "The Salvage Tally House". Saying a name (the player's "Ellen Croft.", the tallyman's "Ellen Croft," he repeats) no longer reads as calling that person, and a new name the scene only speaks of is not given to an unnamed figure unless the speaker gives it as their own ("I'm Ellen Croft"). A place name whose words all stand, in order, in exactly one known place's name is that place. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place is no thing to carry (8B run 110, T3). "I will take the docks", said of the dock-cleaning job, came with GRANT "docks" and "docks x1" went into the pack. Place nouns (docks, harbour, wharf, yard, market, street ...) now read like a job or a deal: not a thing unless the prose hands one over. A name split at its preposition is read by the word before it, so "a map of the docks" is still a map. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone known the turn talks with, shown here, is here (8B run 109, T5). On a turn that stays put, a known person the ops talk with whom the narration (not speech) shows by code is stood where the player is; named only in someone's words, they stay where they were. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone known, shown at a place the walk makes, comes too (8B run 108, T4). Walking to the new Eastern Dock, the prose had "Elias Vane [[B]] watches from a distance" while the save kept him at his desk in the tally house: the rule that stands someone known where the walk ends gave up when the place did not exist yet. People known and shown after the arrival are now held, like new ones, and stood at the arrival once the move is made. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A refused gain never cuts the player's own words, and a thing the player picks up is a thing (8B run 107, T3). "I pick up the contract and read it. A sentence that tells back the player's own line (their quoted words, or their act in "you") is never cut by that trim, and a contract or other paper the player's own line picks up stands like one handed over. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A service the player buys is no job for them (8B run 106, T7). Asking the boatman "What would you want for taking me out there and back?", answered "You're not just paying for the row, you're paying for the risk", filed "Row with Emmot Bexley" as a job Emmot offered the player. A new job is refused when the player's own line asks the other side's price (what would you want / charge / take for, what's your fee) or the offer says the player pays; asking what a job pays ("How much for the salvage run?") is still asking about work. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A person named who is not here is said to be elsewhere (8B run 105, T5). On the east dock the player said "Hugh sent me. - [CLAUDE] The job is no part of a name, and the giver's own offer sentence grounds the job (8B run 104, T1-T2). - [CLAUDE] A job told again takes the telling, and the quest parser gets only the player's line (8B run 103, T2-T3). - [CLAUDE] A short name of a known place is that place (8B run 102, T1). - [CLAUDE] A figure named after a dash is that person, and the other figures are more people (8B run 101, T4). - [CLAUDE] The tallyman stands where the walk ends, also in a place the turn makes (8B run 100, T1-T2). - [CLAUDE] Someone left behind is not the other side of a check (8B run 96, T4). - [CLAUDE] A figure inside a description is no one in the scene (8B run 93, T2). - [CLAUDE] A job told to you is an offer (8B run 89, T3). - [CLAUDE] The way there is no arrival (8B run 87, T1). - [CLAUDE] A name the prose only uses as a common noun is a job (8B run 85, T1). NPC_NEW NAME "Tallyman" ROLE "Salvage Guild Ledger Clerk" for "They are the tallyman" stored a person called Tallyman, and the prose then wrote "The Tallyman [[A]]". - [CLAUDE] The place stood in is no destination asked for (8B run 79, T5). - [CLAUDE] A job at the yard is a job, and a job-named person takes the sex shown (8B run 78, T1). - [CLAUDE] A place said is no place gone to (8B run 77, T6). - [CLAUDE] A name after "the <job>," names someone; it calls no one (8B run 77, T3). - [CLAUDE] The player's own question, and the giver telling it back, offer nothing (8B run 76, T2). - [CLAUDE] A figure in another trade is not the one sought (8B run 75, T1). - [CLAUDE] At the berth after the wreck (8B run 73, T6), three state faults. - [CLAUDE] A vague figure is the one the scene names later (8B run 72, T9). A vague shape (figure, shape, silhouette ...) followed anywhere later by the turn's one listed or introduced new person is that person. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The walk's end is a place it goes toward, not a thing it looks for (8B run 72, T5). "walk out along the flats toward the eastern shoals ... watching the water for the Black Maw's broken mast" took "Black Maw's" as where the walk ended (the model's own move happened to win). The named end of a walk along a route now comes only after toward/towards/to, and never as a possessive. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A promise is no pay and no haul (8B run 71, T4). Taking the beam-hauling job paid 30 gold and put "Iron beams" in the pack: the narrator's "for that, you'll be paid thirty gold" was read as coin received, and the spoken "I'll haul the beams off the east pier wreck" as taking them, with the prose silent on any beams arriving. A sentence that promises pay ("you'll be paid", "will pay") backs no coin, and when the prose shows nothing of an item only the player's narrated act, not their speech, can take it. A refused grant that names something the player already owns is still no gain to trim. Over 30 saved turns with model gold, only this one changes. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place passed is not the one entered (8B run 70, T1). "You pass Millicent's Bakery ... and continue toward the salvage guild's tally house" stood Isk in the bakery: the doorway rule matched any known venue named in the last of the prose. A name after "pass/past/beyond/beside/by" is left out of that match. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone talked about is not shown (8B run 69, T3). Brida's "He's not a man who likes delays", said of the foreman at the docks, gave the figure "a man"; NPC_NEW "Garrik" was bound to it and stood in the tally house. Figures a new person can be bound to come from the narration outside speech only. Over 266 saved turns with new people, one more binding goes (7B Saltmarrow, "Kendra" bound to "a girl named Mara" in a rumour). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player is "you" (8B run 68, T2). The prose leaked the packet's word: "pushed a stack of papers toward the player", "the player's patched tunic". Outside speech, "the player" becomes "you" and "the player's" becomes "your". Two of 769 saved narrations had it. - `app/prose_state.py`
- [CLAUDE] A drawn name fits the one shown (8B run 68, T1-T2). "A man in a soot-stained coat", the tallyman, was given the drawn name "Gunilda"; the next scene wrote "she" and the pronoun pin mixed him and her. A name drawn for a job or a figure now takes the sex the figure was shown with. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] "You accept the offer" is the player's yes to give (8B run 67, T1). The player had only asked what work there was; the prose went on "You accept the offer. Lowen Thatcher gives you a map and a set of instructions." Accepting or taking the offer, job, deal, terms or contract is now a decision made for the player, so the scene ends on the tallyman's offer and the hand-over after it goes; when the player's own line said yes, the prose may tell it. Over the saved drafts, one more sentence reads as such ("as you accept the contract", 7B run 29). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] An offer is spoken whichever way its apostrophes lean (8B run 66, T1). The tallyman's "I've got a job for you ... You'll get a fair share of the salvage" was refused as offered by no one: the parser's quote had straight apostrophes and the prose curly ones, so it was not found inside his line. The spoken-offer check reads both the same. Over 184 saved turns with proposed jobs, 5 more stand once no giver is resolved. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] "You agree to take the job" is the player's to say (8B run 66, T2). The scene closed on a yes the player never gave; the decision rule knew "agree" only after "then/and". "You agree to/that ..." now counts like "you decide to". - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A refusal is no offer (8B run 65, T1). Gregory's "You're not the first to come asking for work, but I don't take on just anyone ... I don't deal in charity" became the job "Settle Debt with Gregory Cropper": the accept words read "deal" in "don't deal in charity" as the player's "Deal.", and "asking for work" and "take on" read as job words. "Deal" agrees only standing alone, and work others ask for, or said not to be given ("don't take on", "won't hire"), is taken out before a line is read for job words. Over 182 saved turns with proposed jobs, none changes. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A room of the building one stands in is a place to go (8B run 64, T4). "I go down to the lower vault to search the old records" inside the tally house, with the prose going down the stairwell into it, left the player upstairs: no rule could resolve a room. A room the line goes to (vault, cellar, basement, back room, storeroom, loft, attic, archives, strongroom, counting room, kitchen), named by the prose and not stopped there, is now found or made as the building's own child (noted as `room_requested`); from the open town no room is made. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A step with no result is talk, and a job that pays moves no coin (8B run 63, T6). Asking Tamsin about the job paid XP 7 and gold 8: the XP stood on a proposed step done ("I owe the guild a winter's credit") that the quest pass then refused, since the reward gate runs first, and the gold on "the mention of a job that pays off debts". A proposed step counts for the model's XP only when its quote shows a result, and a job told by what it pays ("a job that pays ...") backs no coin. Over 29 saved turns with model gold, only this one changes. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A shorter saying of a typed line is that line, in its place (8B run 63, T3). The draft gave the player '"Whatever pays most," you said ... "Name it."' for the typed '"Salvage. Out on the flats, whatever pays most against the ledger." ... "Name it and I'll go."'; it was kept as the player's words and the typed lines were then said again at the start. A line tagged as the player's whose words all come from one missing typed line now becomes that line where it stands, and two halves of one typed line around its tag are said as the one line. A sign's '"Harbour Office."' is no line of theirs. Over 348 saved drafts with typed lines, 4 change, each to say the player's line once. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Taking a job earns no XP either (8B run 62, T2). The model's XP 12 for asking what work there was stood on a "quest change": the parser had proposed an accept of the opening's job, which the engine then refused because the player had not taken it. Only a step done or a job finished counts as progress for the model's XP; an accept, like an offer (run 33), is talk. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A clause with its own subject is not the player going (8B run 61, T1). "You reach the edge of the town, where the buildings give way to the ruins of the old harbour" made a place "Ruins" and moved Isk into it, as the run 43 rule read the "You" at the start of the sentence. A where/which/whose/when clause after a comma starts its own clause. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A code that names nothing goes (8B run 61, T1). "the guild's tallyman [[TALLYMAN]]" showed on the page; older runs had invented codes such as [[N1]], [[H1]] and [[W1]] beside names. Bracket codes outside the engine's own shapes (one to three capitals, or L/I/E/Q and a number) come out of the prose, and a name written in code brackets ("[[Bertram Yates]]") is left as the name. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Choosing the first is the player's (8B run 60, T2). The closing "You choose the first, the one that offers the most chance to prove your worth." picked a job for the player from a list of three; the decision rule read only "choose to/that ...". Choosing, selecting or settling on one of the options laid out ("the first", "one", "this") now counts, and picking one too, though never "pick it up". - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone shown after the arrival goes with the walk, op or no op (8B run 60, T1). "Inside, Milo [[A]] is hunched over a ledger" kept the tallyman in town once his only op, a TALK, was cut with the scene's tail, and the next scene sat a new clerk at his desk. People at the place left whom the narration shows, by code or name and outside speech, after the destination's name are moved there (noted with `via: shown_after_arrival`). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A quote written inside the reward is the job's (8B run 59, T3). The parser closed the reward late, so "offer_line" and "evidence" fell inside it, and "Gut the Drowned Mariner", picked from the tallyman's list and named by him, was refused with no quote at all. Evidence, offer line, difficulty, timer and giver found inside the reward are lifted back to the job before it is judged. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] The face met on an errand is no find (8B run 58, T1). Walking to the tally house kept the model's XP 13, now on "new person": the tallyman one came to see. A new person met inside a building in town is no discovery, like the building itself (run 42); someone met out on the flats, or found while exploring, still counts. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A merchant ship is no merchant (8B run 58, T3). "A wreck of a merchant ship" gave the figure "a merchant", and NPC_NEW "Gervase Grisham", whom the prose never shows, was bound to it and stored. A figure noun followed by the head it modifies (ship, house, post, road, stall, guild, goods ...) is no person. Over 732 saved narrations, only this one changes. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Lifting it out takes it (8B run 57, T7). "I reach into the hollow and lift out whatever is glinting" with "You lift the glinting object from the hollow" refused the ring, as neither the take words nor the take-anything words knew "lift out". Lifting or fishing a thing out, and pulling it out of or free from somewhere (not oneself), ask to take it; lift, fish, pull or draw out "whatever" takes whatever it is. - `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] No markdown in a job's name (8B run 57, T4). The job "Salvage the *Iron Widow*" kept the asterisks the prose's leak repair had already taken out of the scene. Emphasis marks come off a new job's title, summary, description and steps. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] The job's own verb takes it (8B run 56, T3). "I'll clear the Stormbreaker." accepted nothing: the offer was "Clear the Wreckage", the wreck "from the Stormbreaker", and the run 27 rule only read take/do/handle/run/work. "I'll <the job's lead verb> <a word of its title or description>" now takes it, both in the engine's typed accept and in the parser's check. - `app/local_intel.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] "And you do" is the act asked (8B run 55, T5). "He gestures for you to follow him, and you do" had the player follow, as "do" stood for an act the clause test could not read. A pro-verb (do, did, comply, obey) after "for/asks/tells/beckons you to <act>" is read as that act. It is still kept when the line's own walk covers it; the run 55 case, where the line had walked up to the man, stays open and is logged. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The same named job is the job taken (8B run 55, T6). The man the active "Work for the Iron Tide" sent the player to gave directions, and they became a second job, "Investigate the Iron Tide's Raids". A new offer whose title names the same proper name as an active job (word pairs such as "iron tide") is that job; an employer's name (guild, house, company, office, council, watch, crew, reeve, harbour, town) is left out, as one employer gives many jobs. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] The end of a crate is no thing (8B run 54, T5). "I take the end of the nearest crate with the laborers" put "end x1" in the pack. A part of something held (end, corner, top, bottom, front, back, middle, the weight, the strain) is no taken thing, beside the rim, edge and side already listed. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The last of the hold is done (8B run 54, T6). The dock job's finish, "That was the last of the hold. You're clear to go.", was held back as travel ("go") and then as not about the job. Leave to go (clear, free, good to go; you can go) is no going, and "was the last of / the last one" is a result. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A new place walked to from inside a town is part of that town (8B run 54, T4-T7). "Berth Three" came from the foreman's LOC with no parent, so walking back to the tally house was a journey between places and ended outside it, in the street. A place made this turn and reached on foot from a town (not sailed, ridden or journeyed to) is made the town's child (noted as `new_place_in_town`). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone talked to by code alone goes where the walk ends (8B run 53, T1). The opening's tallyman Elsabet [[A]] stood "behind the counter" of the tally house, but the turn's only op for her was TALK "A", a code-only entry that the store only looks up, so she stayed in town and the next scene sat a new captain at her desk. A person reached by code alone, shown by code or name at the walk's end and still at the place left, is moved there (noted as `placed_at_arrival`); their role is untouched. This is likely behind the repeated "new face at the desk" drift of runs 40-51. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A repeat is theirs, and a quoted word is no half of a line (8B run 52, T3). '"The rusted tools," he repeats' was cut as the player's words in the tallyman's mouth, and the split-line join took 'pause at the word "tools."' as the first half, so everything from that word to the echo went and the scene read 'pause at the word "They lie in the old dockyard'. A line tagged as repeated or echoed is the other person's, and two quotations are one split line only with a speech tag between them. Over 326 saved drafts with typed lines, only this one changes. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A giver shown by their job is in the scene (8B run 52, T3). The prose called Ralph only "the tallyman", so his job was filed as offered by no one and then refused as no offer. "The <their job>" in the prose, with no one else listed in that job, puts the giver in the scene. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A spoken line shows nothing arriving, tagged or not (8B run 51, T3-T4). The refused "Runner's Task" trim cut the player's own typed "Whose debt do I collect first, and where do I find them?", and the refused "Debt note" trim cut the fisherman's '"You're here to collect the debt," he says'. A sentence is read as the refused thing arriving only when its narration, outside the quotation, names the thing (any of its words) or says it/them; a hand-over the narration tells still goes. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The way is not the end (8B run 51, T4). "head out along the shore path toward the Hollow Reach" made "The Shore" and stood Isk on it. A place gone along or across, with a named place the walk heads toward after it, is the route: the walk ends at that named place, made as part of the town when the prose shows it. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Another spelling is no new place (8B run 50, T4). LOC_NEW "The Salvage Tally House" while standing in "The Salvage Guild's Tally House" minted a second tally house; the run 37 rule that reads a shorter spelling as the same place covered MOVE only. LOC_NEW now resolves against every known place the same way and is noted (`other_spelling_of_known_place`) instead of minted. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The voicing is one line, even after a narrator beat (8B run 49, T1). For "ask the tallyman what work he'll take", the prose gave Isk three lines, ending with the decision "I'll do it." Two shapes let them through: a paragraph that opened on a narrator sentence before "You step closer ..." did not read as the player's, and a first line built from the typed words did not count as the voicing. A narrator sentence with no one else in it may now lead the player's paragraph (someone acting, as in "and the captain inspects it", still makes the line theirs), and any line kept on a voiced request is the voicing. Over 633 saved drafts, 8 change, each an invented player line ("That sounds reasonable," you say; "Two gold coins each? That sounds fair."). - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Leaving where the walk went is a new move (8B run 49, T1). The line walked to the tally house; "You leave the tally house" walked Isk out again, excused by the run 5 rule that leaving names where the player was. When the place left is the line's own destination, the leaving is the prose's. Over 633 saved drafts this removes 5 such exits (the Thirsty Bell, the salvage yard, the office). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A name told is a person (8B run 48, T1). "His name is Reinald Strand, a sedan bearer by trade" stored no one: the introduction patterns wanted the job followed by with/who/in/from/of, and none read a name told as "his/her/their name is". Both are read now, so the tallyman the prose named is in the save (without him, the next scene sat the woodseller at his ledger). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] One line voices the request (8B run 48, T1). For "ask the tallyman what work he'll take", every invented line on the player's subject stayed, among them "I can work ... I'll do whatever it takes to pay off my debt". The voicing allowance keeps one line, as it already did for an asked question. Over 629 saved drafts, only this one changes. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Asking where a thing is does not take it (8B run 47, T3). "I'll take it" (the job) and "Where do the guild's carts wait?" let GRANT "red stripe cart" put two carts in the pack while the carts stayed at the wharf. The player's line names an item only outside a question, or in a question that itself asks to take it; and a spoken "I'll take it" agrees to an offer, so the take-anything reading looks only at the line outside speech. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A named person keeps their own pronoun (8B run 46, T2). After "You step into the Tally House" was cut as the player's act, the stranded-pronoun repair turned "Kael, the tallyman, looks up from his ledger" into "from Tally House's ledger". A pronoun with someone named before it in its own sentence is theirs, and "the <Name>" in the cut sentence is a place, not a person. Over 621 saved drafts, 5 change, each undoing a wrong swap ("wiping Pike Tavern's hands", "as Lettice Bellamy sorts" for Hargrave). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Speech shows nothing arriving (8B run 46, T2). The refused "salvaged goods" trim cut "Either way, it'll take time."" from the tallyman's list of jobs ("it'll take" read as taking it) and left his quotation open. A sentence inside someone's quotation is no longer read as the refused thing reaching the player. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A question asked pays nothing (8B run 45, T2). "You ask what kind of work pays against the ledger" backed GOLD 50 (+37) on a job offer where no coin moved, as the gate read "pays" as money moving. A clause that asks or wonders (what, how, whether, about ...) is set aside before the money words are read. Over 28 saved turns with model gold, only this one changes. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The job's figure is the one already here (8B run 45, T2). "The tallyman, a man with a sharp eye" left "a man" free, and NPC_NEW "Ralf Wadley" (rat catcher), whom the prose never shows, was bound to it; the new person then kept the model's XP 15. A figure in apposition to the job of someone at this place is that person. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A line no one says is not logged (8B run 44, T0-T1). The opening's TALK "You're late, Flat-runner." had no speech in its prose, but it was logged as the watcher's; the next scene was shown it as already said and gave it, word for word, to the tallyman. A spoken sentence on a scene whose prose has no quotation at all, and whose words the prose does not carry, is now skipped and noted (`talk_line_not_in_prose`); a topic the scene shows is kept. Over 356 saved TALK lines this skips 38, nearly all openings' unsaid greetings ("You're late, Flat-runner" alone in four runs). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A job from a mark whose giver is elsewhere needs an offer (8B run 44, T1). QUEST "Salvage Contract" GIVER "A" pointed at the watcher left in town, and was kept on "You're late, Flat-runner". When a mark's giver is not in the scene, no one else speaks an offer and the words offer nothing, the mark is refused; a mark with no giver named is still trusted. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Only the player's going makes a place (8B run 43, T1). "the sound of waves lapping against the wharf echoes through the hollow alleys" minted a place "Hollow" and moved Isk into it, as the last-resort reader took any "through/into/toward the <place word>". The clause before the preposition has to say "you" now, or open on a participle with "you" after it ("Stepping out onto the driftwood path, you follow ..."). Over 664 saved narrations, 51 readings change, all of them someone or something else moving (a miller heading to the mill, figures through the square, a path toward a ruin, a ship to the Outer Isles). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The Tallyman is a job with any role (8B run 43, T1). NPC_NEW NAME "Tallyman" ROLE "Salvage Tally" slipped past the run 34 rule, which needed the name to be a word of the role; a role word with a job ending (-man, -er, -master, -keeper) counts too. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] An errand in town is no discovery (8B run 42, T1). Walking to the guild's tally house to ask for work kept the model's XP "small" (+6) as a new place, because the line named it. A building inside a settlement (a venue kind, or a building word such as house, hall, guild or shop) walked to by name is now an errand; exploring still counts, and so does a named place that is no building. The same check read `visit_count` from a match row holding only id and name, so every walk back to a named place counted as a first visit; it reads the full row now. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The role names the figure (8B run 41, T1). NPC_NEW "Tobin Yates" ROLE "tallyman" came with "Inside, the tallyman sits behind a desk", but "tallyman" is not one of the figure nouns, so he was dropped and the tally house had no one in it. A new person the prose does not name is now bound to "a/the <their role>" in the narration, unless someone here already holds that job. His appearance read "tall" out of "tallyman"; appearance words now match whole words only. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's name for the building holds three words (8B run 41, T1). "to the salvage guild's tally house" opened "Saltmarrow Salvage", because the asked-building phrase took at most two words before the head; it takes three now, none of them a function word ("the edge of the house" names no building). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone already met, shown where the walk ends, is there (8B run 40, T1-T2). The tallyman from the opening sat "behind a desk" in the tally house, but his op still said LOC L1, so he stayed in the town the player had left; the next turn was told he "is not at this location" and the prose emptied his office. The arrival rule (walk, then talk) now covers a known person too when the narration names them outside speech. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A relative clause has no verb for the tail (8B run 40, T1). The act-drop cut "You know what you're here for" and kept "The promise you made to the tides." as a sentence: the clause test read "tides" as a verb. A guessed -s/-ed verb now has to come before a "you/we/I" relative clause, while a plain "is/was/has" still counts after it ("The salvage you need to recover is deep" stays). Over 606 saved drafts this changes only two such fragments. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The prose names the new person (8B run 39, T2). The draft wrote NPC_NEW "Isolde Frome" for the tallyman while the prose said "His name was Odo Kettle", so the save held a name the story never used. When one new person goes unshown and the narration tells exactly one name nobody holds yet ("his/her/their name is/was ..."), that person takes the prose's name (noted as `name_from_prose`). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The beat goes with the cut line (8B run 39, T2). 'You leaned forward. "What kind of job?"' lost its invented line and left "You leaned forward." standing alone as a paragraph. A short beat of the player's own gesture that only led into a cut line goes with it (noted as `lead_in_beat_dropped`); a beat where someone else acts or something changes hands stays. Over 604 saved drafts this removes 47 such stranded beats and nothing else. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Someone met where the walk goes stays there (8B run 38, T5; mine, from run 12). The figure on "The Northern Coast Road" came with that LOC, the very place the walk was going by name; my run 12 rule saw an unknown place and put him back in the tally house the player had just left. A LOC that is the walk's own destination stands, and an unknown one goes to that destination rather than the place left behind. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A shared phrase in a mostly new line is no repeat of an earlier scene (8B run 38, T2). Gunilda's offer "... The guild's got a contract for a shipwreck off the northern coast, something with a cargo of rare goods ..." shared "a cargo of rare goods" with the tallyman's line the turn before and was cut as said before, so the job "Northern Coast Shipwreck" was filed from a draft the shipped prose no longer showed. Against an earlier scene, a line sharing a run is a repeat only when at least half its content words were said before; within one turn the stricter rule stays (a remark re-answered three ways still goes). Replay: 4 saved lines that shared a phrase but said something new are kept now. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A placeholder is no place (8B run 38, T1). The draft's "Tide reckoner's Last Known Location" was stored as a place on the map. A name ending in "location" or "whereabouts", or "last known / unknown / next / current place", is no toponym now; "The Location Office" still is. Saved place names: 1 of 115 newly refused, this one. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The shells handed over are all of them (8B run 37, T6). "You hand over the shells" took 1 of 2: the draft sized its TAKE "small" and the roll gave one. When the prose (outside speech) hands over, gives, sells, sets down or empties "the <plural>" of a stack the player holds, the loss is the whole stack (`whole_stack_handed`); "one of the shells" still rolls. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The walk ends at the last place the line names (8B run 37, T6). "I climb the seawall steps and carry the shells back to the tally house" saved the player at the seawall while the prose walked into the tally house: the known-place match took the longest name the line held, and "the tally house" was not the stored full name. A known place now also matches by its last two words when no other place shares them, a full-name match is never undercut by a partial one inside it, and of several places the latest in the line wins. Replay over saved traces with each turn's own places: 3 change (this one, and two 7B walks to the one salvage yard of the time that now resolve directly). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The water creeping is no stealth, and stairs are walked (8B run 37, T6). "With the water starting to creep back, I climb the seawall steps ..." rolled stealth (a critical failure) on the water's "creep", and then athletics on "climb ... steps". Creeping counts as the player's ("I creep", "creep along / toward / past ..."), and climbing steps or stairs rolls nothing. Replay: 2 of 643 saved lines change, this one and a 7B "I climb up the stair to the bell-chamber". - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A shorter spelling of here is here (8B run 37, T2). MOVE "The Salvage Tally House" while the player stood in "The Salvage Guild's Tally House" wrote a dropped-MOVE note, and on the legacy path could have minted a second tally house. Two names are one place when the shorter one's words (two or more) are all in the longer one and the head word matches (the Drowned District Chapel is not the district); such a MOVE is cleared. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Leaving the place leaves the talk partner (8B run 36, T5). "I leave the tally house and head down through town to the bay" rolled its (bogus) check against the tallyman still at his desk: the talk fallback took the last partner. A line that leaves, walks out of or steps out of a place no longer rolls against the partner it walks away from, unless it names them. Replay over saved traces: 1 opponent changes (7B run 5, T29, the player walking off from Bryony). - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] Where a ship lies is no lie (8B run 36, T5). "... until I can see where the Stormcaller lies" rolled deception (failed, 7 XP kept on the roll): the trigger read any "lie" or "lies". Deception needs the player's own lie now ("I lie", "a lie", "tell a lie", "lie / lying to, about, that"), and bluffing still counts.
