The world's half after ", and" now stays only when it has its own subject and verb; otherwise the sentence goes whole. Replay over 49 saved 8B drafts: 4 changed, each a verbless remnant ("The knowledge that ...", "The way the two of them watch you"). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Money promised or wanted is not money moved (8B run 10, T3). Osbert offered a salvage job and the draft sent GOLD 50, rolled to +29; the bare-gain check let it through because the prose said "the guild will pay handsomely" and "your hands itch for a blade, or for a coin". A gain the prose names no sum for now needs a sentence (outside speech) where money changes hands (pays, sells, hands over, tosses, pockets ...) and that is no promise (will / would / could pay, if you bring it ...). Replay: 15 saved non-quest gains, 1 dropped (this one); "Petronel hands you the pouch" still pays. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Said once is said (8B run 10, T3). The prose gave the player's typed line in full, then had the player say a reworded copy ("Then tell me what it is, and what it pays," you say). A later player line made only of words the player already said this turn now goes. Replay over 508 saved turns against the committed code: 2 new drops, this one and a 7B "You decide to try one last time, 'Hargrave, are you here?'". - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A MOVE to where the player stands is no trip (8B run 10, T0). The opening sent MOVE "Saltmarrow" with the player already in Saltmarrow, and the journal said "MOVE to Saltmarrow dropped: the player did not ask to go there". It is now no move and no note. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The place is what stands there, not the town it stands in (8B run 9, T0). The opening came back with MOVE "The Boar at Saltmarrow", an inn the prose never mentions, and the save opened inside it while the prose stood the player at the edge of the flats. The head word of "The Boar at Saltmarrow" was "saltmarrow", so prose naming the town counted as showing the move. A place name's words now stop at "at / in / by / on / near / beside / outside"; saved names of that shape: 5, all "The <thing> at <town>". - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The verifier's naming rule is for other people (8B run 9, T0). It failed the opening because the prose never named the player and repeated that issue until the reply ran out (47 s). The rule now says the player is "you" and needs no name; 1 of 450 saved verifier replies raised it. - `app/prompts.py`
- [CLAUDE] A partial verifier patch keeps what the prose shows (8B run 8, T2). The draft GRANTed the "broken wooden beam" the prose has the player pull from the mud; the verifier patched `inventory_changes` with the tide-reckoner alone, and since a patch replaced the whole list the beam was gone with no note. A patched list now keeps each draft entry it leaves out when the prose names it (noted as `patch_kept_prose_backed_entry`); an entry the prose never names stays dropped, and an empty list still clears the key. Replay: 7 saved list patches, 1 left out a prose-backed entry (this one). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A reason is not the act (8B run 8, T1). "keeping an eye out for anything half-buried in the mud that might fetch a price" rolled appraise on the word "price". What a thing might be good for (a "that/which might, could, would ..." clause) is read out of the line before the skill table, the way watched clauses are; replay: 1 saved line has such a clause, this one. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] No one to offend, no blunder (8B run 8, T1). The same failed appraise, alone on the mud, wrote "Botched Appraise: you misjudge the moment and give offense". A social miss with no one on the other side stays a bad miss; the blunder line is kept for a check against someone. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] No pursuit of a way (8B run 7, T1-T2). The scene thread read "I follow the narrow path" as going after "the narrow path", shipped that to the next turn as the thing being followed, and T2 moved the player onto a minted place "Saltmarrow's narrow path" while the prose had them on a roof. The way check now sits in `pursuit_in`, which both the thread and the follow guard use; replay: 5 of 551 saved lines, all paths, ridges or footprints. - `app/scene_thread.py`
- [CLAUDE] A path is walked, not a person followed (8B run 7, T1). "I follow the narrow path down into the drowned district" was read as following someone, "the narrow path", and the turn logged "Followed the narrow path with no place named". A follow target that is a way (path, road, track, shore, ridges, footprints ...) is no person; replay: 4 such lines in all saved runs, all ways. - `app/scene_thread.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sum posted or offered is not coin received, and a dash aside is no missing name (8B run 6, T2). The board note "Lower District, 20 coins for a flat-runner's work" paid GOLD 20 at once (logged as paid ahead), because the sum was in the prose; a gain where the prose only names what work pays or offers, with no coin handed over or received, is now dropped (replay: that one in 14 non-quest gains). "worth a coin—are there" became "worth a coin— Salvage Guild Hall [[L2]] are there": the name-hole filler took a dash inside a sentence for a sentence that lost its subject; a dash now opens a hole only at the start of a sentence or line. - `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A check is not against someone the line leaves behind or asks past (8B run 5, T5). "I leave Brida at the gate and walk to the salvage guild hall ... and ask the clerk about the boats" rolled persuasion vs Brida, the last one spoken to. A name the line leaves ("leave", "left", "away from") is turned away from, and a line that asks someone by their job ("ask the clerk") drops a stale partner whose job is different. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A venue the world's setup names is plausible whatever the settlement's size (8B run 5, T5). The Saltmarrow setup names "the salvage guild" as a faction, and "Salvage Guild Hall was not found: a village has no guild hall" left Isk outside while the prose had her at the guild clerk's desk. A kind of venue whose name or kind word appears in the playthrough's own setup text now passes the size rule; kinds the setup does not name (an apothecary, a jeweller, a temple in this village) are still refused. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A routine social roll earns no XP (8B run 5, T4). "A name, Brida, or I ask at the guild" rolled routine persuasion, and that roll counted as an outcome, so the model's +16 XP stood on a turn where nothing was learned or done. A routine check in a social skill no longer counts toward the outcomes XP needs; other checks, finds, quests, fights and new places still do. (Whether plain talk rolls at all is #88, Stell's call.) - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A piece of the player's line said again on its own goes (8B run 5, T1). '"Morning," you say, your voice carrying the quiet dread of the frontier. "Morning. You look like you are waiting for somebody ..." you ask.' had the player greet twice. A quoted line of the player's that is only the first sentence of a typed line, with its tag, is dropped when the whole line is also in the scene (replay: 3 in all saved turns, each a leftover of a restored line). - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Jobs are judged against the text the player reads (8B run 4, T6). The narrator wrote QUEST "Clear the Rat Infestation in the East" for a Nesta Grisham and a step that the final prose no longer has (her paragraph was cut; the shipped scene is about quarry work); the job check ran on a narration copy taken before the cleanup and offered it. The quest step now gets the turn's current narration, so the job's quote has to be in what the player reads. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A scene that stops mid-sentence is cut back to its last finished sentence (8B run 4, T5). The reply ran out of tokens and the turn ended "... You're not here for the bones, are you? He". A text ending with no closing mark is trimmed to its last finished sentence when enough is left (replay: that one turn in all saved runs). - `app/prose_state.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A reported question must be one the player asked (8B run 4, T4). After only "Where is the guild digging?" the prose went on "You ask if it's dangerous, and she shrugs": reported speech was allowed whenever the player spoke at all. Reported speech ("you ask if/whether/about ...") now has to share at least two content words with the player's own line, quoted or not ("Then I ask her for a coil of rope" still covers "You ask about the rope"). Replay: that one sentence dropped in all saved turns. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A retold line and a planned next step are the player's, not the narrator's (8B run 4, T3). After '"I am here for salvage, same as every dry tide," you say' the prose went on "You're here for salvage, just like every other dry tide, but the guild's hauls ... have piqued your interest": an unquoted "you" sentence carrying most of a typed line the prose already has the player say is now dropped as an echo (replay: that one new in all saved turns). The narrator planning for the player ("you'll need to tread carefully", "you have to focus on the immediate task at hand", "you might be able to get ahead of the game") is read by the thought check as a decision made for them. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's item codes stay on the player's things, and a thought as the subject is inner life (8B run 4, T0). "She wears a patched tunic [[I2]], the same as yours" linked her tunic to the player's: an item code is now left off a thing another person owns, wears, holds or sells ("his", "Avelina's", "she wears a ..."), while "You pick up a rusty key [[I5]]" keeps it. "The thought of leaving this place feels like leaving a part of yourself behind" passed the inner-life check because its subject was "the thought": a thought, idea, memory or prospect about "you" now counts. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The player's own debt is not work offered (8B run 2, T1). "Funny how the debt works ... And if you don't pay your share, they don't let you walk away" made a job "Collect the Debt": the giver check took any job word in the giver's lines, and "pay" is one. Words about the player paying or owing ("you pay", "pay your share", "your debt") no longer count as an offer; a giver who names work and its pay still does. Replay over all 84 saved job proposals: that one verdict changed. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] A new person must be named by the narration, not only in someone's line (8B run 2, T6). "The guild, and the Harbour Reeve. They've been looking for you" made the Reeve a person in the scene; and a part of a name matched any word ("Harbour" in "harbour town"). A new person now counts as shown when the narration names them, a capitalised part of the name appears outside speech, or someone in the scene speaks to them ("Not yet, Hargrave"). Replay over 137 NPC_NEW ops: 6 now dropped (the Reeve, Lettice only asked after, and four junk names such as "A" and "I"). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A gain the prose says nothing of needs the player's line to ask for that item (8B run 3, T8). "I pocket the key and step through the doorway" let GRANT "Rusted lock" into the pack for a lock the prose only had her fingers brush: the take in the line counted for any item. When the prose says nothing of an item, the player's own line has to name it or ask for whatever turns up ("anything worth hauling back"); replay over the saved gains: the lock, plus three grants already known to be wrong (lost anchor, "Five gold coins" as an item, "deck work"). The same turn: "your lantern's glow" with no lantern now goes like the unowned-weapon clause (carried tools and lights; one light by any name), and "you feel a strange pull" is read as a feeling with words before it. - `app/world.py`, `app/prose_state.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] TAKE means losing an item, and says so (8B run 3, T7). The 8B read the op word as English and wrote TAKE "fragments of lock mechanism" for what Isk pried free; a loss of something the player does not own was a silent no-op, so the fragments vanished. The op line now says what TAKE is (an item the player loses, uses up or hands over; what they get is GRANT), and a TAKE of an item the player does not own is read as a gain claim that the grant checks decide like any GRANT. Coin is excluded: TAKE "Five gold coins" is the player paying. - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Curly apostrophes read as straight ones in the player-act checks (8B run 3, T3). The 8B writes ’, so "You don’t ask why he’s carrying it; you just keep moving" passed every "don'?t" pattern; the clause is now read with straight apostrophes (replay: that one sentence in all saved turns). - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Any going counts for the trip guard, and the travel Note needs a place (8B run 3, T1). "I go down to the workers hauling crates": the prose invented Isk's "Just a flat-runner, looking for work" and the dockworker's answer to it; the speech pass dropped her line and kept the answer, because the scene cut's trip guard did not read "You move down the dockside path" as the going. The guard now takes any motion verb, not a short list. The "no resolvable destination" Note goes into the history only when the line names a place; a walk to people does not. - `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Class fixes from the first 8B run (Qwen3 8B, run 2, T1-T10). Inner life the player never gave: the existing thought check missed contracted clauses ("You've", "you're"), lead-ins ("But today, you ..."), feelings framed as a weight or a pull ("You could feel the weight of his words"), states of mind ("you're not ready to give in", "you're not here to beg") and "A part of you knows"; it now reads them, while touch ("the weight of the coins in your palm"), the body the engine keeps ("the weight of your injuries"), being watched and world news ("You've heard that the captain ...") stay (replay: 35 newly dropped in 480 saved turns, read). A restored player line takes "ask" only when it asks something, not from the question after it ("I owe the guild, not you or Colm," you ask, T4-T10). A new person whose name is a known full name with one word a letter off ("Harbour Reevec" beside "Harbour Reeve", T7), or a one-word name of six letters or more, is that person; "Mara" and "Maria" stay two (replay: that one pair in all saved worlds). - `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The automatic context window leaves room for the rest of the desktop (Qwen3 8B, 2026-10-14). The window was sized to fit the free GPU memory at load with about 0.6 GB to spare (22,528 tokens; the 8B's cache is 147 KB a token, 2.6x the 7B's). Browsers, Discord and a screen recorder took the rest, the cache spilled into shared memory, and the opening took 891 s; capped at 16,384 it took 33 s. The budget now keeps 1.5 GB of the card free: the 8B opens at 18,432 on this 12 GB card, the 7B keeps 24,576. - `app/model_limits.py`

- [CLAUDE] A repeated line is no place to end a scene, and a short line said again word for word is a repeat (7B run 10, T1-T3). The scene cut ended on the opening's "Depends, flat-runner. What have you got to offer?", leaving no reply; Jehane's "Very well, then. What do you have to offer?" came back word for word because it had too few content words to count. The cut steps over lines from earlier scenes, whole lines of five or more words count as repeats, and the draft is handed player_asks, the questions to answer plainly. - `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`

An agreeing reply that asks back is not agreement. - `app/quest_parser.py`

Inside, the air is hot", and "walk down to the edge of the mudflats" all left the player in the square. - [CLAUDE] Every turn now records what the engine did, not only what the model said. The turn's trace file gets an `engine` block: every write to the save with the code that made it, every exception the engine caught and hid (the code has about 600 `except Exception: pass`; they are listed by where they were raised and caught, routine number-parsing fallbacks last), refused items, voided spends and gold decisions, the apply-side reports (movement, quests, gear, acquisitions) that never reached the trace before, and a before/after delta of the player, pack, place, clock and new rows with the writes behind each change. Bugs like 7B run 5's +50 gold on a walk can be traced to the line. - `app/turn_ledger.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Time is measured for estimates. `timings.jsonl` beside the traces gets a line per turn (seconds, split into model, GPU wait and engine, the number of model calls, and the gap since the previous turn ended) and one for the setup (start, setting templates, post-start passes, opening); `requests.jsonl` gets every state-changing API call, setup page included, so setup-to-first-turn can be read off one timeline. Neither is pruned. `tools/timing_report.py <trace dir>` summarises a game, and agent-played runs copy each turn's delta and notes into `turns/NNN.json`. - `app/turn_ledger.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/main.py`, `tools/timing_report.py`, `tools/api_bridge_playtest.py`
- [CLAUDE] A test run can be watched live. `tools/api_bridge_playtest.py --viewer-port N` serves the game page from the run's own save, read-only (`MORKYN_SPECTATE=1`: every POST to /api is refused), and the page opens straight into the game and repaints within about three seconds of each new turn (`/api/live-tick`). A person whose job changes gets their workplace claim moved, and someone met inside a shop of their trade works there. - `app/town_grid.py`, `app/town_moves.py`
- [CLAUDE] A loss has to be shown (7B Saltmarrow run, T7): the GM wrote TAKE for the player's boots, tunic, breeches and mother's tide-reckoner on a walk onto the flats, and the pack emptied, because removals were always allowed. A removal now needs the item and a loss (given, dropped, sold, eaten, lost, broken, taken...) in one sentence of the prose or the player's line; anything else is refused and journalled. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Setting out is not finishing (7B Saltmarrow run, T7): 'you head down the slipway onto the mudflats to look for the lost cargo' completed and paid the cargo job while the search roll failed. A step done or a completion whose evidence only sets out, looks for or tries, with no result, is refused. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Asking what a job pays is not appraising (7B Saltmarrow run, T6, a regression of #102): 'what's it worth when I bring it in?' rolled a failed appraise and a speech blunder. A worth question in job or pay words rolls nothing. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] A name written as a link is a name (7B Saltmarrow run, T0): '[[Bertram Yates]]' shipped as markup and the person was seeded under another name, and 'a [[R1]] human' showed a race code. A bracketed name is unwrapped and race and fact codes are dropped from the prose. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place the prose only looks at is not a move (7B Saltmarrow run, T5): a question to the clerk came back with MOVE 'Salt-choked Channels' and prose ending 'the salt-choked channels stretching out before you', and the save went to the channels. When the prose names a talk turn's destination, a sentence now has to take the player there (you with an arrival, a departure or a reach, or a carried trip); the player's own line heading there still counts. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Coins handed over go to the purse (7B Saltmarrow run, T5): GRANT 'copper' TYPE coin put 'copper x1' in the pack and left the purse at 12. A granted item that is only coin is counted into gold. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] 'The salvage guild' is not salvaging (7B Saltmarrow run, T3): the noun read as the player taking something, so a job's anchor (the thing to retrieve) was granted when the notice was read. Salvage counts as a take only as a verb. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Talk-turn moves stand when the story travels (playtest #76, Saltmarrow run). A MOVE on a turn not read as travel was dropped unless the prose used the place's exact name with the player walking: 'Row me out to the old fish market' and a door poled 'to the tower' left the save behind while the prose arrived. The move now stands when the player's words name the place, or the prose shows it by a shorter name or a carried trip. A place merely named in passing ('the tally house' beside the tally board) is no longer walked into. A shop is entered straight from a place next door inside the same town. A dropped MOVE is journalled. A named landmark ('the Lantern Wives' lamp') is a place: it used to be refused and renamed 'Nearby street', so its MOVE matched nothing. - `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A place named in passing stands in the town (#33). 'The salvage guild's office on the harbour wall beside the tally board' was minted with no parent, so every walk from it into the chandlery ended out on the street. A new place the turn did not travel to, described as right here, now belongs to the place the player is in; far-off places and journey destinations keep their own standing. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Talking about someone's comings and goings is not travelling (#112). '"Which channel did Tam take, and when did he leave?"', '"Fetch the reeve"', 'the tally board' and 'walk the line round it' made talk turns travel turns, logging 'Travel action with no MOVE op' nearly every turn. Quoted words count only with the player in them ('want to come with me?'), movement words used as nouns are ignored, and 'I'm not going anywhere' is a denial. - `app/world.py`, `app/prose_state.py`
- [CLAUDE] Shop hours keep out custom, not the household (#113). At 06:48 Philippa's chandlery turned the player away as closed though she lives there and the player had been inside. A keeper at home who is not set against the player now lets a face they know in after hours. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Only real trades get premises (#27). Every person got '<Name>'s <shop>' whatever the job: weavers on a roof and in a sea tower got a Tailor's, the guild's tally clerk a Scribe's Office, a salvager a General Store. Weavers and clerks no longer plan a shop, a trade the era stands in for is not planned or assigned, and no premises are planned outside a settlement. - `app/venues.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Strangers only where the story shows them (#73). A seeded face takes the prose's own word for their trade ('the next runner' is a runner, not Wulfstan the weaver); dialogue a known person speaks no longer seeds a stranger (Tam's lines on the roof made 'Jasper, weaver'); waiting in a lone tower rolls no pickpockets or crowds, and only a fight keeps a face on record (one sleep left three standing strangers at the tally house); CAST off takes a minor face out of the place. - `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`
- [CLAUDE] The closing paragraphs of a long scene are kept (playtest #110, Saltmarrow run): the handoff kept only 8 narration segments and the DSL draft makes one per paragraph, so the 9th paragraph on vanished with no trace (T68: Thorne not moving to the board; T69: the harbour whistling at the reeve). Paragraphs past the cap now join the last kept one. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The overreach trim keeps the player's own work in the draft's words (reopened #97): "ring it. Hard." was cut from "You pull. You pull harder.", "haul the cart up the stairs" from "You take the shafts.", "pin them up on the board" from the pinning, and a rolled mishap's narration from the climb it came from. When the player's line declares physical work, a clause that does that work stays: it shares a word with the line, uses a plain effort verb, or takes hold of part of the work. Invented thoughts, speech, decisions, gestures and contrary acts ("You leave the charcoal") are still cut. In a replay of the run's 101 drafts the trim's cuts went from 18 to 6, all 6 correct. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A line that was only the player's name no longer ships as an empty quote (#108): '"" Jocosa repeats' now loses the quote and its tag. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] No link inside a name the engine does not hold, and no record name on an unnamed description (#109): "Philippa Kemp [[A]]'s General Store" stays "Philippa Kemp's General Store", and "a woman in a fish-scaled apron [[O]]" no longer becomes "a woman in a fish-scaled apron Ursula". - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Speech memory and the player's name (reopened #78): saying your name ('"Isk Tarrow. Thank you."', '"It's Isk."') or writing or signing it in someone's book now tells the people present. A possessive or an object is not who speaks, a word in scare quotes is not a line, link markup is not stored in remembered lines, and a line is only cut as a repeat of the same person's earlier words. - `app/conversation.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Fact titles read as names (reopened #23): "A drowned harbour town ..." was titled "Drowned and town" ("harbour" read as a verb) and "Salvage, debt and a tide that keeps the wrong hours" was titled "Wrong hours of Salvage debt and a tide". A verb-list word in a noun's place stays a noun, and a verb inside a relative clause no longer drives the title. - `app/world_facts.py`
- [CLAUDE] Signing for a job takes it (playtest #114, Saltmarrow run): 'wait at the window to sign for the bell', 'I take the pen and write my name in the book' and, with two offers open, 'We'll take it, Una. The tower and the bell.' left the offer waiting while the draft was told not to write the player taking it. Signing on, writing a name in the book and a plain 'we'll take it' now take the offer the line names by a word of its title or its giver; a bare 'I'll take it' takes the only offer only when the line is not buying something. The DSL draft's instruction now uses the status names the world state shows (player_takes_it_now, turned_down), and an offer the engine's quest clock posted but nobody has said aloud is shown as not_raised_yet instead of the giver's offer waiting for an answer. - `app/local_intel.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`
- [CLAUDE] Going with someone is read (#115): 'I go with Jocosa, behind her' (the Go with button's own words) left the lead waiting, because only a walk the town planner could take to a plot counted, and the lead had lost its place ('the Lantern Wives' lamp' became empty). A line that goes with, after, behind or beside the leader, follows them, or says 'lead on' answers the lead: the draft is told the player is going, and they come along as with the Go with button. The lead keeps the place words it was given. - `app/turn_prompts.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Signing is the first step (#116): accepting and signing in one turn left 'Sign for the contract' current for the rest of the quest, so every later step done advanced the step before the one the story was on. When the first step is signing on and the accepting line signs, the step is done too. - `app/quests.py`, `app/local_intel.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Another giver's job is not a duplicate (#117): Mara's 'Ring the customs bell' was rejected as a duplicate of the guild's 'Raise the customs-house bell' on the shared words. From a different giver, the titles must also open on the same verb, or be near-identical. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Resting, waiting and exhaustion follow the player's line (playtest #119, Saltmarrow run): 'the deep channel' and 'power' as nouns made lines ability turns, 'lie flat' was talk, 'I can't climb yet. I rest ...' was a climb and was itself blocked at zero energy, a breather during an ability use and 'we wait it out' recovered nothing, and 'I wait an hour here' passed six minutes. A typed breather, wait or sleep is now recovery and lasts as long as the line says ('an hour', 'two hours', 'until dawn', 'wait it out'); speech plus a hands-on act is the act; and an act blocked at zero energy rolls no check, so it can no longer leave a lasting injury. - `app/player_resources.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] 'Wanderer' takes roads (#122): five rows of one harbour town on day one earned 'walked many roads and seen many lands'. Places inside another place no longer count, and Wanderer needs five places over five in-game days (Seeker ten over ten); saves seeded with the old rule are moved to the new one. - `app/titles.py`
- [CLAUDE] The character's own trade counts (#103, Saltmarrow run): a Flat-runner whose backstory is running the flats failed 3 of 3 mud-bank climbs untrained, with injuries, because the title, backstory and setup proficiencies never reached the check. On ground or in work the character's title, backstory or proficiencies name, a physical check (and a craft or a reading when the act itself is part of the trade) is routine and stands on the best matching proficiency as related footing. Talk and fights are never home ground. - `app/skill_checks.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The GM can write a person's tie to the player and cast someone made this turn (#106, Saltmarrow run). REL with PLAYER (or you, me) keeps the tie on the person as a fact, and a family tie makes the player's words for that relative aliases of them, so 'Mam' finds her mother; it used to pass as the code YOU, which no row holds. REL and CAST also take a person's name in quotes, so someone NPC_NEW makes this turn can be cast without guessing their code. - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The opening scene knows who the player is (#100): its packet had a name, a title and worn clothes only. It now carries what the player carries (by code), their abilities and what they do, their trained skills with rank, the backstory and the premise. The proficiency pass is told the player's name; it was told "". - `app/prompts.py`, `app/proficiencies.py`
- [CLAUDE] An ability in use says what it does (#101): every use shipped the name "" and 'strength as described' with nothing described. The packet names the ability and carries its description, and the engine note adds a 'What it does' line. - `app/prompts.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/player_resources.py`
- [CLAUDE] People move with the player (#105, Saltmarrow run). A move now takes along anyone the player's own line takes: 'Tam and I go', 'with Tam', 'I carry Tam up the ladder', 'Tam's arm over my shoulder', or a name in a 'we ... together' sentence; someone the prose shows staying behind still stays. And a person the GM casts present, whose record sits elsewhere and whom this turn's prose names, is moved here; they used to be dropped from the scene, so the skiff's passengers stayed recorded at the tower. - `app/scene_thread.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Who the player is talking to follows the line (playtest #104, Saltmarrow run). A role only one person here holds names them ('the tide clerk' went to the weaver who spoke last); a surname nobody else here shares names them ('Reeve Thorne' went to Hereward); a line with no speech, question, order or word of dealing with anyone is said to nobody ('Tam and I set the iron bar', 'I look for Philippa in the crowd' no longer make someone answer, and 'the crowd' is no longer the whole room); a kinship word nobody answers to ('Six years, Mam') no longer falls to the last partner. - `app/conversation.py`


## [0.10.0-dev.12] - 2026-10-13

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. First fixes from the Saltmarrow run: levels rise with XP, sleep heals, a sum the prose names is the sum that moves, checks read only the player's own line (no more persuasion on every turn) with the addressee as the opponent, fewer keyword misreads, and no more names written into sentences or travel scenes cut back to a seat.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Levels rise with XP (playtest #121, Saltmarrow run): the run ended at 293 XP and level 1, because only a model-proposed level_delta could raise a level and the DSL never sends one. Level now follows XP on a fixed curve (100 XP for level 2, 300 for 3, 600 for 4, ...), only ever rises, and journals 'Level N reached'. It is checked after every turn and every quest payout, and it respects the leveling_system playthrough option. - `app/progression.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/quests.py`
- [CLAUDE] Sleep heals (#120): 13 and 17 hours of sleep restored energy but no health. A sleep now heals 1 per 2 hours, and at least a fifth of max health after 6 hours or more; meditation heals 1 per 4 hours; a plain wait heals nothing. Healing never exceeds missing health and never revives someone at 0. - `app/player_resources.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A sum the prose names is the sum that moves (reopened #69/#96): GOLD -9 with 'Nine coins. All you had.' was rolled as a band, and a returned purse of nine coins paid 14. A loss the prose names is taken exactly; a gain of up to 50 that the prose names is paid exactly; anything larger or unnamed still rolls. 'count out', 'put', 'set', 'lay' and 'place' now count as offering coin. - `app/prose_state.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Name repair stops writing names into sentences (#107): 'Turn and wedge.' became 'Tam Kemp [[H]] Turn and wedge', and 'H. says' got another person's name. Only a lowercase verb with no subject is filled now, never inside quotes and never after an initial. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A travel scene is no longer cut back to a seat (#111): 'You take the stern.' was read as the player taking an item, and the whole trip to the customs tower was cut to that line. Taking a position in a boat or on a watch (stern, bow, helm, oars, tiller, ...) is not acquiring, and a turn whose line is travel is never cut at an invented request. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] A turn with someone present no longer rolls persuasion (reopened #89, Saltmarrow run): the skill table read the engine's own note after the player's line ('the player is talking to Tam Kemp'), so walking out, paying, thanking and a silent rope-haul each rolled a contested persuasion, and a fumble gave a speech blunder on a silent turn. Only the player's own line is read now. The opponent is the person the line names, else the person the engine decided the player is talking to, else the only person present; it was the first local in the list, often a listener or someone who had left. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] Fewer checks from stray words (#102): 'lie flat' is not a lie, 'round mine' is not mining, 'no cart's worth you' is not a price, 'strike the flint' is not a blow, climbing into a skiff is not a feat, 'watch how fast the water climbs' is the water's climb, and a denied verb ('I'm not hiding') is no act. Real lies, prices, blows and climbs still roll. - `app/skill_checks.py`
- [CLAUDE] The API-bridge playtest harness lists only items you hold (#118 was a harness artefact: the game already hides zero-quantity rows). - `tools/api_bridge_playtest.py`


## [0.10.0-dev.11] - 2026-10-13

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. A new 100-turn lore teaser, Saltmarrow, played with Claude as player and GM through the cloud-API path; the API bridge harness and a read-only world database exporter; the run's findings logged as #100-#122 with ten older issues reopened.

### Added

- [TOOL] `tools/api_bridge_playtest.py`: play Mørkyn through its cloud-API path with an agent answering every model call. A local OpenAI-compatible server writes each request to `<dir>/calls/NNNN.request.json` (with a readable view) and waits for `NNNN.reply.txt`; player lines arrive as `<dir>/inbox/NNN.txt`, `!wait <minutes> <kind>` presses the Wait/Meditate/Sleep button, `STOP` ends the run, and `--resume` continues a run dir. Each turn is recorded in `<dir>/turns/NNN.json` and the world database is exported every 10 turns. All data paths are isolated inside the run dir.
- [TOOL] `tools/export_world_db.py`: export a world database read-only (`mode=ro`) to `<label>.json` (every table, columns, row counts and rows; long cells cut to 4,000 characters unless `--full`) and a readable `<label>.md` (player, places, people, items, abilities, skills, quests, dice, engine journal, row counts).
- [DOCS] New 100-turn lore teaser, Saltmarrow: Claude played player and GM through the cloud-API path (228 model calls) while the engine ran unchanged. `docs/showcase/saltmarrow-100-turns.md` with the full transcript, metrics, setup, findings and start/midpoint/final database exports; the README's teaser section now points to it, and the old engine-only run stays linked.
- [DOCS] Issue log: 23 new issues from the run (#100-#122) and reopen notes on #23, #27, #33, #69, #73, #76, #78, #89, #96, #97, one entry per root cause with every turn it was seen.

## [0.10.0-dev.10] - 2026-10-13

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Fixes #96-#99 from the README example turn on the 8B (a purchase the prose shows is granted and paid, the coin the player offered stays in the scene, one act advances one quest, an item named once is one), theme abilities follow the world's magic level, and the README says MLE runs in place of a llama.cpp server.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Buying from someone is asking for the item (playtest #96, the README example turn on the 8B): 'ask Finnian if he'd sell me a whetstone. I'll pay 2 gold for it' was not read as wanting anything, so the whetstone the prose hands over was refused and the 2 gold voided. 'sell me' and 'pay ... for' join the one take vocabulary that the grant gate and the overreach trim share. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The coins the player offered stay in the scene (#97): 'You pull out two gold coins and hand them over' was cut as an act the player never asked for, although the line offered the gold and the draft was told to show it. A sentence about coin is the player's own when their line pays, buys or gives coin. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] One act advances one quest (#98): 'I set the last polished blade on the rack' completed both 'Polish swords' and 'Learn metalworking' and paid both. Step and completion updates that cite the same sentence now advance only the quest whose title and current step fit it best; the rest are rejected as same_act_as. - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] One thing named once is one (#99): 'a whetstone' drafted as QTY moderate rolled two. An item the line or prose names with a/an/one/the, never plural and never with a count, rolls band small (one); stacks still roll. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] A rare-magic world no longer draws theme abilities as often as a cultivation world: since the library, the offline fallback drew templates by theme alone, so 'rare' and 'cultivation' both came out 86-90% supernatural and test_magic_coherence failed about two runs in three. Supernatural templates now weigh 0.4 in a rare world and mundane ones 0.5 in a common one; magic off still draws none. - `app/ability_library.py`
- [TEST] tests/test_playtest96_whetstone.py: 11 cases; 9 fail on the code before the fix. The 8B turn replayed on a save copy now grants one whetstone, pays 2 gold and completes one quest.
- [DOCS] README: MLE runs in place of a llama.cpp server (the same engine, in-process, the default), and Model setup shows MLE and the llama.cpp server side by side with full GGUF paths.

## [0.10.0-dev.9] - 2026-10-12

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Mørkyn's own window with the launcher settings in the game, abilities whose growth the engine computes from a model-picked template and seed, a theme ability library that learns verified new powers, a death screen and Ironman, read-aloud narration, fixes #85-#94, thirteen built-but-not-wired modules, and the README's MLE section with changelogs split out.

### Added

- [CLAUDE] Theme ability templates that the library learns from. `content/ability_templates/` holds 204 hand-written abilities over the 17 director themes (12 each, every one through the same quality gate as a generated card, in the two-part growth shape, flagged supernatural or not). `app/ability_library.py` scores the setup against each theme's keywords so a custom setting draws from its best two or three themes (a "neon megacity where corporate sects teach qi" mixes cyberpunk and wuxia), and hands the model eight fresh templates per call, the least used first, none supernatural in a no-magic world. The model adapts a template (`template_id`) or invents a new power (`"new"`); a new one that passes the quality gate on engine growth, is not a near-copy of anything in the library and is not a fallback card is stored in `data/ability_library.json` (beside the world database; `AI_RPG_ABILITY_LIBRARY` overrides) with the setup's themes, and is offered in later calls. Abilities a game starts with count as chosen and come up more. Fallbacks draw theme templates before the generic pool, and every pool card is now tagged and never learned. Measured before (8B, 125 independent abilities, 4 themes): no repeat inside one batch, 0-6 repeats per ~30 across separate rolls, "echo" in 22 of 125 names across every theme, and 27 pool cards unlabelled. - `app/ability_library.py`, `content/ability_templates/*.json`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`, `tools/playtest_setup_presets.py`
- [CLAUDE] Abilities grow by the engine, in two parts. The model picks a growth template (compounding, steady, soft cap, breakthrough, passive, item-bound, flat) and a seed in words (pace slow/steady/fast, start weak/ordinary/strong, what grows); `app/ability_growth.py` turns that into the rank ladder (the world's `rank_scale`), XP to each rank (scaled by pace and difficulty), XP per use with risk multipliers, soft caps or breakthrough walls, and the strength at each rank, and writes `growth_math` from those numbers so it cannot contradict itself. Each ability now has a rank: `abilities.growth` / `growth_state` store the spec and rank/XP/uses, every use earns XP through `apply_ability_use` (risk from the game: an active fight is dangerous, a fight at low health life-risk, the player's own contest words contested), and the storyteller is told the rank, progress and any rank-up instead of being asked to do arithmetic. Measured first on Qwen3 8B (`tools/ability_growth_8b.py`, 79 abilities over 3 themes): model-written math copied the prompt's stock examples in 58 of 79, at most 2 per theme had no fault, and applying its own math the model named the right rank 28% of the time; no live game ever changed an ability. The setup card shows pace, start and "grows" with a read-only preview from the server (`POST /api/ability-growth/preview`), and the in-game sheet shows the live rank. - `app/ability_growth.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/player_resources.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Mørkyn opens in its own window. `Morkyn.bat` starts `morkyn_app.py` (pywebview + WebView2, under `pythonw`, so no terminal stays open): a boot page shows the startup log, then the game loads; closing the window stops the server and any managed llama.cpp server, and a Windows job object stops them even if the window is killed. The game is still a website any browser or phone can open while it runs. `--browser` opens the system browser instead, `--console` runs in a terminal (any OS). `morkyn_app.py` is the entry point a packaged .exe will build from (`--serve` replaces `-m uvicorn` when frozen). - `morkyn_app.py`, `app/launcher.py`, `static/boot.html`, `Morkyn.bat`, `requirements.txt`
- [CLAUDE] The terminal launcher's settings moved into the game. The main menu has a Gatehouse strip (Where, Engine, Pipeline, and the address phones use); Settings has the rest of the Advanced board (port, models, context and response tokens, llama.cpp GPU layers / flash attention / log / startup wait, draft format, consolidator, fast verification, skip DSL verifier, where the game opens) plus Restore defaults. Settings that need a restart say so and offer Restart now: the server shuts down cleanly through `POST /api/launcher/restart`, the launcher starts it again with the new prefs, and the window follows, to the new port if it changed. A start that fails (port in use, missing GGUF) shows why, with a form to fix Where, port, engine, or GGUF path and retry. - `app/main.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`, `static/ui/interact.js`
- [CLAUDE] Pop-out (↗) in Mørkyn's window opens a separate native window titled like the browser popup ("Mørkyn — Player"), with the same header; it syncs with the game over a BroadcastChannel because it has no `window.opener`. In a normal browser ↗ still opens a browser popup. - `static/app.js`, `static/popout.html`, `morkyn_app.py`
- [CLAUDE] The window and pop-out title bars wear the theme on Windows 11: caption in the void (`--bg`), title in the thread (`--thread`), a dim thread line for the border, re-sent when the theme changes; the icon is the logo's Ø drawn as one thread of light instead of a 16-px crop of the key art. Native snap, drag and caption buttons are untouched. - `static/ui/frame.js`, `morkyn_app.py`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] A setup card's growth type never reached the game: the Start request model had no `power_type` field, so every ability started as linear. It now carries `power_type` and the growth seed. - `app/main.py`
- [CLAUDE] Start re-rolled every ability's lock, overwriting the card's "Starts usable / Needs unlock" and locking the OP seed power on turn one (11-13 of ~26 abilities per theme in the 8B run). Locks are rolled once, when the powers are made. - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Ability generation no longer spends a model call rewriting growth math (it ran on every compounding roll) or retries for math the quality gate could not parse. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Good model abilities were thrown away for a shared cost: three cards scoring 100 were denied as a batch for "meditation-hour recharge spam", the 8B answered the retry by echoing the prompt, and the empty reply was padded from the seed pool and labelled "llm_retry". The clone-cost repair now runs before the gate, and a failed retry keeps the model's own last batch (the pool stays the final fallback, labelled as one). On the 8B, 12-ability runs per theme: every growth line consistent (36 of 36, was 3 of 79), cards in the seed pool's names down to 1-6 per 12 from most of wuxia. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Web Settings showed defaults instead of the saved launcher prefs: the PowerShell Gatehouse writes `data/launcher_prefs.json` with a BOM, which the Python loader could not parse. It also turned the Gatehouse's `network` reach into `local` on save, and dropped `llama_cpp_flash_attn`, `llm_log_mode`, `dsl_skip_verify` and `llm_startup_timeout` from the file. One table of keys and env vars now serves the launcher and the server. - `app/launcher_prefs.py`
- [CLAUDE] The app window forgot localStorage (theme, layout, tutorial flags) on every launch: pywebview defaults to private mode. Its profile now lives in `data/webview/profile`. - `morkyn_app.py`
- [CLAUDE] Context-size tips pointed to the Gatehouse menu's option C; they point to Settings. - `app/failsafe.py`, `static/ui/failsafe.js`
- [CLAUDE] Scrolling a dialog (Settings, Speech, Load / Edit save, NPC roster, starter logic, legal, Forge path, death, failsafe) or the encounter dialog drew the rows over its frame: the cut-corner frame is painted as background and a scroller clips at its padding edge, so content scrolling past the top and bottom covered the edge lines and corners, and the scrollbar sat on the edge. These shards now draw the frame in a transparent border band as wide as the corners (`--shard-band`), outside the clip; padding gives the band back so nothing moves. `tests/test_ui_shard_scroll.py` fails if any shard is given its own scrolling without the band. - `static/ui/skin.css`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] The On/Off groups in Settings were tall empty boxes with each radio stacked over its word: they inherited the setup page's four-row toggle grid (rows for a description Settings never has) and the Settings "caption above input" label layout. Scoped to Settings: legend, then the choices on one line. - `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] Terminal windows flashed open during play (several per turn with the narration pipeline on). The new app window runs the server without a console, so every console tool it ran got its own window: `nvidia-smi`, which `app/gpu_gate.py` runs on every model call, opened a Windows Terminal window each time; `git`, `pip`, `powershell` and the managed llama.cpp server could too. Reproduced from a console-less process (3 calls, 3 terminal windows), then 0 with the fix, and 0 over 5 `/api/gpu-gate` calls on a supervised server. `app/quiet_children.py`, installed from `app/__init__.py`, makes `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` the default for every subprocess the app starts unless the caller asks for a console. - `app/quiet_children.py`, `app/__init__.py`
- [CLAUDE] The Player / Character pop-out drew the body art frame on top of the face frame and over the text beside it ("racter art", "ce + body"): a pop-out-only rule still squeezed the art into the 160 px column of the old single portrait, while the art is now a 250 px face + body pair. The rule (and a dead one for `.playerPortraitFrame`) is gone. In the same pop-out the art buttons were smaller than in the in-game sheet (the button-size reset was scoped to the drawer), the stat tiles left an empty strip (`auto-fill` reserved empty tracks), and in a narrow card Generate ran off the edge (the kind select would not shrink). All 13 tabs now pop out with no overflow or overlap at 1100 and 560 px. - `static/styles.css`, `static/popout.html`
- [CLAUDE] The pop-out never picked up new CSS: `popout.html` named fixed `?v=popout-skin-1` URLs, so any browser that had opened one kept that stylesheet for good. It is now served at `/popout` with the game's content token. - `app/main.py`, `static/popout.html`, `static/app.js`, `morkyn_app.py`, `docs/UI_RULEBOOK.md`

- [CLAUDE] Health 0 is a thing that happens (playtest #88, from the 59-turn Deeper-idea run on the 8B, where health reached 0 at turn 15 and 44 turns followed). Every turn the draft, the verifier and the DSL contract get `mechanics_context.health_state` (health, band fine/bruised/hurt/critical/down, conditions) and the player view says `health: 0/20 (down)`; the turn health reaches 0 is narrated as the drop, and the next turn is the coming round by the setup's death rule: health back to a quarter, 'lasting injuries' adds a severe lasting wound, 'permadeath threat' rolls a d20 and a 1-3 costs 2 max health, 'narrative setback' marks the scene lost. The player's line that turn does not happen and rolls nothing. - `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`
- [CLAUDE] Asking a question no longer rolls a hard contested check that can wound you (#89-#92). A plain ask/greet/talk is routine (two easier, only a natural 1 goes wrong); convince/persuade/haggle/plead/demand keep the full roll. The contested DC sits at the skill's base for an average person (base + power - 8) instead of 15-20. An unskilled miss is a mishap on a natural 1 or a miss by 7+, and only the natural 1 is critical (28 of 49 rolls were 'critical' before). A social fumble is a blunder the prose can show, not a limb injury; a bad physical miss costs 1 health and no condition; a physical natural 1 still leaves a lasting injury. Conditions merge: the same limb hurt again is one row with a count. - `app/skill_checks.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`
- [CLAUDE] Verification memory keys on the scene, not the player's words (#93): `focus_terms` left the scope signature, so the same check in the same scene with the same references now matches (282 rows under 282 signatures in the run). - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The Ideas and Deeper idea remember what the player already tried (#94): `scene.recent_player_inputs` carries the last six player lines with a rule not to repeat or rephrase them, and `world_state.player.condition` plus `injuries` with a rule that a hurt player's move answers the body first. - `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Work and craft actions roll real dice against the player's proficiencies (#85a). The setup payload now sends the Checks tab, so 'Dice checks: On' is kept (it was stored as off, and save 83a1ec4c rolled nothing); old saves take the Checks tab from their saved setup form once on load. A declared act (polish, mend, forge, cook, build, haul, treat, sing...) is one reading for the check skill, the energy kind and the minutes, so polishing a blade rolls Smithing, costs as physical work and takes 20 minutes; routine work is easier and only a natural 1 hurts. A check uses the best matching proficiency, read through its 'tracked by' note (Rigging tracked by carpentry counts for Craft), a neighbouring trade at half rank, or untrained at -2 for craft work, and the roll line names it. Doing the work is practice: a new trade is learned and a known one grows. The draft is told the act's outcome before it writes (act_outcome) and the next turns are told how it went (previous_act); checks the model proposes are no longer rolled after the prose. - `static/app.js`, `app/main.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/prose_state.py`, `app/skill_checks.py`, `app/player_resources.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] A quest task done in play finishes the quest and pays it (#85b). Polishing the swords at the armory with Finnian there never moved 'Polish swords': only the quest parser could advance a step, and it did not. The engine now matches the player's declared act to the open quest step at that place (one quest per act), rolls it with the act's check, finishes the step on a success only, and tells the prose the outcome and the pay before it is written. Every completion (play, the parser, the Advance step button) pays through one function: gold, XP (with the economy, leveling and growth settings), karma, and reward items, with a journal line naming the amounts and a Gold and quest line in the turn's rewards. One offer sentence no longer makes two quests. The turn's rewards show every gold rise, like XP. - `app/quests.py`, `app/quest_parser.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/llm.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Review repairs for #85. A gold offer reaches the draft on every turn again (#75 had been tied to turns after a rolled act). Asking someone to do work, watching it, promising it for later, quoting it or asking a question about it is not the player doing it: no roll, no work cost, no practice, no quest step. Work and talk in one line roll both; a performance stays social; campaign skill triggers come first. A retry of failed work is told only the new roll, and every prose pass is told how the last attempt went. A quest step needs more than the verb ('polish my boots' does not finish 'Polish swords'), needs the giver there for work at the giver's place, and one act cannot finish a second quest through the parser. Practice growth is paid once, an exhausted body's attempt is a failure in the prose too, and quest reward items rewind. - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prose_state.py`, `app/skill_checks.py`, `app/quests.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`

### Added

- [CLAUDE] A "You died" screen and an Ironman mode (user request 2026-10-11, TODO n30). The engine records a death in `settings.player_dead` (the browser reads `state.dead`), `play_turn` refuses every turn after it, and the main menu's Continue does not offer a dead character. Under `death_rules` 'permadeath threat' a 1-3 on the coming-round d20 is now the death turn (narrated as the death, no HEALTH line) instead of a max-health cut; the earlier autosaves stay loadable. Ironman (a Rules toggle, off by default) makes health 0 the death whatever Death says, and removes the character's saves; a full export and the slots are copied to `data/graveyard/` (`AI_RPG_GRAVEYARD`) first, and the screen does not say so. The screen (`static/ui/death.js`, loaded with the bundle) shows after the death turn's prose, when a dead save is opened, and when a turn is refused: who, the cause, day, turns survived, place, level, then Load a save / Main menu, or Begin again for ironman. - `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `static/ui/death.js`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`
- [BE] Text to speech for the narration (TODO n24): one settings row `tts_config` (provider off / piper / openai / elevenlabs, off by default; `AI_RPG_TTS_*` env overrides win), a catalog of known-good presets (six Piper voices; OpenAI `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, `tts-1` and a custom OpenAI-compatible URL; ElevenLabs Multilingual v2, Turbo v2.5, Flash v2.5; each with voice, model, sample rate, speed range and request size), text cleaning (no `[[codes]]`, bare `L1` codes, HTML, markdown, op or debug lines), sentence-aware chunking, the three provider calls over `urllib`, the Piper install and voice download path, and a status probe. Routes `GET` / `POST /api/tts-config`, `GET /api/tts-catalog`, `POST /api/tts-status`, `POST /api/tts/speak` (one paragraph in, `audio/wav` or `audio/mpeg` out; 400 off, empty or not ready, 409 while a turn or image job holds the GPU, 503 backend unreachable) and `POST /api/tts/install`. Local synthesis runs behind the GPU gate and answers 409 instead of queueing, so speech never competes with a turn; cloud calls skip the gate. - `app/tts.py`, `app/main.py`
- [FE] Read aloud: one Play / Pause / Stop bar under the turn's narration speaks it paragraph by paragraph (the next paragraph is fetched while the current one plays); right-click inside the narration offers Play selected text, Play this paragraph and Stop (a name's menu keeps its own items and gains these); playback stops when a turn, Continue, Wait, Regenerate or Rewind is sent or the main menu opens. Speech settings (provider, voice or preset, speed, API key with 'leave blank to keep', Test, Install local speech engine) sit under the main-menu Settings panel and behind a Speech item in the play menu. A missing Piper voice downloads on the first Play. Nothing shows while speech is off. - `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/ui/interact.js`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`
- [TEST] Speech regression: cleaning, chunking, defaults, env overrides and key masking, catalog shape, speak refusals (off, empty, gate busy, too long, no key), request shaping for all three providers with a mocked transport, the Piper path with a fake engine and fake voice files, install error paths (pip failure, download failure, bad voice file) and the status probe; 65 unittest cases, nothing leaves the machine. A node check runs the pure read-aloud helpers of app.js and syntax-checks app.js and interact.js. - `tests/test_tts.py`, `tools/test_tts_ui.js`
- [BILD] `.env.example` gains the commented `AI_RPG_TTS_*` block (provider, enabled, preset, voice, model, base URL, API key, speed, chunk chars, timeout, voice dir) and `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY`. The local engine is not a requirement: `piper-tts==1.8.0` stays out of `requirements.txt` and is installed on demand from Speech settings into the running interpreter (GPL-3.0-or-later wheel, player-installed, never bundled); voices land in `data/tts-voices/` (gitignored). - `.env.example`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n1): settlement market simulation. One `market_state` row per settlement and goods category (stock in days of consumption, demand, production and consumption per day from the settlement's size and type), a season read from the world day, scarcity events rolled from the campaign seed and kept in `market_events`, a small seeded drift so a rewind replays the same market, `settlement_multiplier()` as the one price multiplier trade reads, `tick_day()` for whole days returning world-event proposals it never inserts, `market_lines()` for one or two world facts, and a settings row `economy_config`. The module creates its own two tables through `ensure_schema`; `init_db` does not call it and nothing in the live game imports it. - `app/economy.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n15): prices and trade. The first time an item is priced it is recorded in `price_ledger` and `price_index` and kept; later sales start from that price or the running average and move with the economy multiplier; a purchase becomes a `trade_offers` row only `accept_offer()` turns into a purse and inventory proposal; haggling (`resolve_haggle`: skill check, relationship band, keeper mood, counter price, up to three rounds), alternatives from the same seller and a typed answer reader are pure functions; `fulfilment_proposal()` returns the changes and applies nothing. Every amount is an int of the smallest currency unit. Not called by the live game. - `app/trade.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n18): goods-for-service agreements. "These 15 iron ore for a blade, you keep the leftovers" is an `agreements` row with what each side gives, the work owed, a due time in absolute world minutes and a leftovers policy; it is offered and haggled as a trade offer of kind service or barter, `agree()` makes the row, `tick()` moves it through ready, lapsed and forfeit as the clock says, and hand-over, delivery and cancellation are proposals (inventory changes, a money delta, journal notes, world events) with an `agreement_events` audit. Not called by the live game. - `app/agreements.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n17): currency by theme. Currency sets (medieval coin with 100 copper = 1 silver, 100 silver = 1 gold, 100 gold = 1 platinum; credits; cash; post-collapse scrip that leans on goods), detection from the world's theme, smallest-unit storage with format, parse and convert, the two bridges to the single `player.gold` number, a settings row `currency_config`, and `MIGRATION_PLAN`, the written twelve-step plan for moving that number. Nothing here reads or writes `player.gold`. - `app/currency.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n16): hunger and thirst. Two 0..100 needs in one settings row `player_needs` fall with world minutes (faster when travelling, slower asleep, thirst faster in heat) and rise when the player eats or drinks an inventory item the word tables recognise; bands give a recovery multiplier, fatigue per hour, energy and health loss per hour and a sustained-low penalty after twelve hours under 25; eating and drinking are inventory-change proposals; status lines and a prompt block are returned as text. Not called by the live game. - `app/needs.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n23): body-location health. Head, torso, each arm, each hand and each leg; a wound has a kind, damage, severity 1..4 and exactly one engine-owned basic state (bleeding, open, bandaged, broken, splinted, infected, healing, bruised), each a data row for what it does per hour, rolls per day, blocks and which treatments move it where; bandage, splint, clean, salve and stitch are engine facts (a bandage stops bleeding and does not heal); model-added modifier states are stored and shown but never read by a rule; capability effects (two-handed work, climbing, fine work, travel speed, combat) come from a table; `health_from_body()` and `wounds_from_health_delta()` map to and from the one HP number. Tables `body_wounds` and `body_health_log`, created by the module alone. - `app/body_health.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n13): capture and restraint. One settings row `restraint` and an audit table `restraint_log`; `may_move()` answers every position change with one verdict (map step, story walk, town walk, leave, Travel press, location move, venue enter and exit) and in-place play stays open; release comes from world time or a condition (skill level, quest stage, payment, world event, the captor); a capture counts only when the prose signal is backed by an engine fact (a lost check, a lost fight, an outnumbered ambush, surrender, an event); an escort is a planned path over the existing walkers applied by the engine's own writers; `RESTRAIN`, `RELEASE` and `ESCORT` are parsed but not registered as ops. Not called by the live game. - `app/restraint.py`
- [LLM] Built, not wired (TODO n14): scene cast with placeholder slots. `build_involved()` lists the people, items and places a beat may use as `NPC_1` / `ITEM_1` / `PLACE_1`, reusing known codes and offering at most one new face; `bind()` maps `{NPC_1}` in prose and ops to `Name [[CODE]]` and proposes the NPC_NEW rows for new faces with drawn names; `verify()` gates an unbound placeholder, a slot bound to gear, an item or place used as an agent, a clothing name, cast overflow and a speaker outside the cast; `propose_repairs()` answers with a deterministic refill or cut or flags one prose re-ask. No table, no prompt text, no opcode change; nothing in the draft flow calls it. - `app/scene_cast.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO g8): settlement visits. One `settlement_visits` row per settlement id with first visit, last visit and count; `record_enter()` returns whether this was a first visit, a proposed journal line and, while no ruler exists, a seeding proposal (ruler, two officers, two workers with roles and power ranks); re-entry within 30 world minutes on the same turn is the same visit; `ensure_settlement_ruler` stays the one seeder. Not called by the live game. - `app/settlement_visits.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n22, the engine-answer gap): wild places. A where-question about hunting grounds, a ford, a cave, a ruin, standing stones, fresh water, high ground, woods, a marsh, a road or a hidden camp is answered from the tiles around the player plus landmarks, hidden bases and lived knowledge, in the direction-hint shape local intel already uses (told or not, blurred past four cells, a compass word and one sentence), with a Travel there candidate for the prompt gate. No table, no settings; not called by the live game. - `app/wild_places.py`
- [GPLAY] Built, not wired (TODO n2): a shared real-time clock. A settings row `realtime_clock` holds the ratio of world minutes to real minutes, the enabled and paused flags and an anchor; owed world minutes are caught up in Wait-sized slices with the effects of a Wait of that length, capped at a day and re-based, never while a turn is in flight or travel is pending; `catch_up()` without an apply callable only plans and writes nothing. Hosted multiplayer is a documented non-goal: one campaign's clock in one process. Not called by the live game. - `app/realtime_clock.py`
- [FE] Built, not wired (TODO g17): display sequencing. A queue for the one narration area: scenes show one at a time, an ambient line that arrives while a scene is streaming is held and shown when the stream has finished (order turn, then seq), a line whose turn is older than the last scene shown is dropped, a third waiting scene finishes the current one instead of cutting it; pure sequencing over render and show callbacks that never touches the DOM. Not loaded by any page. - `static/ui/scene_sequence.js`
- [FE] Built, not wired (TODO n22, the map-drawing gap): the set destination on the maps. `draw(canvas, destination, player, mode)` paints a flag on the target world cell on the lens and the Large map (a rim arrow when it is off the drawn window, a ring when it is the player's own cell), the target city cell on the Settlement canvas and the target plot or cell on Streets, reading only the canvas, the painter's meta and the arguments it is given. Not loaded by any page. - `static/ui/destination_marker.js`
- [TEST] Built, not wired: thirteen test modules for the thirteen parked files, 464 unittest cases plus 46 node cases, each with a `test_module_is_a_leaf` case that fails as soon as any file under `app/` or `static/` names the module (or a page loads the script), with the module's docstring lines asserted; writer modules add a "no foreign writes" check (player, inventory, npcs, journal and settings rows untouched); no model, no network, temp paths only. The full suite goes from 2190 to 2654 cases with the same pre-existing failures and none new. - `tests/test_currency.py`, `tests/test_economy.py`, `tests/test_trade.py`, `tests/test_agreements.py`, `tests/test_needs.py`, `tests/test_body_health.py`, `tests/test_restraint.py`, `tests/test_scene_cast.py`, `tests/test_settlement_visits.py`, `tests/test_wild_places.py`, `tests/test_realtime_clock.py`, `tools/test_scene_sequence.js`, `tools/test_destination_marker.js`
- [DOCS] Built, not wired: the TODO list gains a "Built but not tied in" section (one row per parked module: what exists, the hook points, the Turn on checklist, the test count, and the decisions still open), the partial and backlog rows are marked, and a new page collects every module's hook list and checklist with the shared shapes they agree on. - `docs/TODO_NEXT.md`, `docs/BuiltNotWired.md`, `docs/README.md`, `CODEBASE_INDEX.md`

### Changed

- [DOCS] Version notes and devlogs move out of the README: `CHANGELOGS.md` indexes `changelogs/<version>.md` (0.10.0-dev, 0.9.12, 0.9.1-wip, 0.9.0) and `changelogs/devlogs/` (the GitHub release notes, moved from `RELEASE_NOTES.md`, and Markdown copies of the itch.io devlogs). The README keeps a two-line Latest. The MLE section gains a table of what the LLM does against what the engine does, and one real 8B turn shown end to end: the player's line, what the engine decided first, the packet the LLM got, its raw draft, and what the engine kept, cut, rolled and refused. That turn's four engine mistakes are logged as playtest #96-#99. - `README.md`, `CHANGELOGS.md`, `changelogs/`, `docs/PLAYTEST_ISSUES.md`
- [DOCS] README gains an "MLE — the Mørkyn Large Language Model Engine" section: what the model writes (prose and closed ops, never code), the order of one turn (engine reads the line, rolls checks, builds context; model drafts; verifier and repairs; engine applies, rolls bands, gates gains), setup split the same way, and the local runtime. Highlights and RELEASE_NOTES.md cover the app window, engine ability growth, the theme library and Ironman; new itch devlog `Itch.io/Devlog-0.10.0-dev.9.txt`. - `README.md`, `RELEASE_NOTES.md`, `Itch.io/`
- [BE] The speech API key never leaves the server in clear: `get_state()` blanks `settings.tts_config.api_key` for every payload that embeds the state, `GET /api/tts-config` returns `api_key_set` and a four-character hint instead, `GET /api/export` scrubs it from the settings table (`scrub_tts_export`), and a blank key on `POST /api/tts-config` keeps the stored one. - `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/tts.py`

## [0.10.0-dev.8] - 2026-10-08

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Fixes #69-#82 from the dev.7 hand-played game (town edges, Leave and walls, energy, prose/state agreement, NPC voice, quest offers) with Accept / Decline, Go with / Stay and Travel there prompts, and a UI layout pass. Not yet play-tested.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Review repairs, town (#70, #71): a port to another cell of the same town that the edge joins merged into a nearby point (or that the metre rounding put one tile off its join) gets its own stub again, so those cells no longer fail with 'a port is not on a road' (four cities of the live world did); the exit planner skips an outer edge that faces water or a mountain and the marker is never set on one; a world-map pick from inside a town is checked (walkable, within a walk of the exit) before the walk out is written, and a step refused after the walk out answers with the walk out instead of an error; a typed leave is a denial only when the negation governs the leave itself ('without a word I leave the city' leaves). Stored cells are unchanged; only cells generated from now on get the stubs. - `app/town_grid.py`, `app/town_moves.py`, `app/main.py`
- [CLAUDE] Review repairs, energy (#80): a line is costed as talk only when it is said in that line (speech, second person, or a this-line address), not because someone was addressed before; work done while talking is physical; 'learn', 'teach' and 'cast' no longer make a spoken line training or a fight; a spoken line with a stray 'go' is talk; a typed move outside a town pays its old travel point and keeps the zero-energy gate (only a town walk is charged by the walk); a typed rest or meditation recovers at the Wait control's rate; a rewind gives back the turn's energy and carry remainder. - `app/world.py`, `app/player_resources.py`
- [CLAUDE] Review repairs, prose and state (#69, #75, #76, #77): only prices and payments the prose names pass unrolled; a reward the prose names still rolls its band, and coin the prose shows received with no GOLD op mints nothing; paid and received amounts are read outside quotes, so an NPC's terms are not a payment; one amount the narration shows paid wins over a haggled offer; a refused offer is not charged even with a GOLD op; a gesture ('gives you a warm nod') is no acceptance and only the first answering quote counts; 'I'll take the job for 10 gold' is not an offer to pay; a purchase is refused only for a price somebody named (a band-rolled cost is clamped to the purse as before), and a payment the gates undid is trimmed from the prose; a sentence that also shows a kept item stays; an offered item is not a hand-over. A refused move now ends the scene before any op is applied and the cut part's ops go with it; a tail cut keeps a move, take or payment the player asked for; a scene cut at an NPC's question is not lengthened again. The declared act skips names ('Blind Owl Forge'), nouns ('the forge') and wishes ('would love to learn how to forge'); a denied clause ends at 'so i' / 'and i'; one take vocabulary for the act pass and the grant gate; 'just as you asked' is not a new act. - `app/prose_state.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Review repairs, people (#73, #74, #78, #79): every typed paragraph is read for out-of-character notes and quoted speech is never split; a figure already bound to someone here is not free for a new name, and a figure alias is used only where its person is; the player's name may be used in the reply on the turn they give it; party members know the name; '"Yes," she says.' is not dropped as said before; the keeper of the venue the player is in answers the next line; /ask ignores out-of-character notes; after an all-note line the last turn is put back on screen. - `app/conversation.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/ask.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Review repairs, quests (#81, n20): the offer check reads job words only in someone else's sentence, a giver's refusal anywhere in the exchange vetoes it, a bare nod is not agreement, 'I need a hand finding the inn' is not an ask for work, and a bare 'apprentice', 'deliveries' or 'show you how' is not an offer; a typed accept or decline without a quote counts only when it can mean one offer, and a decline must refuse the job ('pass on the message' does not); a typed acceptance the engine will act on is shown to the narrator as player_takes_it_now; the gate's one-turn-late ask reads the engine's last player line, not the filtered summaries. - `app/quest_parser.py`, `app/local_intel.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/llm.py`
