## [0.10.0-dev.6] - 2026-10-07

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Writer-off fixes #30-#54 and the zoomable town grid (roads, plots, plotted shops).

### Added

- [CLAUDE] Town grid, slice C (docs/TownGrid.md 8): the map zooms into the town. The Settlement view gains **City | Streets** chips; Streets draws the cell the player stands in and its city neighbours at fine resolution from `GET /api/town/view` (about 6 px a tile, the − / + chips and the mouse wheel zoom it, drag pans, every world cell the same size so a smaller side has bigger tiles): avenues and main roads as the wide thread, streets as the thin one, alleys dotted, only the roads and plots the player has seen; shops and services in the thread colour, closed ones dashed, temples and offices in the label colour, gates bright; a plot's name where it fits, a point otherwise; seen cells nobody has walked show their skeleton and unseen cells a dashed outline; the player is the map's ring and needle on their real road tile. The road mask is inflated in the browser (`DecompressionStream`, the same zlib packing `town_grid` stores). Clicking a plot selects it, previews the walk as a fading thread along the known roads, and opens a card under the map (name, kind, street, ward, hours and open now, keeper if met) with **Walk here**, which posts the engine's own click-walk (`POST /api/town/walk`, the route typed moves share) and shows the minutes, and **Go in**, which only writes "I go into <name>." because going in is a turn. Clicking a road walks to it. Right-click or long-press on a plot opens the interact.js menu: Go to (writes "I walk to <name>."), Go in, and **Walk here now** in the red moon. Arrow keys and the pad walk to the next junction on the current road, into the next cell at its edge. Leaving the cell waits for the scene like long world travel. Clicking a seen cell on City zooms to its streets; World goes back. Entering a town switches the map to Streets once per entry unless the player chose World since (a per-viewer `localStorage` flag), and the view follows the player into the next cell after a turn. Checked headless at 1440 and 390 px on an isolated scratch server (a generated world with the player in a three-cell city): click-walk moves the marker to the plot's door and advances the clock, no page errors, no sideways scroll. - `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`, `static/ui/interact.js`, `tests/test_town_ui.py`, `docs/TownGrid.md`, `CODEBASE_INDEX.md`, `README.md`
- [CLAUDE] Town grid, slice B (docs/TownGrid.md): the player now stands on a road tile inside a town and the engine, not the draft, decides where they go and which buildings exist. `town_position` (snapshotted by key) holds the fine tile and the world marker follows it; stepping into a city on the map lands at the gate on the edge crossed (or the road nearest that edge's middle, or the shared port from a neighbouring cell), stepping out clears it, and a game start or old save is placed on the shopping ward's road. Before the prompt, `plan_turn` reads the player's own words: a plot name they know, a trade ("go to the bakery", "find a smith": the nearest known plot, else the nearest generated one, else a bounded lookup of at most 2 more cells, or the whole of a city of 5 cells or fewer), a street or ward, "enter the inn" at its door, "step back out", or "out the north gate". It walks the roads (fine BFS per cell, cells crossed at their shared ports, generated only as the walk reaches them, at most 5 a turn), charges `tile_minutes` per tile, stops at 40 minutes with `partial`, and previews the energy spend. The draft gets a `movement_contract.town` block (ward, street, where the player stands, up to 8 seen `places_here` with open and hours, streets off, and `arrived`, `walking`, `blocked` or `none` from this turn's plan) instead of `venue_name_options`, `venue_kinds_possible` and the invent-a-name sentence; cast options drop venue names; the three DSL movement bullets that invited inventing a building, said going in costs the next turn and asked for WALK are swapped for town versions only while the block is present (the legacy prompt is unchanged byte for byte). In `resolve_movement` a plotted town never mints: a MOVE to an invented "Blind Owl Bakery" is re-aimed at the nearest real bakery and the prose name is replaced when that is unambiguous, a prose entry with no MOVE goes into the plot at the door, LOC_NEW buildings the grid lacks are not stored, an unshown move is still dropped (#6c), and a known place outside the grid hands back to the world rules. After the snapshot `apply_town_turn` commits the position, records `town_seen` (roads within 40 m, signs within 15 m, the street ahead), spends time, weather and energy on the turn's own connection through `_spend_travel` (split out of `apply_map_travel_step`; `player_resources.preview_travel_spend` is the pure preview), and realizes the plot as a `locations` row (child of the adopted or created settlement row, stamped kind and hours, "<name> on <street>" plus an alias on a clash) only when the player goes in and it is open. Plot rows are entered positionally from their door (`venue_entry_check`), `venues.row_kind` reads their stamped kind, workplaces in a plotted city claim a free plot of the trade in a generated cell (the plan carries the plot's real name, no row until visited) or stay nameless ("works at a bakery") until one is generated, and a direction answer records the stall or notice as told. The story walk skips in town (`plan_story_walk(town=True)`), leaving through a gate puts the marker outside it, rewind and load put the marker back on `town_position`, and `town_seen` rows of a rewound turn are now deleted by max id (a slice A gap). `POST /api/town/walk` walks to a plot's door or a clicked tile without going in (409 when confined, too tired, or leaving the cell while travel is locked). Not done: the live 8B check, because the GPU was in use; the UI is slice C. - `app/town_moves.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/tile_world.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/local_intel.py`, `app/player_resources.py`, `app/venues.py`, `app/example_pools.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_town_movement.py`, `docs/TownGrid.md`, `CODEBASE_INDEX.md`, `README.md`
- [CLAUDE] Town grid, slice A (docs/TownGrid.md): every city world cell can now be generated into a road network, plots and plotted shops, lazily, one cell at a time, and read back through two read-only routes. Not yet walked or drawn (slices B and C). `app/town_grid.py` keeps the legacy avenues exactly, connects each cell to its neighbours and the world's roads through ports computed from the city record alone (`city_ports`), lays main roads, side streets on a ward-sized grid, alleys in dense deep blocks, and asserts every road tile and port is connected. Blocks are cut into frontage plots by ward geometry, landmarks stand at temple, government and military anchors, gates sit at the gate ports, and the rest are yards. The stalls `local_intel` already told players about become shops in their own ward (none unplaced in the probe); far notices land in the cell they point at; every other frontage plot rolls by ward and density, era- and size-fitted from a kind weight table that has a row for every `VENUE_KINDS` key. Names come from `example_pools` draws: a new `street_name` kind and a `venue_kind` / `street_name` context for `venue_name`, so a bakery's name always reads as a bakery ("Wheel Street Bakery") and only inns, taverns and bars wear sign names. Each name belongs to one cell of its city by hash, so names are unique per city and a cell comes out byte-identical whichever cell was generated first. Rows live in the new `town_cells` table (never regenerated once stored), knowledge in the append-only `town_seen` (in the rewind max-id set); both are ordinary save tables filtered to the campaign's maps, pruned with their map, and cleared by a new playthrough or an old save; `locations.plot_id`, `locations.city_id` and `npcs.workplace_plot` are migrated in for slice B. `GET /api/town/view` and `GET /api/town/plot/{id}` show only seen roads and plots and never generate (a seen ungenerated cell shows its in-memory skeleton); the settlement route gains a `town` block. Measured by `tools/probe_town_grid.py`: a 128 cell about 0.2 s warm (design target 0.15 s, test limit 1 s), 38 KiB stored, about 1,900 plots; a 25-cell city 3.1 s for every cell (median 0.13 s); a 47-cell 9 x 9 box 8.5 s in memory, per cell median 0.17 s; market wards 0.52 shops per frontage plot after caps, residential 0.04. Two build deviations from the design are written up in its slice A notes (kind caps scale with the ward's frontage plots, not the cell side; side streets cross ward boundaries). `venues.venue_kind_from_name` skips words that are not in the text before running the regex (same results, faster). - `app/town_grid.py`, `app/example_pools.py`, `app/venues.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/tile_world.py`, `app/main.py`, `tools/probe_town_grid.py`, `tests/test_town_grid.py`, `docs/TownGrid.md`, `CODEBASE_INDEX.md`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Playtest #43 / #45 / #48 / #53 under the writer-off default (live Qwen3 8B traces): the verifier, backstory mode, trace names and setup seeds - `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/starter_logic.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/world_facts.py`, `app/proficiencies.py`, `app/mle.py`, `app/rng.py`. With AI_RPG_DSL_SKIP_VERIFY=1 every live turn still went to verify: the skip waited on the verifier's summed certainty, which subtracts for NPC/event records, short narration and talk intent and never reached 0.6. The skip now reads what the draft changes (items, equipment, quests, skills, abilities, gold/xp/level/health/karma, MOVE/WALK, unresolved refs) and length is no longer scored before the depth retry. Every turn was classed as a conversation because the engine's "Conversation (engine decided)" footer was classified with the player's line; intent and focus terms now come from the line, plus the conversation engine's own decision for plain lines. The verify prompts stop policing length and wording, and the DSL parser's skipped malformed-op notes stay in `_dsl` and the trace instead of `self_check.issues_found`, where the verifier answered them by rewriting prose for 27-36 s a turn. An explicit canonical backstory mode ("nameless drifter") is no longer overwritten by the gear/vibe harmonizer, whose arrival phrases now match whole words ("awoke in" is not "woke in"). Trace files and narration ledgers carry the campaign id, so a second game no longer overwrites the first. Setup is reproducible: the world-facts asks and proficiency name shapes draw from the campaign seed, model calls take a seed (in-process and llama.cpp), setup phases run in seeded blocks with one seed per call, and a seeded in-process call evaluates its whole prompt. Two live setups of one form and seed now match exactly. Tests: `tests/test_playtest_batch5_fixer_43_53.py` (fixture `tests/fixtures/playtest_batch5_fixer_43_53.json`, verbatim live traces); `tests/test_aria_map_findings.py` and `tests/test_dsl_op_isolation.py` moved to the new skip rule and op-note home.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #50 / #33 / #34 / #35 / #51 under the writer-off default: one shop per entry (the shop the turn names - MOVE, LOC_NEW or its sign - is the venue, with its own trade, instead of a second invented "<Place> General Store" or "<Name> Shop"; a parent the line walks to holds the shop); a shop-entry line no longer makes the companion the only one who answers, so the draft stops casting them as shopkeeper; an NPC_NEW the prose never shows is bound to the one figure shown or dropped, and the DSL asks for NPC_NEW only for someone ===NAR=== shows; split fact titles end on a noun phrase ("Frontier dark fantasy", "Small settlements"); companions who come along join party_members, so the Party tab, party bonus and morale see them. tests/test_playtest_batch5_writer_off_33_51.py.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #39/#40/#41/#46/#49 (batch 4 live gate and the writer A/B, Qwen3 8B): slot words, doubled item names, unbought spends, a stale movement report and Chinese tokens - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/rng.py`, `app/mle.py`, `app/script_guard.py`. The DSL op list was a template of value-shaped snake_case slots, and the 8B wrote them back as values (GOAL choose_direction, TALK "topic_of_the_exchange", which then re-entered later prompts); it is now a legend that says in words what follows each op, with no snake_case tokens and no fixed "GOLD -moderate" example, and a GOAL, TALK, FOCUS or SUMMARY that is a single snake_case token is dropped as malformed, on parse and on write, and kept out of get_state's conversations. The known-name tagger tagged "charred ledger" inside "charred ledger satchel [[I5]]" and the label expander then doubled the satchel's name; tagged spans are held out while shorter names are tagged, and a name with an inner tag counts as present. A GOLD loss outlived the GRANTs it paid for when the verifier patch emptied inventory_changes (gold 12 to 1, nothing bought); it is voided with a band-report line, while a toll or fine with no grant still costs, and a rolled loss reads "= 5, lost (-5)" instead of "+3 = -5". The movement report said "model" with a destination while the venue gate kept the player in place; a refused, closed or unreachable venue now sets status "refused" with the reason and journals venue_refused. CAST and the scene merge unwrap a "[[B]]" to its code and drop codes no NPC holds. Han, Kana and Hangul vocabulary pieces are masked at sample time in the in-process provider (scanned once per model, `AI_RPG_SCRIPT_GUARD=0` turns it off), and every provider's reply is repaired after generation ("warm空气" becomes "warm air"; an unknown run is dropped) with the hit traced as script_repair - `tests/test_playtest_batch4_fixer_39_49.py`

## [0.10.0-dev.5] - 2026-10-06

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Live-model fixes #28-#53 and the paragraph-writer A/B; writer off by default.

### Added

- [CLAUDE] A staged live playtest as a Claude Code workflow (`.claude/workflows/staged-playtest.js`): ten stages from settings to save/reload on the local 8B in a temp data dir, each passed system kept on for the next. A stage counts as passed only with evidence and no listed failures, decided by the script; the fixer reports status, reason and files, and only a real fix earns a retest. README updated for 0.10 (new systems, writer-off default, measured turn times) and the docs index lists PLAYTEST_ISSUES.md. - `.claude/workflows/staged-playtest.js`, `README.md`, `docs/README.md`

### Changed

- [CLAUDE] The paragraph writer (narration pipeline) is now off by default; the draft's own prose ships. A blind live A/B on Qwen3 8B (12 turns per arm, same scripted moves) scored writer-off at 3 restated sentences against 18, 17.9 s per turn against 32.5 s, readability 4 against 3, and every addressed NPC answering; writer-on still invents fewer player actions (7 against 16), which batch 5 fixes in the draft ask. It stays a launcher toggle. - `app/launcher_prefs.py`, `Morkyn.ps1`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Playtest #30/#42/#52 under the writer-off default (live Qwen3 8B A/B): the draft's own prose stops acting, thinking and taking for the player, and the depth floor is a setting - `app/llm.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/world.py`. The draft ask drew the player boundary around speech, gestures and feelings only, told the model to "narrate the journey", and demanded 1000+ characters for a one-line action; the 8B filled them with "You nod, your mind already racing", "you wonder if", "And you have no choice but to follow" and "You decide to buy". The asks now give the player their further actions, thoughts, conclusions and decisions, and the length ask follows the depth floor and shrinks with the action. A narrow structural pass after the speech drop removes unquoted "you" clauses that think, decide, gesture, speak or take beyond player_line, keeps perception and NPC actions, and records each drop. Grants: a pickup, pocketing, purchase or find the prose writes for the player needs the player's own taking words (a hand-over or reward still stands on the prose), "find" is no longer take intent, and "You set the vial down" is a loss. Depth: `AI_RPG_MIN_NARRATION_CHARS` (default 600, 300-1500) replaces the hard-coded 1000 when the writer is off; the menu trims keep their own 400 floor; the retry asks for modest growth with only player_line, and an expansion that adds a new person, name or line of speech is rejected. "You have two choices: X, or Y." and its summary line are trimmed. Live rerun: depth retries 3/12 turns (was 6/12).
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #35 (live Qwen3 8B smoke run): the player's gear stays on the player, the starter report matches the inventory, and ranks and fact titles hold - `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/starter_logic.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/setup_coherence.py`, `app/proficiencies.py`, `app/world_facts.py`. The paragraph writer got the whole inventory as "item the player carries" on every beat, and the 8B gave the player's work gloves to the nurse and the boots to the courier; now an item is offered only when the draft slice, the player's words or the ops name it, as "worn by the player (you)" or "carried by the player (you)", and the writer is told those belong to the player and no one else. Start kept the required worn cards the fact-check stripped while the popup said "Removed", so the stripped list, player messages and gm_brief's "only these" list are rebuilt from what was seeded; a card in a body slot counts as clothing, and "work top", "chinos", "beanie" and the like are clothes (a pickaxe is a tool, not an axe). "hidden" is an amnesia start only when the memory policy or story says the memory is gone, and an amnesia start keeps what the backstory gives ("her only possession a cracked compass", "her old tools"), one per thing named. The arrival detector reads a described place ("awoke in the ruins of the sky-temple of Vael'Kara") and puts the start location there. "I pocket the crystal" no longer mints the prose's "glowing shard": a claim whose head noun is held, or a renamed claim when the player's words name a held item, grants nothing. An ordinary start caps proficiencies at the lowest quarter of the scale (D on F..SSS, was B), and a rank the setup already gave a proficiency is its ceiling in the post-start pass. Split fact titles never end on a joiner or stand as one word ("Survivors harness forbidden magic", "Power earned through calculated risks", "DM stance"), and the post-start pass retitles split rows - `tests/test_playtest_live_starter_35.py`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #33 (live Qwen3 8B smoke run): shops become venues, questions stay put, and the prose walks the way the map does - `app/venues.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/tile_world.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`. The venue kinds were medieval only, so "Chrome Wrench Garage", "Ito Salvage" and "Bianchi's Clinic" (names the engine's own pools drew) classified as nothing and every venue rule skipped them; garage, salvage yard, clinic, pharmacy, diner, bar and laundromat now exist with hours and sizes, every trade word the venue-name pools draw has a kind, venue_kinds_possible lists the world's era, and a role maps to its era's premises (a modern bartender works a bar). An explicit MOVE was trusted before any venue rule ran: on a turn that goes inside it now stamps a kindless building as a venue of the settlement where its tradesperson stands (write-once), or opens the shop inside the settlement the MOVE named, and the gate lets that turn walk there and go in when the prose shows both. "I slip back out and go into the nearest salvage shop" read as an exit; the last doorway motion now wins and an exit from a place that is not a venue does not stop the going-in. Entry in prose read only the first preposition, so "through the side entrance and into Ito Salvage" and "through the creaking door ... Inside, the garage" were missed; every preposition is read, a door is the way in, and the prose's own name for the shop is used. The opening's mechanic now works at the garage (a lone kindless building of his trade is adopted by his settlement, and on entry the longest-standing person of the venue's trade is bound before anyone else). A question about someone else's movements ("Where were you headed?", "Who runs the inn?") is no longer travel, so the unasked-move gate drops a MOVE to a place named only in the answer; speech (including a quote split across paragraphs) and a perfect tense ("everything you've left behind") no longer count as the narration moving the player, a named MOVE the prose never names carries prose_mismatch, and a dropped move still lets the med-bay the prose entered become a venue. Known places in the movement contract carry the engine's bearing and distance, and a travel turn is told the way the walk goes (the player's direction, the bearing of the place they name, or the map's own fallback heading), which the map walk then takes - `tests/test_playtest_live_venues_33.py`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #31/#32/#34 (live Qwen3 8B smoke run): who is talked to, who comes along, and who exists - `app/conversation.py`, `app/scene_thread.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/world.py`. A known NPC the player names while she is elsewhere is "called" (the writer is told who) and is moved here when the prose shows her, so she becomes the target instead of last_speaker. Companions asked along (now also "stay close", "with X beside me") are kept on the scene thread even without a pursuit, and they and the person followed move with the player; looking for footprints is a side action, not a new pursuit. A tech level that names an era ("wasteland iron age") wins over setting words, so a frontier dark fantasy no longer gets pharmacists and bike couriers. NPC_NEW keeps two-word names whole and a bare first name binds to the known person. Prose seeding no longer reads "she says" or "perhaps answers" as people, seeds after the move, recalls an already-seeded face, and stores what the prose said about them instead of a placeholder.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #29 (live Qwen3 8B smoke run): paragraphs no longer restart on the previous paragraph's last line or end inside a quotation - `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`. The beat writer got about 400 characters of a 480-character paragraph twice (its own payload and the brief) under "Continue from previous_paragraph_tail", and in 6 of about 22 live beats it opened by restating the tail. It now gets only the last sentence, as `last_line_already_written`, and is told to open on something new from draft_slice; the brief's copy is gone. A paragraph whose opening sentences exactly match the previous paragraph's last two is trimmed at any length, at the boundary only (the 45-character short-line exemption still holds everywhere else), both at beat commit and on the finished turn. The char-cap cut now lands only where quotes balance and treats a curly closer as a sentence end, and an unclosed quote counts as truncated, so the beat takes the existing rewrite path.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #30: the narration stops speaking for the player - `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`. PROSE_VOICE, the DSL draft prompt and the beat writer now say that the player's input is all that "you" say and do this turn and that a line spoken to the player ends the exchange; the draft request and every beat brief carry the input as `player_line`; the writer's "keep its speakers and facts" rule is narrowed to the other characters and the world; and the draft's length asks for 1000-1200 characters on a short action instead of a fixed 1000-1800 (the floor stays at the 1000-character depth minimum). A structural check drops whole quotation units the narration gives the player ("you say", a continuation of it, or a dialogue paragraph that opens on the player's action) when the words are not in what the player typed, from the draft before the beat writer sees it and from the finished turn on every path, and records the drop in self_check. Gestures are left to the ask.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest #28 (live Qwen3 8B smoke run): opening NPCs stand beside the player again, not at a place called "The" - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/scene_thread.py`. The draft wrote `LOC The Frostbound Crossroads` unquoted and the parser kept only "The", which became a location row; every opening NPC went there, the code repair tagged every "the" in the prose as [[L4]], and an examine turn ("the frost trail on the underbrush") was read as a pursuit that moved the player to "The". An unquoted LOC now runs to the next flag like ROLE (a location code still stands alone). The op list no longer shows `<slot>` placeholders, which round 1 copied as literal text (`LOC <The Wasteland's Edge>`, `GOAL <...>`, `TALK <Eli Dasgupta>`): slots are lower_case words and LOC asks for a code or a quoted name; a `<...>` span the model still writes is read as one quoted argument. A place "named" only by articles or prepositions, or holding a stray bracket, is never minted (the NPC stays beside the player), never linked by the code tagger (old saves that hold one), and never matched by the follow repair. "trail", "track", "shadow" and "tail" count as pursuit verbs only in a verb position of the player's own.

Auto sized the window against 92% of the whole card with a fixed 900 MiB overhead, ignoring the 1.1 GB the desktop already held, and opened Qwen3 8B at 38,912 tokens; the first call never left llama_decode. The window is now sized against free memory (plus what our own loaded model holds), compute buffers grow with the window, and auto stops at 24,576 (a turn needs about 16k). On the same card it now picks 24,576; env and custom values still win.

The pass now shows the stored rows as already known and asks for 3-5 kinds of new lore drawn fresh per call from a `world_fact_ask` pool (named factions, customs, taboos, place lore, history, laws), and for each race's traits and gifts beyond magic, with setup-decided magic access kept by the engine. Without a model every race gets engine-rolled traits; titles are the fact's subject noun phrase ("Kingdom of Eldoria", "Power through combat prowess") and tags are nouns; faction pressure is one row that named model factions replace; codes the 8B wrote into a fact's text become links; a "custom"-only race list resolves to a real one; `player.race` is stored at start (setup `player_race`, a "reborn as a <people>" backstory, else human) and reaches the packet and `GET /api/world-facts`. On Qwen3 8B with game 2's options the pass now stores 6 enriched races and 3-5 new facts per run (was 0).
- [CLAUDE] Custom proficiencies are skill nouns at this start's ranks, and a rule's title is its own (playtest #22) - `app/proficiencies.py`, `app/example_pools.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`. Game 2 still rolled "master the dance of shadows (E), learn from the ancients (C), forge unbreakable bonds (D)", stored them at C/C/D (`adjusted: []`) for an idea that says "start ordinary with one weak compounding seed power", and titled rule F7 "Crafting Mastery" over a rule about Unbreakable Bonds. The custom_skills ask (the single-field roll and the powers-phase group roll) now carries three to five proficiency names drawn fresh per roll from a world-filtered pool (`draw_proficiency_names`: craft, lore, field, people and magic shapes, magic only where the world has it, one matching the backstory's trade when it names one, used names excluded), with its own examples rule. A verb-led name is found structurally (`leading_verb`: a short first-word lexicon, `-ize`/`-ify`, a verb before a determiner; "Dance of Shadows" and "Trade Lore" stay names); "master/learn/study ... X" becomes X (`noun_form`), the rest get one re-ask together (`rename_with_model`) and are dropped if still nameless, both in the setup roll (`settle_setup_text`) and in the post-start pass. The cap was empty because the session theme that reached Start said "ordinary"/"steady": Start now floors the stored power fantasy with the idea's own words (`setup_composer.floor_power_fantasy`), the pass reads the idea from the start form too, and a weak, seed or compounding start keeps every proficiency at the bottom rank, the seed one step up only when the idea names it. A rule keeps the model's title only when its words are in the rule's text; otherwise the engine titles it from the text ("Unbreakable Bonds Limit"). Live rolls on Qwen3 8B for this fix found three cleanups that broke the text: the comma cleanup split inside parentheses and deduped "hard limit S)" out of every phrase after the first (and dropped a repeated bare "F"), the seed alignment ate "(F," after the seed's name, and only the label opening a parenthesis is now taken as the start rank ("capped at S" is a limit, not a rank to lower). Tests: `tests/test_playtest_proficiency_names.py`.

## [0.10.0-dev.4] - 2026-10-05

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Playtest fixes #1-#27: conversation targeting, scene thread, venues, world facts, proficiencies, example pools.

### Added

- [CLAUDE] The map can show a settlement's own grid (playtest #19). A World / Settlement switch above the map lens draws the settlement you stand in, or one you pick from those you know, from `GET /api/tiles/map/settlement`: each world tile of the city as a square, its wards as hairlines where the owner changes, streets as faint light, gated wards in the moon colour, your position, and the places you have been on their tile with the venues inside them and whether each is open now. What is drawn follows the world map's knowledge rules: a settlement is listed once a tile of it was seen or you were told of it, and a tile you have not seen is outline only, with no wards sent. The view is read-only and never marks anything visited. `cell_raster()` samples a tile's ward flood without building one dict per fine tile, so a 55-tile metropolis answers in about 0.15 s - `app/tile_world.py`, `app/world_scale.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issue #1: Custom Proficiencies are asked for and kept in the shape the field promises - `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/proficiencies.py`. A random fill came back as twelve slogans ("weave light, master the dance of shadows, earn trust through deeds, ... braid destiny") because every ask only said "skill discovery, training limits, progression rules, or named proficiencies" and the field rolled before the backstory and powers it should come from. Every ask that writes `custom_skills` (single-field roll, group contract, optimize note, coherence review, quality retry) now sends one shared `CUSTOM_SKILLS_SHAPE`: named proficiencies with start rank on `rank_scale`, tracking and hard limit, or checkable training rules, built from the character and the world, with no sample names; the domain ban list left the field note. `custom_skills` moved to the `powers` phase, after the backstory and ability cards, and its single-field roll gets a 90s MLE timeout (measured 39-43s on Qwen3 8B against the 45s default). After start, a second post-start pass asks the model for closed `SKILL|name|rank|tracking|limit` and `RULE|title|text` lines; the engine refuses names that read as sentences (articles, pronouns, prepositions), missing tracking or limit, duplicates, fragment-list rules and rules that restate a proficiency or a setting; a rank off the scale starts at the lowest rank and one above the start's cap is lowered to it; a doubled-name line and a bare rank as the limit (both seen live) are read as what they are. It stores skills in the new `player_proficiencies` table and `player_skills`, rules as `world_facts`, and rewrites `custom_skills` as a summary of the rows (setup text kept as `custom_skills_setup`).
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issue #8 (first slice): world setup strings become small engine rows, fetched by relevance each turn - `app/world_facts.py`. The logged game set six peoples and "Only elves and Spirit touched/spirits have magic.", but the turn packet carried `world_races` as a bare label and dropped `custom_style` and `race_magic_rules` entirely, so nothing told the writer that Aria, a human, has no magic. Two new per-world tables: `world_races` (code R1.., name, short traits, `magic_access` from a closed enum, short ability rules, lifespan in years, size band) and `world_facts` (code F1.., kind from a closed enum, title, text of at most 200 characters, links to other codes, tags). `world.start_playthrough` fills them with a deterministic split: one row per listed people, magic access read from the rule sentences ("only" closes magic to the peoples left out, negations give none, learn/train words give learned), each rule and `custom_style` sentence a linked fact, `faction_pressure` labels as faction rows; familiar peoples get engine numbers and others roll theirs from the campaign seed. A post-start, pre-turn-1 phase (`run_post_start_passes()`, called before the opening) runs registered passes; the first asks the model once, in a closed `RACE|...` / `FACT|...` line format with no example names, to adjust race rows and add facts, and the engine refuses bad enums, over-long text, unknown races, unresolved links, race links the fact does not name, and restatements of stored facts. It is skippable (`AI_RPG_POST_START_MODEL=off`, or no model file) and the split alone plays. Each turn, `build_prompt_context` adds `world_facts`: the races of the people present (with who is of that people), races the player names, facts linked to those or sharing the turn's words, and one tone fact, under a 900-character budget; the handoff keeps the key, the draft and verify packets carry it as `world_state.world_facts`, and it is dropped early under token pressure. Worlds started before this get their split on first read; saves carry both tables. Read-only `GET /api/world-facts` shows the rows and the last post-start pass report. Probed on Qwen3 8B in isolation: asked for codes it invented one-letter ones (now asked for names, codes still accepted), and it restated setup sentences as new facts (now refused); the first call after start can load the model cold, so its timeout is 180s.
- [CLAUDE] A map the player receives reveals ground instead of taking a pack slot. A grounded gain of a map, chart or atlas (not a map case) is copied onto the active world map by `reveal_chart_area()`: terrain within its reach joins the revealed tiles and towns and landmarks there become known markers; reach comes from the item's own words (small/local 10 tiles, plain 16, detailed/regional/large 24). The journal records what it added, and the opening's no-free-kit rule does not refuse a map. - `app/world.py`, `app/tile_world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Scene history in the play view. A History button in the Scene header shows earlier turns above the current one, in the same scroll column: each turn is a collapsed card (turn number and what you did) that opens to your words and the narration. Ten at a time; a new turn or opening History scrolls the current turn to the top, so earlier turns are reached by scrolling up, and "View more" at the top pages further back, from `GET /api/turn-history?before=&limit=` once the page's 160-row journal window runs out. The Scene header's Map button now appears only when the map is not where it normally sits (side panel hidden, map floated or off stage, or a custom layout), and a Side button hides or shows the right-hand panel; both choices are remembered. - `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`, `app/main.py`
- [CLAUDE] Quest parser: the narrator offers work in the story, a small parser pass instates it, and the engine decides. Before this the turn model had no way to create a quest (no op, no turn key, nothing in `apply_turn`); the live database held 0 quests while traced narrations handed out jobs ("You've been tasked with escorting a shipment of silk from here to the western border"). Now the narrator may mark work with the DSL ops `QUEST "<title>" GIVER .. STEP .. [AT ..] [REWARD ..]` and `QUEST_DONE <code> <accept|step_done|complete|fail|abandon>` (aliases OFFER, JOB, TASK), but marking is optional. After the narration is final, `quest_parser.needs_quest_parse()` (regex only, so quiet turns cost nothing) decides whether to run one focused parser call (`quest_parser` phase, 450 tokens, `AI_RPG_QUEST_PARSER=0` turns the call off, `AI_RPG_QUEST_PARSER_TIMEOUT`). Its proposals and the narrator's marks merge into `turn["quest_changes"]`; `validate_quest_changes()` accepts a new quest only when its evidence is quoted from this turn's text and reads as an offer, request or acceptance (not "no one is hiring"), the giver resolves to a known NPC or a board, it is not a duplicate of an open quest, and rewards are within bounds; updates need a legal status change. `apply_quest_changes()` creates the rows through `create_quest`, accepts, advances, completes (paying the reward) or fails them, journals each one, and attaches `quest_report` (created, updated, rejected with reasons) to the turn, its trace and the state. The narrator now sees active quests every turn (the handoff used to drop them), "Do not invent extra jobs" is replaced by guidance to offer work that fits the scene, unknown DSL ops are traced as `unknown_ops` instead of vanishing, `GET /api/quests` adds `offered`, and the Quests tab shows offered jobs above active ones. - `app/quest_parser.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/quests.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Starting gear is a list of items, each with its own card, and it reaches SQLite whole. The old Worn gear box dumped every item into one "Item" field with the slot empty, "Effect type" on Look only and "What it does" blank, so no starting item ever gave a stat. `app/gear.py` now owns the item shape (name, slot, description, the six stat bonuses, the item's own stats: weight, durability, protection, value, rarity, and a list of abilities in the same shape as special abilities); the setup form posts `starter_gear` and the comma string `starter_equipment` is derived from it (names only) for the art prompt and the arrival fact-check. Three required cards (feet, torso, legs) exist from the moment the settings window opens; they cannot be removed, their slot is fixed, and Randomize rewrites their name and description for the world unless the card is locked. `starter_gear` is a composer field that depends on `world_style`, `tech_level`, `player_sex`, `world_races`, `backstory_mode`, `character_backstory`, `appearance` and `start_location`, so it rolls after them and the prompt ships their values; the model is asked for local materials and names and a mundane kit, with stats and abilities optional. Whatever the model leaves blank the engine rolls from the world (`roll_item_stats`: weight by slot and material, durability from condition words and economy, protection by garment, value by economy and rarity; `roll_stat_bonuses`: usually nothing, sometimes one +1 that fits the slot) and whatever it over-fills the engine clamps (per-item bonus -2..+3, kit total by start power: 1 for a weak start, 2 normal, 4 strong). A Roll stats button on each card calls the engine directly (`POST /api/setup/gear-stats`), and `POST /api/setup/gear-defaults` names the three basics for a world. Each gear ability card is the same UI as a special ability, minus Randomize (gear abilities are not tied to the randomizer yet). `start_playthrough` writes one inventory row per item with `equipped_slot`, `stat_modifiers`, `stat_links`, `granted_abilities` (carrying `growth_math` and `power_type`) and the new `item_stats` column, so worn gear feeds `player.effective_stats` from turn one. A LEGS body slot joins the default equipment slots. - `app/gear.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `static/app.js`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] The character set up for a game agrees with itself (playtest #21) - `app/setup_coherence.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/starter_logic.py`, `static/app.js`. Game 2 was Miriam with a "she" backstory stored as male, arrived at "The Empty Lot" while play began at Eldoria's Edge, was described in a leather jerkin and sandals while wearing boots, tunic and trousers, and lost the backstory's small enchanted lantern. The backstory ask no longer says "use they/their": single-field, group, draft/optimize and quality-retry prompts carry the pronouns of the chosen sex (the name's sex in the pools when the sex is unset), and the retry prompt's fixed job and transport lists (it named "stagehand" and "ferry", both in game 2's backstory) are gone. A rolled name the pools know as the other sex is replaced by a fresh draw for the chosen sex. Start first calls the new `POST /api/setup/identity-check`: a chosen sex that disagrees with the backstory's pronouns or the name is shown in the setup status line and Start stops once (pressing it again keeps both); an unset sex is filled from the pronouns, then the name, and `start_playthrough` infers it the same way. The setup coherence pass was the one step that renamed `start_location` after the backstory was written and patched the place alone; the backstory's arrival now follows it (a locked backstory keeps its place and the patch is dropped), a rolled backstory is aligned too, and Start aligns any named arrival with `start_location`. At Start the worn cards are the truth: an unnamed basic (no name, a legacy placeholder, or a card the page filled with engine defaults, now sent as `engine_default`) takes the clothes appearance names for its zone, then appearance's clothing tags are rewritten from the worn cards. Items the backstory says the character carries become carried cards with no bonuses, ahead of the extras, and go through the starter fact-check; a demoted card ("small enchanted lantern" to "small lantern") used to be dropped because the kept-items filter matched the old name, and "jerkin" counted as a pocket trinket. The player row now gets the fact-check's backstory, mode and memory too. What Start changed is in `settings.setup_coherence` - `tests/test_playtest_setup_coherence.py`.
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issues #26 and #14 (prompt side): examples come from large pools, a few fresh per call, never a fixed list - `app/example_pools.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/world.py`. Two games opened with "Aria the baker", two characters were "Miriam Shaw", and a setup face came back "amber eyes ... faint scar on right cheek" from a contract example. `FIELD_CONTRACTS` no longer carries `examples`; the single-field roll and the lint repair draw 3-5 examples per call from pools filtered by era, magic, naming culture, climate, water, settlement size, sex and age band (names by culture, NPC jobs widened from `_SEED_ROLE_POOLS`, venue name forms, clothing by slot, kits, economy/quest/faction phrases, race rules rendered against the world's own peoples). Hair colour and length, eye colour, a notable mark and the clothes' colour are rolled by the engine (`roll_setup_values`), sent as `engine_rolled` on the single-field and group paths, and put back by `apply_rolled_values` if the prose drops them. The player-name roll gets drawn `name_options` and `avoid_names` (recent games' player names, kept in the new non-campaign table `player_name_history`); the offline fallback draws too. Every draft packet carries `cast_options` (names unused in this world, jobs for this place, venue names), the NPC_NEW rules point at it, and the venue rule gets `venue_name_options`; `_upsert_npc` and `create_shell_npc` record each created name in `name_ledger`. Measured offline, 300 single draws per kind in five worlds: 260-298 distinct names, 36-104 jobs, 227-251 venue names, top entry at most 3% for those kinds. The old initials-hint test was rewritten to the new direction.
- [CLAUDE] Review of the playtest #16 venue fix: only the narration taking the player indoors makes a venue, and only in the place they are in - `app/venues.py`, `app/world.py`. The entry reader took a shopkeeper's spoken "Why don't you step into my shop?", "you step in front of the bakery", "head in the direction of the inn" and "lets you in on a secret about the tavern" as the player going inside, which mints a venue and moves the player into it; speech is now skipped and those phrasings are not entries. The shown-entry rule also ran before the travel rules, so "You reach Riverford at dusk. You step into the inn" opened the inn inside the place the player had left; a turn that names or mints another place now travels there first. The venue rule's own sample names ("<place> Apothecary", "The Salt Crow") are gone from the prompt - `tests/test_playtest_review_places.py`.
- [CLAUDE] Review of the playtest #14 and #16 name fixes: the stored-role rewrite no longer eats ordinary prose, and one name per person covers the opening that logged it - `app/llm.py`. The rewrite that puts a person's stored job in front of their tag matched any article and word pair, so "You hand the loaf to Aria [[A]]" became "You hand the baker Aria [[A]]" and "a smiling Aria [[A]]" became "a baker Aria [[A]]". It now acts only on job-shaped words (a trade suffix or a known trade, no preposition), so game 2's "a cartter [[C]]" is still corrected. The "a <job> named <known name>" check only knew people who already had a code, and in game 2's opening Aria was filed that same turn, so the final prose kept "A net mender named Aria"; this turn's new people now count by name. A plain noun ("a dog named Aria") keeps the name. `name_reuses` and `role_mismatches` were also dropped by the handoff cleanup before the trace was written; they now reach the turn trace. Checked with an offline replay of game 2's turns 1-4 through the full pipeline (model replies from the traces) - `tests/test_playtest_review_names.py`.
- [CLAUDE] The in-game time is shown where you play (playtest #18). Game 2 had world_day 1 and world_minute 507, but the only clock was the "This place" card below the map, so the player saw none. The Scene header now reads "Day 1 · 08:27 · morning" and updates on load and after every turn, wait and walk through the existing `world_time` paths. The word comes from the engine: `part_of_day()` adds `part_of_day` to every `world_time`, so the narrator's packet carries it too - `app/world.py`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`
- [CLAUDE] A new game starts in named, rolled gear, not "boots, tunic, trousers" (playtest #17). The start form now keeps the gear cards (`gear`). The page no longer invents names: an unnamed basic stays blank with a hint and the engine names it at Start. `gear.is_placeholder_basic()` treats an unlocked required card named only "boots", "tunic" or "trousers" with no description or abilities as unnamed, so `normalize_gear_list()` names the slot for the world (old saves and legacy strings included); the page's `ensureRequiredGearCards()` does the same so the cards show the engine's names before Start. A locked card or one with a description keeps its name - `app/gear.py`, `app/world.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] An opening gift reaches the inventory (playtest #13: "She hands you a crusty loaf" and the journal read "Rejected unearned gain: loaf of bread x1 (named=True, prose_says_arrived=True ...)"). The opening refused every item the player did not already own; it now refuses only kit. A new item in the opening counts like any other turn's grounded gain when the final prose hands it over, and `_is_opening_kit()` still refuses weapons and armour (by type or name), rare-and-above rarities, anything enchanted, with stat modifiers or granted abilities, and dimensional storage. Maps were already exempt - `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The prompts name nobody and give no sample jobs (playtest #14: two games in a row opened on "Aria the baker" beside a net mender and a carter). This reverses the earlier decision in this changelog to keep the NPC-name examples after measuring 12 hits in 320 NPCs: the examples were copied in both playtest games. Removed: `NPC_NEW NAME` ("Aria", "Thornrow", "Captain Vesk" and the counter-examples "Woman", "Old Man", "Hooded Figure", "Guard"), `NPC_NEW ROLE` (carter, net mender, baker, off-duty guard, ferryman, "hooded stranger", "cloaked local") and the `GRANT "rope"` band example from the DSL rules; "a carter, a net mender, an off-duty guard, someone's apprentice", "a baker who heard something, a ferryman who will not cross tonight" and "plate armor for a baker or mage robes for a clerk" from the narration prompt; "Low Gate Timber Arch [[L1]]", "Sarah [[A]]" and "cracked phone [[I1]]" from the opening block; "Ash, River, Patch, the Red, Ashwalker" and "Elena Croft, Tomas Reed, or Mira" from the name-retry rules of the setup randomizer. Each rule now states the form instead: a real given name made for this world and this person and not one already used in world_state, a job word or two that fits the place, the item's name in quotes then QTY and a band. One name on two people is caught after narration: in the name repair, "a <job> named <Name>" where Name is a known person whose stored role is a different job loses the name ("A net mender named Aria weaves" becomes "A net mender weaves") and is listed in the turn's `name_reuses`; a plain description ("a woman named Aria") or the same job keeps it - `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] "Aria's [[A]] story" no longer becomes "Aria [[A]]'s [[A]] story" (playtest #15). The known-name pass tagged a name followed by "'s" even when the writer had already put the code after the possessive. `_inject_entity_codes_for_known_names()` now reads "Name's [[CODE]]" as the name present and writes it once as "Name [[CODE]]'s" (straight or curly apostrophe) before tagging untagged names - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Tests for #13-#15 replayed from the game 2 trace texts - `tests/test_playtest_opening_names.py`
- [CLAUDE] Shops are places and people have workplaces (playtest #16: Aria the baker sold the herbs in Elara's shop). Prose that takes the player through a door into a shop, inn, forge, temple or other building with no MOVE now makes that building a venue inside the current place (reused if it is already there) and moves the player in (rule `venue_shown`); whoever the prose shows letting them in or keeping the counter is bound as keeper and stands inside, and whoever came in with them (or is with them on the scene thread) moves in too. A kind the settlement is too small for is kept as a general store rather than dropped, since the player was shown inside. The draft is asked for the MOVE on every turn outside a venue, the #6c unshown-move drop spares a move whose entry the prose shows, the paragraph writer keeps a draft's entry (`scene_facts.player_goes_inside`) instead of being told the player stays in the street, and a plain "shop" or "store" in a MOVE name is a venue word. NPCs with a trade get a workplace: a baker gets a bakery kept by them as a child of their settlement, created the first time they stand where the player is (the size rule does not apply: the baker already exists), and a venue's keeper works there; stored in `npcs.workplace_id`. The draft's `current_location` carries `keeper`, `venues_here` and per person `works_here` or `works_at`, the writer's `scene_facts` carry `keeper` and the same per cast member, and both are told that someone whose workplace is elsewhere is a visitor who does not sell, keep or own what is here. An unquoted multi-word `ROLE` (`ROLE message runner LOC L1`) keeps every word up to the next flag, location code, attitude or name. The job written in front of a person's tag ("a cartter [[C]]") is replaced by their stored role, and an appositive job that differs is reported on the turn as `role_mismatches`. - `app/venues.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_playtest_venues_roles.py`
- [CLAUDE] The scene thread survives a side action (playtest #20, ended the test). The engine now keeps what the player is in the middle of as a small stored record: the pursuit in their own words or the accepted quest and its step, the target ("the hooded figure"), and who came along (asked in the player's line and shown going along in the prose). It is updated from the player's line (a new pursuit, someone asked along, the player letting it go) and after the turn from the quest report, the move and the prose; it ends when dropped, when its quest is completed, failed or abandoned, or after six untouched turns. The draft and verifier get `world_state.scene_thread` with an instruction that a side action happens inside it, the paragraph writer and consolidator get `scene_thread`, and the suggestion ask offers at least one move that carries it on. Following now agrees with position: going after someone counts as travel, the draft and writer are told that without a move the player is still in the current place (`movement_contract.no_move_means`, `scene_facts.player_stays_in`), the player follows into a known place the prose names (rule `follow_named`), and otherwise the journal says they followed with no place named and stayed. The narration no longer makes the player's choices: the shared prose voice and the writer's closing beat ask to end on the situation, and a closing sentence where "you decide/choose/opt to" commits the player to something their line did not say is dropped. Stored in `settings.scene_thread` (saves and rewinds). - `app/scene_thread.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_playtest_scene_thread.py`

- [CLAUDE] The repeat-word filter no longer hides the words of a power's name. It hides content words used more than three times in recent scenes and spares names, but the spared list held only people, places and items: ability, skill and proficiency names, NPC abilities and an item's granted abilities or enchantments were open to it, so a power used often would come out half-written (playtest #10). - `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_playtest_power_names_kept.py`
- [CLAUDE] Review of the playtest #1-#9 fixes, from an offline replay of logged turns 2-6 through `play_turn` - `app/conversation.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/proficiencies.py`. Turn 5's "come out now", commanding whatever is hiding, was resolved to Aria as the one the player was talking to, which tells the draft only Aria may answer; a call to someone unseen ("come out", "whoever is hiding", "show yourself") now addresses nobody present, everyone here only listens, and a tag or name still wins. - [CLAUDE] Playtest issue #4: input suggestions were bland ("Inquire about local rumors", "Negotiate a favor with Aria") and the panel had no close button. The packet was world lists plus the whole setup (about 39,000 characters on the logged save) with no scene text, so the model could only offer moves that fit anywhere. `generate_input_suggestions()` now sends a `scene` block built by the engine: the last 900 characters of the last narration with `[[codes]]` removed, the questions that narration left open, the people in it (present or named) with role, attitude, what they care about, known facts and who faces the player (from the conversation target), and active quests and open offers; `world_state` keeps tone, the player, every carried item by name (`carried_items`, since the turn planner keeps only items the query names and the inventory is what made the one good suggestion), skills, abilities and a few summaries, and drops the setup block, so the logged save's packet is about 10,000 characters and the output budget is unchanged. `world.get_input_suggestions()` keys its lookups on the last scene's words instead of a fixed phrase. The ask is for moves that only fit this scene, naming a person, thing or place, answering an open question or working a want or quest when there is one; no sample answers. A "Deeper idea" button posts `deeper: true` to `/api/suggestions` for one action (170 characters) and a one-line why (90), phase `input_suggestion_deeper`, rendered as its own card with a use button. The panel has a close (x) button and Escape inside it closes it (regen text kept, focus back on Ideas); close stays usable while the model is busy, and the panel's height is capped to the room under the top bar so the head is never covered on a small screen. - `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`, `tests/test_playtest_suggestions.py`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issue #2: saving a preset under a name already in the list gave two identical names, and renaming had no obvious control. `uniquePresetLabel()` gives the bare name to the first and the lowest free number from 1 to the next, so a number freed by a delete is reused (no 1, 2, 3, 15, 17 gaps); built-in names count as taken, replacing the old " copy" suffix, and a rename may keep its own name. Save, New and rename (typing in Preset name) all use it. A Rename button beside Save and Delete opens the preset card and focuses the name box; Enter or leaving the box shows the name it was stored under and says so when a number was added. It is disabled for built-ins and game starts. - `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `tools/test_preset_label_unique.js`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issues #7 (major) and #6(a), #6(c): the game could not tell who the player was talking to. Turn 5 revealed a hidden figure (CAST interacting C); the active scene then listed Aria and Ashwalker both as "replies to the player", so turn 6's untagged insult went to Aria and the prose gave her the player's words. `app/conversation.py` now decides the addressee in the engine before any model call, in a fixed order: an @tag (code, `@C<name>` or alias); a present NPC named in the player's own words (a name inside the quote or after "about" is what the line is about, not who hears it; "Aria, ..." inside a quote addresses her); a group phrase (everyone, you all, both of you; bare "them" is not one, turn 6 used it of one figure); the player's pick on the new chip; then whoever the last turn left facing the player: someone it revealed or brought in (CAST interacting a newcomer), else the addressee if they answered, else the last NPC who spoke in the final prose. The draft footer leads with the result (who answers, who is only listening, that the quoted words are the player's), `world_state.conversation_turn` carries it to the draft and verify passes (the handoff keeps it), the paragraph briefs and the consolidator get `conversation` with who_answers / listening_only, and the newest logged NPC line is left out of world_state when it belongs to someone the player is not talking to. The state is the `settings.conversation` row (in saves, and restored by a rewind) and `active_scene.interacting` now follows it instead of growing with every CAST; an old save without the row takes the newest interacting code. A "Talking to" chip above the input shows the target after each turn, and its list picks a person here, everyone, or nobody (`POST /api/conversation/target`). #6(c): world.db confirmed the engine moved the player to Plains2 on turn 6 (a talk line; the draft wrote WALK northeast / MOVE Plains2, the final prose stayed in the lane); a move or map walk on a turn with no travel intent that the final prose does not show is now dropped (movement status `dropped_unshown`). Two older tests cast a code no NPC had; their fixtures now put that person in the scene. - `app/conversation.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/skin.css`, `tests/test_playtest_conversation.py`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issues #3, #5 and #6(b): the paragraph writer (`narration_para_N`) invented an inn and tagged it with the player's boots code (shown as "Inkwell Inn Well-Worn Boots"), turned the draft's baker into a cartographer, answered one remark three times, and put the player's threat in Aria's mouth. It was a separate call per beat that got a 120-character op summary, bare codes, and the player's input as new text every beat. Each brief now carries `may_mention` as code, name, kind (and role for people; unnamed codes are left out), `scene_facts` (where, the cast the draft put in the scene with roles, the player's move, and who said what with the player's quoted words marked `speaker: player`), `scene_draft`, and `draft_slice`, its share of the draft in order and never cut inside a quotation. The writer is told the draft already decided what happens, to add texture, and not to add a named place, person or job. Only the beat that answers the player is told to respond; later beats get a `reply_status` that the reply is given. `already_said` now includes earlier paragraphs' spoken lines (newest last) as well as their first sentences. A beat that gives the player's words to another speaker, or repeats an earlier paragraph's spoken line, is rewritten once with the problem named, then dropped. The consolidator gets the player's words and watches for a reply given twice. The repeat pass on every turn adds two tests: a re-worded sentence (content stems, so grows/growing and reordering match, at 0.7 with five or more content words each) and a re-answered spoken line across paragraphs (a whole quotation sharing a five-word run with three content words, or re-worded the same way). A complete spoken line that restates an earlier one is now dropped whole, not just its opening fragment. Code expansion no longer writes an entity's name after words that name something else: a [[code]] after a different proper name, or after a determiner and a noun that is not part of the entity's name (for non-people), is dropped and the words kept; a role before a person's code still expands. - `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_playtest_paragraph_writer.py`, `tests/test_aria_map_findings.py`
- [CLAUDE] Playtest issue #9: asking a baker "these interest you?" gave two loaves and two tarts. Four causes. Item bands rolled dice ("small" = 1d2), so the model's habitual `GRANT "x" QTY small` for one object came up 2; the `item_count` table is now a count (trivial and small 1, moderate 2, large 3, huge 5) and an explicit number such as `QTY 20` is kept as written instead of being re-rolled. Grounding asked only whether anything arrived anywhere in the text, so one hand-over grounded every named item and nothing read what happened to it afterwards; `_prose_item_outcome()` now reads the prose per item and per sentence (a hand-over, purchase, pick-up, loot, craft or "into your hands" is a gain; a hand-back, a gift to someone else, a refusal or eating it on the spot is a loss; third-person verbs such as "she takes the tart" are never the player; "take a bite/sip" is not a pickup), and the last word on the item in the FINAL narration decides, so a draft hand-over the final prose turns into "you hand it back" grants nothing. The draft-naming check also wants the item's head noun. The prose said "The baker at CAR": the mention `@Caria` matched the code pattern as `@CAR`, which then reached the paragraph writer's cast; the pattern now needs a word boundary after an `@code`, and the name repair renders a bare known multi-letter code as `Name [[CODE]]` and removes a referenced code that names nothing (with its preposition, or as "Someone" at the start of a sentence). The DSL prompt states what QTY counts and what may be granted. - `app/rng.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `tests/test_playtest_item_grants.py`
- [CLAUDE] Fixes from a two-turn game where Aria "handed over" a map and then asked to be paid for it. The depth retry (which lengthens a short scene) was asked for "NPC reaction, consequence, and concrete choices" and grew a 470-character opening to 2,310 with "Let me offer you a small map ... Take this", no op behind it, so nothing reached the inventory or the map and the next turn treated the gift as a sale; it now asks for texture only, and an expansion that adds a hand-over the draft did not have is rejected (`depth_retry_rejected`) and the shorter true scene kept. The DSL skip-verify setting skipped the fact check unconditionally, including a 0.24-certainty turn that granted and took an item and opened a quest; it now skips only at 0.6 certainty or more with no item, gold, XP, skill, ability or quest change. A repeated speech fragment ('"Twelve gold...', an unclosed quote or a trailing ellipsis) is dropped like any other repeat; it opened the same reply three times because it was under the 45-character floor, and complete short lines may still echo. - `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`
- [CLAUDE] Saving no longer replaces the scene. "Saved campaign slot: ..." was written into the narration box, so the turn the player was reading vanished while Last input stayed; saves, "Model settings saved" and "World imported" now show as a short status in the Scene header, an import repaints its own last turn, and the five setup-screen "Model randomizer unavailable" notices go to the setup status line instead of overwriting the loaded game's scene. - `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Fixes from the "Miriam Shaw" save. Growth speed was applied twice: the dice roller already multiplies a rolled skill or XP gain by the world's growth speed, then `_apply_skills` and the XP code scaled it again, so one prayer on a "very fast" world made a new Prayer skill at 8 (rolled 2, x2.2, x2); rolled amounts now carry `growth_applied` and are not scaled twice. An item gain must be named in the draft prose the ops were written against (now kept as `_dsl.draft_narration`) or the player's words: the draft never mentioned beads, a later rewrite added "beads clasped in your hands", and two prayer beads were granted; "presses ... into your hands" with a longer object now counts as arrival. Ask no longer echoes its own prompt (one answer was the prompt's sample sentence word for word, the next invented "The packet you received includes a voice recording"): the prompt has no internal wording or sample, a follow-up pronoun reuses the last subject, an unmatched question gets a plain "which one?" without a model call, and an item's record carries its journal history. A turn with no model summary is summarised from the scene's first sentences instead of "response: scene advanced with DSL ops". `NPC_NEW` reads bare arguments by kind (a location only from a location code), so `NPC_NEW Dockwick "Dockwick" carter L1 friendly` no longer mints a place called Dockwick; replacement NPC names avoid place names and first parts other NPCs already use (Ivycoil, Ivywick, Ivyfield), on every naming path. The server warns (`stale_server` notice) when its Python changed on disk after it started: this save was set up by an updated page against an old server, which ignored the gear list. - `app/world.py`, `app/rng.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `app/ask.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/naming.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/failsafe.py`
- [CLAUDE] `tools/playtest_setup_presets.py` isolates every data path (`isolated_data_env`: world.db, saves, history, source index, consolidated facts, traces). It set a non-existent `AI_RPG_DATA_DIR`, so its scratch servers and the live quest checks wrote into the real `data/world.db` and save folder. - `tools/`
- [CLAUDE] Quest gate tuned on a second and third live Qwen3 8B run: offers phrased as "looking for a hand with the nets" or "needs help loading cargo" now count, a giver the story names before an NPC row exists ("the ferryman") is kept by name, and a narrator mark keeps its trust when the parser proposes the same title. Third run: "Ferryman's Job" offered on the asking turn, active after "I'll take the job", the repeat proposal rejected as a duplicate; turns took 26 to 31 s on Qwen3 8B (72 to 94 s before `/no_think`). - `app/quest_parser.py`
- [CLAUDE] Qwen3's hidden reasoning reached the player: on Qwen3 8B the prose-repair reply came back as "<think>
Okay, the user wants me to rewrite the given scene..." and became the narration. Every provider reply now passes `strip_reasoning()` (a closed `<think>` block, and an unclosed one cut by the token cap), and a Qwen3 model is sent `/no_think` so it stops spending the budget and the time on reasoning. - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The browser's own lock roll re-locked the only power of a progression start after the server had opened it; it keeps one usable now. The transmigration template's closing sentences were its rules written as prose ("no free power", "without a hero starter pack", "the story starts cold at the landing, not mid-local intrigue"); they are in-world lines now. Final 8B pass: 5 lint hits over 6 runs (3 controls, Overpowered, Cyberpunk, Adventurers), down from 57 over 8, the rest being backstories that name the start location in other words. - `static/app.js`, `app/setup_composer.py`
- [CLAUDE] Third batch from the Qwen3 8B preset playtest. The Simple walk now rolls `tech_level`, `world_races` and `start_location` before the fields that read them: gear was rolled against the default "iron age" (a cyberpunk kit got wool trousers) and every backstory was written before its start location existed. The browser snapshot always carries existing ability cards, so a field rolled before powers in the order (custom skills on Start) sees them, and the engine pins the seed named in custom skills to a usable card (the model wrote "weak seed skill: Ember Resolve" beside four powers none of which was Ember Resolve). The tech-level prompt asks for an era of technology ("mythic age" came back). - `app/llm.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Second batch from the Qwen3 8B preset playtest. Seed abilities all shared two boilerplate sentences, so the near-duplicate check scored every pair of 30 seeds as duplicates (435 of 435; 2 now); each roll looped four de-duplication rounds of failed model calls, and the last resort renamed a card after another seed ("Salt Taste" that writes a clean line, "Heat Tolerance" that angles a mirror) and appended "Focuses on a different practical niche". It now redraws another seed and never renames. Twelve seed hints that opened with a noun ("You can flame leans toward the larger lie") are rewritten as verbs. Idea-bank sparks go to prose fields only: short fields answered with a spark verbatim (tone "Witty, tired, and allergic to corporate hope slogans", faction pressure "Corporate security that smiles with non-lethal policies"). The model's intent may only raise growth to the weak-seed compounding frame when the idea's words ask for it, so Adventurers, Depths and Academy no longer get "OP_MC_FRAME: start with one weak seed power" in custom skills; that label is also stripped at Start. An idea that mentions levels or XP switches levelling on ("leveling and ranks on" did not). A progression start always has one usable power (the Overpowered seed came back locked). The backstory note gives former-world jobs and crossings only to transmigrated or reincarnated starts, and no longer lists example jobs (three runs became clerks); the transmigration template no longer replaces a native backstory. "Mosswake Gate", the database's default location, is out of the arrival banks. Model gear in a finger, neck or wrist slot needs a name that fits (a wooden comb went on FINGER). In the browser: Simple mode no longer copies the World vibe over the genre ("Setting frame: Cyberpunk Mythic. Power here is earned: talent starts small and grows with risk and training; nobody is b"); intent-decided switches (levelling, skill levels, proficiencies) skip the model; Start no longer applies the default-selected preset when nothing was confirmed or typed, fills an empty backstory and engine-placeholder gear instead of skipping every Simple field, keeps fields the idea just decided, and no longer pads the backstory with "Their past is known; they are near the opening with ordinary means and local pressure." - `app/llm.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/gear.py`, `app/world.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Setup fixes from a headless Qwen3 8B playtest of New game (`tools/playtest_setup_presets.py`, three controls plus presets). The offline fallback answered every single-field request with 3 to 8 extra fields it had cross-checked or defaulted (`backstory_mode`, `world_style`, `tone`, ...) and the page applied them, so a transmigrated start flipped to "known" on Start; it now returns only the requested fields. It also rolled every field blind from a flat pool: it now uses the compiled intent first (levelling on for a progression idea), a tech level that fits the genre (a frontier fantasy had drawn "spacefaring salvage"), a World vibe that is never blank, custom skills that name the seed instead of "Do not seed starting skills", no racial casting when world magic is forbidden, and a name that reads as the character's sex (a male character had been named Nadia Voss). The keyword intent wrote a player power rule into the World vibe ("Power fantasy: start weak, growth compounds through play"); it writes the world fact now and switches levelling on. Seed abilities' base text no longer carries design notes ("At F rank the effect is brief, incomplete, or easy to miss. With practice it may grow toward legendary ego-weapon"), and the model's inspiration list no longer shows seed names (Qwen3 8B returned "Weapon Name", "Guest Right" and "Bone Broth" verbatim). Ability names drop retry wording ("Eclipse Sigil (Reinvented)"); the ability prompt says the game has no AC, saves, advantage or damage types and asks for one cost stated once. The name prompt steers with a random starting letter per name part instead of examples (four 8B rolls in a row ended in "Voss"). A backstory ends at `start_location` when one is set. Model gear follows the item's name when its slot disagrees (a pocket knife on the wrists, a satchel on the waist) and far-off model weights (boots at 6 kg) are re-estimated; a player's own card keeps its choices. A carried item gives no stat bonus. The race-ability and inventory-rule prompts describe the peoples and the world, not the player, in words rather than d20 shorthand. - `app/llm.py`, `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/gear.py`
- [CLAUDE] MLE no longer loads a different model than the one named. With the launcher set to `qwen3:8b` and only `qwen2.5-7b-instruct-q4_k_m.gguf` in `data/mle-models`, `resolve_model_path()` fell through to "the only GGUF in the folder" and ran Qwen2.5 7B while every screen said qwen3:8b; a path to a GGUF that does not exist also reported ready. The only-file fallback now stands in for the default name alone, a named model or path that is missing fails with the usual "no model loaded" detail, and `status()` reports the `file` it loaded plus a `substituted` note when the default name resolved to a differently named file. - `app/mle.py`
- [CLAUDE] `tools/playtest_setup_presets.py` drives New game in a headless browser like a player: three controls (untouched, offline fallback, Overpowered idea typed by hand) and built-in presets through Confirm preset, recording every setting, which calls fell back, and page errors. - `tools/`
- [CLAUDE] Powers had two folds: the section header and, inside it, a second "Powers" dropdown the builder was moved into through an empty `setupAdvancedPowersMount` div. The inner dropdown and the mount are gone; the section header is the only fold, its summary line (count and randomize range) sits at the top of the panel, and Randomize no longer forces the inner fold open. - `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] A starting-gear comma list imported from an old preset loaded as one card. The legacy importer split on semicolons and newlines only, so "cloak, pouch, vial, tool, bread" became a single card holding every name with no slot, the same shape as the original Worn gear bug. It now splits commas outside brackets, picks the slot from a slot word or the item name, and marks the first feet, torso and legs item required; `tools/test_simple_gear_hyphen.js` covers it. - `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] The llama.cpp server provider gets the full story contract when its window holds it. `fitting_system_prompts()` handed it the compact contract at every size, a leftover from when that provider meant a small GGUF at 8192, so a 32k llama.cpp server never saw the full narrator rules while MLE did. It is now judged by its resolved window like MLE. The quest and stage forms in the Tools pane keep a 720px measure instead of running the whole screen — `app/llm.py`, `static/styles.css`, `tests/test_context_budget.py`, `tests/test_shared_prefix.py`
- [CLAUDE] Setup fields have parents, and Randomize honours them. A roll through the Overpowered preset with the sex locked to male came back with a feminine name, and the World vibe box held a power rule. Causes: dependencies existed only between phases, and inside the identity phase (and in the "character" group and the Simple walk) the name rolled before the sex; the name's contract never mentioned the sex while the identity rules shipped example names, which a 7B pastes; and `custom_style` read the `power_fantasy` intent and banned growth timers but not growth slogans. Now every field contract may declare `depends_on`; `dependency_sorted()` puts parents first inside each phase and in every group list, the composer tree reports `dependencies`, and the browser walks its lists in that order. Each requested field ships `agree_with` (its parents' current values, locked ones under `locked_parents`) and `decide_first` for a parent not yet set, on the group, generic single-field, and name-button prompts. The name contract says the name must read as `player_sex` and fit `world_style`; the example names are gone from every prompt. `custom_style` no longer reads the power frame, bans growth slogans, and is asked for as a description of the world: the contract, the single-field note, the group rule, and the repair pass all say "describe the world" and list what that means (the land and its climate, who lives there and who holds power, how magic or technology sits in daily life, how power is earned by anyone who has it, the mood of play), with `world_style`, `tone`, `tech_level`, `magic_level` and the idea shipped as the context. The fallback for a progression preset states the world fact ("Power here is earned: talent starts small and grows with risk and training; nobody is born strong."). There is no regex gate on the vibe; the prompt is the fix. Hair, face, and clothes read the body and the world instead of the power frame. The preset is "Overpowered", open to any setting and origin — `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`, `static/app.js`, `tests/test_setup_hierarchy.py`

## [0.10.0-dev.3] - 2026-10-04

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. Structured starting gear, quest parser, scene history, setup playtest fixes.

### Added

- [CLAUDE] UI rebuilt as three CSS layers: `static/ui/tokens.css` (every colour, font, radius, shadow; five themes as a hue), `static/styles.css` (layout only), `static/ui/skin.css` (the look). The look is derived from the logo and key art: a black void with a cast of one light, shards with cut corners, thread hairlines, the logo image as the main-menu title.
