Rules in `docs/UI_RULEBOOK.md` — `static/ui/`, `static/index.html`, `static/popout.html`, `app/main.py`
- [CLAUDE] `static/ui/interact.js`: section headings fold and remember it; hovering a name shows a translucent peek card; right-click on a name, an inventory row, or a map tile opens an action menu (talk, ask, examine, equip, step, travel, pin, details); the LLM and app settings forms show only the selected provider's fields — `static/ui/interact.js`, `docs/UI_RULEBOOK.md` §3.7
- [CLAUDE] Nine new director presets: Isekai, Wuxia, Academy, Wasteland, Salvage, Gothic, Noir, Apocalypse, Hearth. Every built-in idea is rewritten in the composer's own vocabulary (difficulty word, tone phrase, magic/economy/loot option names, location-theme keyword first) so the chip and the rolled world agree — `static/app.js` `DIRECTOR_PRESETS_BUILTIN`
- [CLAUDE] `tools/capture_skin_shots.py` screenshots every surface in all themes against a scratch database — `tools/`
- [CLAUDE] Failsafes. A model failure stops the turn and opens a dialog that names the problem, lists what to try (close apps using a lot of RAM, lower Context tokens, start the server, check the key), and offers "Continue anyway": the offline narrator for a turn, or the 8192-token context when the requested size did not fit. The MLE loader no longer shrinks the context silently; `AI_RPG_CONTEXT_FALLBACK=auto` restores that for scripts. `/api/turn` and `/api/continue` take `allow_fallback`; `/api/model-status` and `/api/llm-runtime` carry a `problem`; `POST /api/failsafe/mle-fallback` accepts the smaller context — `app/failsafe.py`, `app/mle.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/ui/failsafe.js`, `tests/test_failsafe.py`

### Changed

- [CLAUDE] Clicking a neighbouring tile on the map lens now walks there. The lens draws a player-relative window while its tiles carry world coordinates; the click handler added only the window offset, so every click read as thousands of tiles away and nothing moved — `static/app.js` `bindLocalMapClick`
- [CLAUDE] Layout pass. New game: the presets card starts collapsed, the six optional sections (Identity, Powers, World, People, Rules, Checks) start folded with their descriptions as summaries, and optional fields sit in two columns above 900px instead of one column below 1500px; the page went from about 14,000px of scroll in a 375px window to about 1,600px in a 730px window. Play: the Move card holds only the pad, map-art tools fold under their own heading, the travel hint is one line, the last-input band is one line, and narration sits in an 80ch reading column inside the wide scene box — `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `static/ui/interact.js`
- [CLAUDE] The narration block is a flex column, so the scene text starts under its heading instead of after a blank band; checkboxes are 16px, not 42px fields; the empty map well is painted from the `--ink` token — `static/styles.css`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] The model verifier returns a verdict, not a whole turn. The verify pass asked for "a corrected full turn JSON"; a real trace showed that reply overrunning its token cap at 5,224 characters and costing 71% of a 107 s cloud turn, mostly re-emitting narration the draft already had. The verifier now answers with `verdict` (pass or revise), `issues`, and a `patch` of only the turn keys it replaces, with a replacement narration only when the prose itself is wrong. `_resolve_verified_turn` merges the patch over the draft, keeps the draft prose unless replaced, and writes `self_check` from the verdict so the verification memory and the UI see the same shape as before. The legacy full-turn reply is still accepted; an echo of `world_state` still trips the breaker. Verify token caps are about half what they were (local rich 1300 → 800, API rich 3600 → 1500, compact 700 → 500), and the trace records the reply kind, the verdict, and the patched keys — `app/llm.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `tests/test_verifier_verdict.py`
- [CLAUDE] Local models get one system prefix per turn. MLE (llama-cpp-python in-process) and the llama.cpp server reuse their KV cache only for an identical token prefix, and a turn was sending three or four unrelated system prompts (the DSL draft contract, `VERIFY_PROMPT`, `PROSE_REPAIR_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, and the JSON contract for the depth fallback), so every call after the draft re-read a fresh prefix. `system_prompt_for_phase()` now gives the verify call, the prose repairs, and the JSON depth fallback the draft's exact system prompt (per-turn blocks included) as a prefix with the phase's bare contract after it, on providers `mle` and `llama_cpp` only; the `openai` provider keeps the small per-task prompts because its tokens are billed and nothing is cached. The draft call sends what it always sent, and the trace's start event records `shared_prompt_prefix` — `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_shared_prefix.py`
- [CLAUDE] Character art is no longer inlined in every state payload. `get_state()` names `player_portrait` and `player_fullbody` by `kind`, `updated_at`, a content `token`, and a `url`, never `data_url`; `GET /api/player-art/{kind}?v=<token>` serves the stored image bytes with immutable cache headers for the current token and 404s when nothing is stored. The browser fetches once per token and keeps the copy in localStorage (`morkyn-player-portrait-token`, `morkyn-player-fullbody-token`); a state entry that still carries `data_url` (older server) is used as before. Each `/api/state` poll and turn payload was shipping about 1.2 MB of base64 the page already had — `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`, `tests/test_player_art_route.py`, `tests/test_turn_pipeline_shortcuts.py`
- [GROK] The version string the game shows is `V0.10.0-wip`. The tagged stable release remains `v0.9.12` — `app/main.py`
- [GROK] The local story provider id is `mle`, and the model field is `mle_model` (default `qwen3:8b`). A saved model name from the previous local engine is copied into `mle_model` when that field is empty, and those old keys are dropped from the in-memory config. Launcher prefs for this machine are already `mle`. llama.cpp, Forge, ComfyUI, and cloud APIs stay — `app/llm.py`, `app/launcher_prefs.py`, `Morkyn.ps1`, `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Token limits follow the model. The context window, soft response target and hard cap used to be one global each (a launcher pref exported as env, a row in `model_config`), so a 32B inherited the 7B's numbers and the launcher's first 8192 followed every model. `app/model_limits.py` resolves them per model, explicit env > the player's saved numbers for that model (`settings.model_limits`, keyed by GGUF file name, MLE name, or API model) > automatic. Automatic reads the GGUF header without loading the model (parameter count, trained context, layers and KV heads for the cache cost per token) and nvidia-smi for the card, and opens the largest window up to the trained one whose cache fits beside the weights; with only a name it uses a tokens-per-billion scale (7B → 32768, 14B → 18432, 70B → 8192) capped by the trained context, and response caps step with size (≤4B 1200/1600, ≤10B 1500/2000, ≤20B 1800/2400, above 2000/2600; API models 1500/2000). `get_model_config()` carries the result as `context_window`, the two caps and `model_limits.source`; MLE opens the file at that window and reopens it when the number changes; a managed llama.cpp server starts with it. The LLM settings form gained a Token limits block: the automatic numbers and why, a Context tokens field beside the two caps, "use my own numbers for this model and remember them" (editing a number ticks it), and Back to auto; Save Model stores them per model through `limits_mode`. `GET/POST /api/model-limits` read and set them directly. Launcher prefs default to `llama_cpp_context: "auto"` and `0` for the caps; a number is still an explicit override exported as env, and the Gatehouse asks the app (`python -m app.model_limits --context <gguf>`) for a managed server's window — `app/model_limits.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/mle.py`, `app/main.py`, `app/launcher_prefs.py`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `Morkyn.ps1`, `tests/test_model_limits.py`

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Randomize Abilities reaches the model and says where the cards came from. "Randomize This" on a card swapped in a local preset without asking the model; the section lock emptied a direct `field:special_abilities` request on the server so the page filled from its local pool; a model error swapped in the seed pool while the quality gate re-labelled it `llm`; and the only notice of any of this went to the hidden game-view output. Now both buttons call the model with the current setup, a direct request survives the section lock, a fallback carries `fallback_used`/`fallback_reason`, and the setup status line reports model or fallback. Both buttons stay locked, with a visible hint, until the setup has some text to build on (a world description, an idea, a backstory, or skill/race notes) — `app/llm.py`, `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `tests/test_ability_randomize_fallback_marker.py`
- [CLAUDE] The DSL `CAST` op reaches the world. `scene_cast` was missing from `TURN_SHAPE_KEYS`/`TURN_SHAPE_ORDER`, so `_clean_turn_for_handoff` dropped it on every handoff and `settings.active_scene` was only ever written by the debug cast command; speaker notes, bare-name resolution, ask subject and local-intel scene codes ran on an empty scene all campaign — `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_scene_cast_handoff.py`
- [CLAUDE] Continue carries `allow_fallback` to every turn it can play. The opening turn and a forced world-event beat went through helpers without the flag, so with the model down those turns were written by the offline narrator and the forced beat was resolved instead of raising the failsafe dialog — `app/world.py` (`play_opening_turn`, `play_world_event_turn`, the in-turn forced-event steal), `tests/test_continue_turn_failsafe.py`
- [CLAUDE] `quest_clocks` and `name_ledger` are campaign state: deleted on a new playthrough and carried by export/import and campaign slots (`WORLD_TABLES`, `RESTORE_ORDER`, `_REPLACE_ONLY_WHEN_EXPORTED`). A new campaign inherited the last one's offer clocks (a reused NPC id skipped its propensity roll and stayed frozen until the new world day passed the old one) and its minted names — `app/world.py`, `tests/test_playthrough_tables.py`
- [CLAUDE] The DSL trace entry reads `_dsl` before the cleanup drops it: `ops_count` was null on every DSL turn and `malformed_ops` never reached the trace file. `_dsl` now survives the cleanup like `_narration_pipeline`, is stripped from the verifier's draft by `_prompt_draft()`, and lands in `debug.dsl` on the turn payload — `app/llm.py`, `app/world.py`, `tests/test_scene_cast_handoff.py`
- [CLAUDE] The map-move 409 hint is shown, not "[object Object]". `/api/tiles/map/move` answers confinement and exhaustion with an object in `detail`; `stepMap` and `walkToTile` now read it through `apiErrorMessage()` so the travel banner and the alert carry the server's line — `static/app.js`, `tests/test_map_move_error_message.py`
- [CLAUDE] After MLE loads at the 8,192-token fallback, `context_window_tokens()` returns the loaded context (`mle.loaded_context()`) instead of the env value, so the compact contract is chosen, the packet is pruned for 8,192 and the response cap fits; before, every turn overflowed llama_cpp three times and fell back — `app/llm.py`, `app/mle.py`, `tests/test_mle_context_window.py`
- [CLAUDE] llama_cpp's own overflow wording ("Requested tokens (N) exceed context window of M") and llama-server's ("exceeds the available context") count as context-length errors, so the draft takes `draft_compact_retry` and the verifier `verify_compact_retry` instead of re-sending the identical overflowing prompt as `draft_retry` — `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_mle_context_window.py`
- [CLAUDE] Quest tables are inside the rewind record. `tick_quest_timers` runs on every `apply_turn`, so Regenerate ticked a timed quest twice and could fail it a turn early, and quests or offers created inside the rewound turn stayed; `quests`, `quest_steps` (by id and max id) and the whole `quest_clocks` table are now snapshotted and restored — `app/world.py`, `tests/test_rewind_undoes_turn_writes.py`
- [CLAUDE] Rewind undoes what `play_turn` wrote before the snapshot. The social check's `npcs.attitude`/`trust` and `last_social`, and the write-once pronoun pin, are captured by `_capture_pre_turn_rows()` before the write and handed to the snapshot as `result["_snapshot_rows"]`; the settings keys a turn writes (`active_scene`, `last_social`, `travel_ready`, ...) are snapshotted with their absence, so a regenerated turn no longer stacks trust or asserts a discarded narration's pronoun — `app/world.py`, `tests/test_rewind_undoes_turn_writes.py`
- [CLAUDE] A dead model is one failed call, not three. With no GGUF path saved, the DSL draft failed, the JSON draft was tried anyway, and then the JSON retry ran too: three calls and 24 seconds for an error known before the first one. On a real timeout the same path waited the full draft timeout twice. `_try_dsl_draft` now raises refused, timed-out, and unconfigured-model errors (`_is_model_unavailable_error`), and the JSON draft's generic retry is skipped for them. A reply the parser cannot read still falls back to the JSON draft — `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_turn_pipeline_shortcuts.py`
- [CLAUDE] A reply the response cap cut off is closed locally before the model repair runs. A verify pass came back as 5,224 characters ending mid-string and the 26-second repair call returned the same characters with the quotes closed. `_close_truncated_json` keeps a string cut mid-prose, drops a clipped bare literal (15 is not 150) and a key that never got its value, then closes the open arrays and objects; garbage still goes to the model repair — `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The prose-only repairs (depth, voice, answer act, recall) carried the full JSON turn contract (~9.5k tokens) as their system prompt, a prefix no other call in the turn shares. They now carry `PROSE_REPAIR_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (~1.1k tokens: return prose only, keep codes, no menus, plus the voice rules). The whole-turn JSON depth fallback keeps the JSON contract it needs — `app/prompts.py`, `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] Three things the turn built every turn and dropped before the model: the server-rolled skill checks (`mechanics_context.resolved_checks`, with the attitude a failed social roll stored on the NPC), the narrator-only NPC psychology block (grudges and agendas written by `app/npc_psychology.py`), and the per-action skill search that had replaced the catalog. The system prompt had a rule for each. `_mechanics_view` now ships `resolved_checks`, `action_spend` when blocked, `collapse`, `area_reputation`, `social_reputation`, and `player_inventory_codes`; `npc_psychology_context` and `skill_check_context` are on the handoff allowlist and in the visible world; the scene instruction says what each is for. A test lists every `mechanics_context.*` key the rules cite and fails when one is not shipped — `app/prompts.py`, `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_turn_pipeline_shortcuts.py`
- [CLAUDE] Character art was carried three times. The two base64 strings (1.2 MB) sat inside `settings` on every `/api/state` and turn payload beside the top-level copies the UI reads, and inside the planner packet, where the handoff filter dropped them after serialising them twice and the trace writer kept them: 2.6 MB per trace file, 2.48 MB per state response. The settings copies are removed in `get_state` and the packet copies in `build_prompt_context`. Measured on a live save: state response 2.48 MB → 1.26 MB, planner packet 2.48 MB → 41 KB — `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] The cleaned context is built once per turn; the DSL draft reused `planner_to_draft` instead of running the same cleanup again as `planner_to_dsl_draft`. The entity-name repair note is written once per turn instead of once per normalize pass (it had landed five times in one `self_check`) — `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] An over-budget turn packet is pruned as JSON, not cut as text. `enforce_token_budget` shrank a too-large user prompt by cutting it from the middle and inserting a marker, and every turn prompt is one JSON object, so the model received an unparseable packet. When the prompt parses as a JSON object it now shortens `world_state` strings past `WORLD_STATE_STRING_CAP` (400), then drops `world_state` keys in `WORLD_STATE_DROP_ORDER` (unlisted keys first; `player`, `current_location`, `world_time`, `turn`, `movement_contract`, `naming_contract`, `recall_contract`, `mechanics_context` last) and re-serialises after each step, so the packet stays valid JSON and the instruction, player input, turn kind, output contract and instructions fields are never touched; the middle cut runs only when that is still not enough. The diagnostics dict records `keys_dropped` and `strings_shortened`. Non-JSON prompts keep the middle cut — `app/llm.py`, `tests/test_token_budget_json.py`
- [CLAUDE] A dead model now costs one timeout per turn instead of one per prose pass: the depth ladder stops after a timed-out or refused prose retry instead of sending the whole-turn JSON retry with the same timeout, and the voice, answer-act and recall passes read a shared `model_unavailable` flag on the failed usage row and skip their own call. Reasoned on a llama_cpp draft timeout of 900s: the player waited 1800s (plus a managed-restart wait on a refused connection) before the unverified short draft returned; now 900s. Content failures still fall through to the JSON fallback — app/llm.py (`_failed_pass_usage`, `_model_unavailable_noted`, `_ensure_narration_depth`, `_ensure_narration_voice`, `_ensure_answer_act`, `_ensure_recall_specifics`), tests/test_dead_model_prose_passes.py
- [CLAUDE] `mle.status()` no longer parks behind an in-flight generation: when the model for the resolved path is already loaded it reports without taking the lock (as `loaded_context()` already did), and otherwise it waits at most 2s and reports `busy: true` with a detail saying MLE is generating. Test Connection and POST /api/failsafe/mle-fallback previously blocked for up to AI_RPG_MLE_TIMEOUT (300s) while a turn ran — app/mle.py (`status`, `_loaded_report`, `_STATUS_LOCK_WAIT`), tests/test_mle_provider.py
- [CLAUDE] The MLE per-call timeout is now an error, not a silent truncation: `_SampleMask` records when the deadline forces EOS and `_generate` raises `MleNotReady` with `timed out after Ns`, which the existing timeout guards (`_is_timeout_error`, `_is_model_unavailable_error`, `_transport_error_message`) and the failsafe (`timeout` code) already recognise. A 300s stall that produced half a JSON draft was previously parsed as a finished turn or sent to a JSON repair call against the saturated model; now the turn short-circuits the way a server timeout does — app/mle.py (`_SampleMask.hit`, `_generate`), tests/test_mle_provider.py
- [CLAUDE] Skill checks roll from the campaign seed and land in the dice audit. `play_turn` resolved the player's check with an unseeded `random.Random()`, so a rewind + regenerate of the same input could turn a 17 into a 2 and apply a server-authored injury that the first pass never had, while every band roll on the same turn reproduced exactly; the d20 was also never written to `dice_rolls`, so `/api/dice/recent?turn=N` showed the gold and not the check that decided the scene. Both `resolve_check` call sites now take `rng_for('skill_check', turn, seed, salt=index:skill_code)` and `apply_turn` records one `kind='check'` row per resolved check (`source='skill_check'`, natural in `rolls`, total in `value`, outcome in `band`). Measured: three regenerates of one input give one natural. — app/world.py, tests/test_dice_rolls_mirror_applied_turns.py
- [CLAUDE] Rewind drops the rewound turn's dice rows. `rewind_last_turn` deleted journal, summaries and model logs for `turn >= N` but left `dice_rolls` alone, and a regenerate re-rolls the same seeds, so each attempt appended identical rows: the per-turn feed showed three 23-gold rolls for a turn that paid once. Measured: one banded turn regenerated twice now leaves exactly the single-pass row count. — app/world.py, tests/test_dice_rolls_mirror_applied_turns.py
- [CLAUDE] Band authority `off` is no longer a dead mode. The prompt and the DSL emit bands only, and `resolve_turn_bands` returned before translating any of them, so with `off` every GOLD / XP / HP / KARMA / GRANT / TAKE / SKILL / trust / fame band was silently dropped with no journal line: the prose paid the player and found rope, the record showed nothing. A band now becomes its notation's fixed minimum through `rng.fixed_magnitude` (no dice, unscaled, sign from the prefix), the band key is consumed, and the mapping is reported on `state.dice_rolls` and recorded in `dice_rolls` with `fixed=True`; a bare number still passes through unchanged. Measured: GOLD small / GRANT rope QTY small / SKILL Climbing DELTA small under `off` now pays, creates the rope row and raises Climbing 3 -> 4. — app/world.py, app/rng.py, tests/test_band_authority_off.py
- [CLAUDE] Rewrite and Wait/Meditate/Sleep go through the failsafe. `regenerate_last_turn` and `play_wait_turn` called `play_turn` with the default `allow_fallback`, the two routes never mapped `FailsafeBlocked`, and the browser had no dialog loop for them, so with the model still down the 'Rewrite once the model is back' path wrote another offline-narrator turn with only the small banner. Both helpers take `allow_fallback`, `/api/regenerate` (new `RegenerateRequest`) and `/api/wait` carry the flag and answer 503 problems like `/api/turn`, and `requestTurn`, `regenerateTurn` and `requestWait` share one `postTurnWithFailsafe` loop. Because the rewind has already consumed the snapshot when the model refuses, a refused rewrite keeps `{turn, input_kind, content}` under the `regenerate_pending` setting and the next Rewrite replays it at the same turn instead of undoing a second turn; a new action drops the stale entry. A refused wait puts the world clock back so Retry does not charge the minutes twice. — app/world.py, app/main.py, static/app.js, tests/test_continue_turn_failsafe.py
- [CLAUDE] A retraced map step is not the same encounter forever. `move_player` seeded the travel roll from the map seed, the from/to coordinates and `len(visited)`; once the surrounding tiles were revealed that expression stopped moving, so the same step replayed the identical natural, kind, count and awareness d20 on every pass — one extreme roll made a road step a permanent ambush or permanently quiet while danger still moved with night, weather and fatigue. The pacing turn now goes through `roll_travel_encounter(turn=...)` into `roll_encounter`; the same turn still seeds the same roll, which rewind needs. — app/tile_world.py, tests/test_travel_encounter_turn_seed.py
- [CLAUDE] `map_walk` and `gear_check` survive the injury refresh. `play_turn` re-attached only `dice_rolls`, `movement` and `voice_check` after the injuries path re-read state, so on a travel turn whose check injured the player both measurements vanished from `payload.state`, and neither was ever at the payload top level. One tuple, `TURN_STATE_TELEMETRY_KEYS`, now names the set on both sides, and the payload carries `map_walk` and `gear_check` beside `movement`. — app/world.py, tests/test_turn_telemetry_survives_injury.py
- [CLAUDE] A context too small for the full story contract is told to the player. The PowerShell launcher seeded new prefs with `llama_cpp_context = 8192` while `app/launcher_prefs.py` said 32768; the server honoured 8192 (llama.cpp logs `n_ctx_seq (8192) < n_ctx_train (32768)`), switched to the compact contract, and said so once on the console only, because nothing had failed and the failsafe only fires on a failed load. `context_contract_notice()` now rides on `/api/llm-runtime` and `/api/model-status` as `notice` whenever the window the model really has is below the contract plus headroom; the browser polls once at boot and shows the Model status dialog (no fallback, quiet for ten minutes), and Test Connection prints the summary and tips. The launcher default is 32768 and the Context tokens prompt names the 12288 floor. A test asserts both launcher defaults agree and hold the contract — `app/failsafe.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/main.py`, `static/app.js`, `Morkyn.ps1`, `tests/test_context_notice.py`
- [CLAUDE] The New game form no longer sprawls. On screens 1500px and wider the setup panel took a full-bleed two-column layout meant for the Advanced step rail, so Simple mode (the default) stretched a text box across the whole monitor; below 1500px a one-column override collided with the Face field's two-column span and opened an implicit 243px sliver that squeezed Origin and Hair. Simple mode keeps the centred 1200px panel at every width, the Primary grid is two equal columns with the long text fields on their own rows, and the short fields pair up (Name | Origin, World difficulty | Hair) instead of leaving empty cells — `static/styles.css`

## [0.10.0-dev.2] - 2026-10-01

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. MLE in-process model, seeded wilderness grid, local intel, encounter board, setting rules, quests, titles, party, NPC relationships and hidden psychology, the player agent.

### Added

- [GROK] MLE (Morkyn LLM Engine) loads a local GGUF in-process and returns that completion only. `status` is ok only after the file has loaded. A missing file raises `MleNotReady` and names the path to set. Resolution order is an existing file path, `data/mle-models/<name>.gguf`, `MLE_GGUF`, then the single `.gguf` in that folder. Context comes from `AI_RPG_CONTEXT_TOKENS` (default 32768) and retries once at 8192 if that load fails. Weights are not rewritten — `app/mle.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/launcher_prefs.py`, `Morkyn.ps1`
  - A call can pass `hide_words` and `keep_words`. The mask applies while the next token is chosen, and only when the decoded piece is the whole word. A split piece stays visible. Length-1 pieces, digits, letter-digit codes, punctuation, structure words, and keep-words stay choosable. The returned text is not edited afterwards.
  - Narration drafts pass that list as `hide_words`. `keep_words` carries names, job titles, and the draft JSON keys — `app/llm.py`, `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_mle_provider.py`
- [GROK] Seeded wilderness on a 16,383-cell side. A city is a connected clump inside a 9 by 9 neighborhood, at most 81 cells, and a one-cell-wide line of that length is rejected. The center cell is 128 by 128; outer cells are smaller. The widest fine span is 1,152. The only theme input is a density percent. The model does not place cities, notices, or coordinates — `app/world_scale.py`, `app/tile_world.py`, `tests/test_world_scale.py`, `tests/test_map_walk.py`
- [GROK] Local intel. The engine owns directions, heard-about cells, shop stalls, notice boards, and personal-quest clocks. Heard cells stay separate from walked cells. A mark is refused on a cell in neither set. Black-market answers fail closed. An offer does not appear on the first day of its 30-day month — `app/local_intel.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py`, `tests/test_local_intel.py`
- [GROK] Encounter board. A fight tracks named people who are the target, who step in, or who are already on the board. A crowd is not a combatant list. A move the player has not seen is stored as what was seen, with no effectiveness attached — `app/encounter_board.py`, `app/world.py`, `tests/test_encounter_board.py`
- [GROK] Setting rules, written before the opening scene. The setup field stays the short choice. SQLite `setting_templates` stores the sentence that says what that choice means, and the same text is copied onto the playthrough. A new game always stores a fallback. A cloud story provider may replace that fallback once, before the first scene, and only when the returned rule names the choice. `AI_RPG_SETTING_TEMPLATES=0` keeps the fallback and does not call a model. Local model servers are not started for this write — `app/setting_templates.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/prompts.py`, `app/turn_dsl.py`, `tests/test_setting_templates.py`
  - A player list is one preset per label. `common, uncommon, rare, epic, legendary, unique and unknown` is seven rungs, low to high. The word `and` splits labels. A built-in phrase such as `earned and uncommon` stays one choice. A model rule that drops a label, or glues two neighboring rungs with `and`, is discarded and the engine preset is stored. Combat ranks use the same split.
  - The catalog covers rank, economy, quests, factions, NPC stats and skills, density, difficulty, loot, magic, death, narration, skill style, tone, tech, check difficulty, system style, leveling, the in-world window, proficiencies, dice checks, event checks, encounter checks, new-skill frequency, proficiency access, and world peoples.
- [GROK] Each world theme grows its own ground. Deep Caverns is cavern, mushroom, crystal, lava, water, and cliff, with wet limestone, glow-fungus, raw crystal, basalt, and black water. The other themes have their own bands and materials. A people-kind is a leaning for a stretch of ground, not a census, and a stretch may be empty. Deep Caverns leans toward creatures that lurk in the dark and dwarves. A saved map keeps the bands it was given. A map with none uses the theme's bands — `app/world_scale.py`, `app/tile_world.py`, `static/app.js`, `tests/test_world_scale.py`
- [GROK] Setup screen. The world-preset card folds the same way character art does. The two world-map views are labeled, side by side on a wide window and stacked on a phone. Start new game rolls a seed and asks for a map at that seed. The older 36 by 36 board stays available as the legacy map — `static/index.html`, `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] Hidden NPC psychology layer. A narrator-only system that tracks the deep inner life of NPCs — private emotional states, hidden agendas, and family ties that drive behaviour the player can never directly observe. Three new tables (`npc_private_feelings`, `npc_agendas`, `npc_family_ties`) hold the data; `app/npc_psychology.py` is the full logic module. The player sees only the existing affinity/fear/respect badges; the psychology layer is purely narrator-side.
  - `npc_private_feelings` records feelings like ROMANTIC_INTEREST, RESENTMENT, SUSPICION, ADMIRATION, JEALOUSY, GRIEF, and LOYALTY_CONFLICT, each with an intensity (−100 to +100), a target (player or another NPC), a trigger event, and an `is_revealed` flag.
  - `npc_agendas` records hidden goals (REVENGE, PROTECTION, MANIPULATION, SEDUCTION, SABOTAGE, DEFECTION) with a priority 1–10, current state (ACTIVE / COMPLETED / ABANDONED / PAUSED), and free metadata.
  - `npc_family_ties` links existing NPCs by kinship (PARENT, CHILD, SIBLING, SPOUSE, RIVAL_KIN) with an `is_known_to_player` flag. Only links to NPCs already in the world — never generates filler NPCs.
  - `trigger_private_feeling()` creates or accumulates a feeling; `get_npc_psychology_context()` produces a compact narrator-only prose block; `check_agenda_triggers()` probabilistically fires agendas each turn; `try_reveal_feeling()` decides whether an NPC discloses a feeling based on warmth, role, and personality.
  - Wired into `app/world.py` `apply_turn()`: before `generate_turn`, the psychology block for all NPCs in the current scene is injected into `prompt_context["npc_psychology_context"]` (narrator system prompt only). After the turn, `check_agenda_triggers()` runs for scene NPCs — if an agenda fires, a `narrator_hint` journal entry is written. On NPC kill in `_apply_deterministic_combat`, `on_npc_killed()` cascades RESENTMENT (−60 to −90) and a REVENGE agenda to surviving family members via `npc_family_ties`.
  - Wired into `app/relationships.py` `update_relationship()`: when `|affinity_delta| > 20`, probabilistically auto-seeds a private feeling — big gain may seed ADMIRATION or ROMANTIC_INTEREST; big loss may seed RESENTMENT or SUSPICION.
  - Three demonstration scenarios seeded via `seed_demo_psychology()` / `POST /api/npc/psychology/seed-demo`: (A) merchant with RESENTMENT −75 and a REVENGE agenda, (B) guard with ROMANTIC_INTEREST +42, (C) innkeeper with GRIEF and a hidden SIBLING family tie — `app/npc_psychology.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/relationships.py`, `app/world.py`, `app/main.py` (`GET /api/npc/{npc_id}/psychology`, `POST /api/npc/psychology/seed-demo`)
- [CLAUDE] Fight / combat state and initiate-fight flow. NPCs can be placed into combat from the UI or by the `initiate_fight` function. Ambient `fight_nearby` events fire as full combat turns during Wait and Rest. Fight state persists in the world and is cleared on resolution — `app/world.py`, `static/app.js`
- [CLAUDE] Quest / Objectives system. Quests are chains of up to 6 steps with optional timers and hidden sub-tasks that reveal on arrival. Each quest has title, description, per-step objectives, difficulty, and rewards (gold + XP + optional items). `create_quest`, `advance_quest_step`, `tick_quest_timers`, and `seed_starter_quests` are the public API; three starter quests (delivery, investigation chain, timed errand) seed an empty world. Active quests are injected into the LLM context each turn so the narrator knows what the player is trying to accomplish — `app/quests.py`, `app/db.py` (quests + quest_steps tables), `app/main.py` (`GET /api/quests`, `POST /api/quests/{id}/advance`, `POST /api/quests/seed`), `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] In-browser quest creation form. The Quests tab now includes a form for authoring new quests directly in the UI: title, description, difficulty, rewards, and up to six steps each with title, description, optional location, and hidden-until-step flag. Submits to the backend quest API — `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] Titles system. Players earn titles (e.g. Dragonslayer, Peacekeeper, Wanderer) by meeting in-game criteria that are checked automatically at end of each turn. Fifteen starter titles are seeded on first run; each carries an optional `stat_bonuses_json` that is added to the player's effective stats when `titles_enabled` is true. A hidden-title flag lets GM tools assign titles the player cannot see. New journal entries announce each new title — `app/titles.py`, `app/db.py` (titles + entity_titles tables), `app/main.py` (`GET /api/titles`, `GET /api/titles/all`, `POST /api/settings/titles`), `app/world.py`, `static/app.js` (title chip badges below player stats), `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] Party system. Friendly+ NPCs (affinity ≥ 40) can be invited into the player's travelling party. Party members contribute a flat combat damage bonus each turn (guard: 6+level, companion: 3+level, guide: 1+level), and their morale (0–100) shifts with events — +15 on quest complete, −10 when the player is near death, −2 idle each turn. A member whose morale reaches 0 leaves with a narrative reason and a −10 affinity penalty; dismissing a member manually also costs −10 affinity. Departed members and new titles are written to the journal each turn — `app/party.py`, `app/db.py` (party_members table), `app/main.py` (`GET /api/party`, `POST /api/party/invite`, `POST /api/party/remove`), `app/world.py`, `static/app.js` (Party panel in character sheet sidebar, invite button on NPC cards), `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] NPC-player relationship system. Each NPC tracks three axes toward the player: affinity (−100 Hostile to +100 Devoted), fear (0–100 Terrified), and respect (0–100 Revered). Named bands are stored and injected into the LLM context so the narrator can write dialogue and behaviour consistent with history. Twelve preset event deltas cover the common cases (helped, attacked, saved life, quest completed/failed, intimidated, bribed, caught lying, killed a friend). The `npc_player_relationships` table persists between turns — `app/relationships.py`, `app/db.py`, `app/main.py` (`GET /api/relationships`, `GET /api/relationships/{npc_id}`, `POST /api/relationships/{npc_id}/update`), `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Relationship badges on NPC entity cards. The People tab now shows relationship band badges inline on each NPC card — coloured chips for affinity, fear, and respect bands — so the player can see standing at a glance without opening the API — `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] LLM context enrichment. Every turn now includes three additional blocks from `app/world_context.py`: a cultural naming guide (Nordic, Eastern, medieval English, Arabic, Slavic patterns with sample names), a named loot reference (28 items across four rarities, each with a flavour description), and an ability registry (20 abilities across combat, social, stealth, craft, medicine, survival, and arcane domains). The intent is to reduce invented names that drift between turns and keep generated loot and abilities consistent with the world's existing vocabulary — `app/world_context.py`, `app/prompts.py`
- [CLAUDE] Quest timer tick wired into `apply_turn`. `tick_quest_timers` is now called at the end of every turn so timed quests auto-fail when `turns_remaining` reaches zero. The call is in a `try/except` so a missing table on an older DB does not break the turn — `app/world.py`
- [CLAUDE] Autonomous player agent (`player_agent.py`). A standalone script that runs a full play session using the same local 8B model in both roles: the server narrates, and the agent acts as the player, seeing only the narrative text and its character sheet. The player role receives no world-state JSON, no entity codes, and no GM context — `player_agent.py`
- [CLAUDE] Lore Bible in player agent. The agent maintains an entity tracker built from the narrative it has seen. Each turn it parses the narration for named people, places, and objects and accumulates them into a running "What you know so far:" block injected into the player's context. The agent knows only what the prose has told it — `player_agent.py`
- [CLAUDE] Player agent goal directive and debug command blocker. The agent receives a goal directive at session start and pursues it across turns. A command filter blocks meta-commands and debug strings from appearing in the agent's player action output, so the narrator never receives OOC text — `player_agent.py`

### Added — In Progress

> The following systems are partially implemented. APIs and data models are in place but UI, auto-wiring, and balance tuning are incomplete. Saves created now will carry the schema; the systems will be finished in subsequent patches.

- [CLAUDE] **Titles system** *(IN PROGRESS)*. Earnable titles with stat bonuses. Players accumulate titles through play (deeds, quest outcomes, combat wins). Each title carries a short description and one or more stat modifiers. A pre-game toggle lets the narrator offer titles explicitly. Schema and seeding in place; award logic and UI integration incomplete — `app/titles.py`
- [CLAUDE] **Party system** *(IN PROGRESS)*. Invite NPCs into the player's party and remove them. Party members contribute morale and combat bonuses. Morale tracks across events (loss in combat, quest completion, rest). Schema and invite/remove API in place; morale propagation to combat and narrator context incomplete — `app/party.py`, `app/main.py`
- [CLAUDE] **Hidden NPC psychology** *(IN PROGRESS)*. Private feelings, hidden agendas, and family ties stored per NPC as narrator-only context — never sent to the player's prompt, only to the GM prompt block. Lets the narrator write consistent subtext and dramatic irony. Schema in place; injection into narrator context and authoring UI incomplete — `app/db.py`, `app/world.py`

## [0.10.0-dev.1] - 2026-08-29

> Pre-release on the way to `0.10.0`. First 0.10 work after 0.9.12: the save editor, separate reincarnated and transmigrated packages, magic-aware rolls, UI fixes.

### Added — In Progress

> The following systems are partially implemented. APIs and data models are in place but UI, auto-wiring, and balance tuning are incomplete. Saves created now will carry the schema; the systems will be finished in subsequent patches.

- [CLAUDE] A save file can now be edited, by a person in the browser and by an agent over HTTP, through one API. Neither could before: of 79 mutating endpoints none touched a save's contents, the closest being `/api/import` (which replaces the entire world) and save / load / delete. Changing one item's quantity meant finding `data/campaign_slots/<slot>/world.json`, learning its shape, parsing five megabytes of JSON and writing it back with a hand-made backup — which is exactly what trimming a duplicated seed from 3 to 1 took earlier the same day. `GET /api/save-editor/{slot}` returns the editable tables, their columns and what was left out; `POST` takes a list of `set` / `delete` / `insert` edits - `app/save_editor.py`, `app/main.py`, `tests/test_save_editor.py`
  - The browser editor and an agent run the same module deliberately. Two implementations of "what may be edited" would eventually disagree, and the one that disagreed quietly would be the one writing to a save file.
  - All or nothing: every edit is validated against an in-memory copy and the file is rewritten only once all of them succeed. A save half-edited by a typo in the fourth operation is worse than a refusal. A `where` that matches nothing is an error rather than a no-op, `where` is required for `set` and `delete` (its absence would mean every row), a backup is written before the file is touched, and `dry_run` reports what would change including the previous values.
  - `world_maps` and `settings` are refused. They are generated tile data and base64 character art, they are 99% of a save's bytes, and a malformed edit to either breaks the map or the portraits with no useful error. They are reported by name and row count rather than hidden, because a caller that cannot see a table exists cannot tell "not editable" from "not there".
- [CLAUDE] The main menu has an **Edit a save** button, and every save in the save browser has an **Edit** button. Either opens a grid of that save's tables where values can be changed, rows deleted and rows added; changes queue up and nothing is written until you apply. The confirm dialog is built from the server's own dry run rather than from what the page believes, so the number in the dialog is the number that lands - `static/app.js`, `static/index.html`, `static/styles.css`, `tools/test_save_editor_ui.js`
  - The main-menu route opens the browser in a third mode beside load and save. Editing was reachable only two levels into **Load**, behind a button that is not what you came for, and pressing Load to edit a save is the kind of step people do not find. In edit mode the row's own click opens the editor rather than making the small button the only target, and Load / Overwrite are dropped from the row so the one thing that screen is for is the obvious one.
  - Three decisions are made in the page before anything is sent, and each can be wrong in a way the server cannot see. Which row an edit points at: the API applies a `set` to every row its `where` matches, so a clause fitting two rows changes the wrong one and reports success — a row with nothing unique about it is shown read-only rather than guessed at. What type a value keeps: every field hands back a string, and writing `"1"` where the save held `1` would turn a number column into strings one edit at a time, so a value that cannot keep its type is refused and the field marked rather than converted. And what the confirm says. The harness covers all three, and each is checked by breaking it on purpose: eleven deliberate regressions, eleven caught.
  - Two warnings sit above the grid because both are ways to believe an edit landed when it did not. Editing a save does not change the game in progress — the live world is `data/world.db` and the slot has to be loaded — so a **Load this save now** button appears once a write succeeds. And an autosave slot is overwritten by the next turn, so it says to use a named slot instead.
  - Verified end to end against a copy of a real save: 22 editable tables, both generated tables blocked with their row counts, quantity written back as `7` and not `"7"`, a backup holding the value from before, all 25 `world_maps` rows and their 133,337-character tiles intact afterwards, and every other table byte-identical.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] Reincarnated and transmigrated are now separate packages. They were the same one: a reincarnated character with a modern-job backstory came out **byte-identical** to a transmigrated one — *"Before the transfer they were a hospital logistics technician in Osaka… when awareness returned they were at Sect Outer Court"* — with a note reading "Transmigrated package enforced", while `backstory_mode` still said `reincarnated`. The codebase's own definition (`app/llm.py`: "reincarnated = grew up in this world + former-life fragments") was never applied. Reincarnated now routes to the local-life rewrite it was always meant to get: a local vocation, faint otherworld memory, no transport and no arrival beat. Transmigrated keeps its transfer beat unchanged - `app/starter_logic.py`, `tests/test_origin_backstory.py`
  - Three discarded values, one branch. `harmonize_identity_to_world_vibe` **accepted** the caller's arrival classification as `arrival=` and never read it again; it computed `out_mode` as transmigrated-or-reincarnated and then passed the literal `mode="transmigrated"` to `ensure_isekai_arrival_beat`, walking past that helper's own guard (`app/setup_composer.py:5569`) which returns the story untouched for a reincarnated life; and its fallback prose and note were hardcoded transmigration regardless.
  - The mode now settles residence on its own. `lived_here_long` required the *story* to also say "village" or "childhood" before believing `reincarnated` — but a reincarnated character whose backstory is still a modern CV is exactly the case needing the rewrite, so requiring the marker up front excluded it. A stray "woke in another world" sentence no longer overrides the mode either: rebirth is the claim, the arrival line is the leftover.
  - Found by an AST sweep for this repo's recurring shape — a value computed, then thrown away — not by playing. Of 30 candidates it flagged, this was the one real defect; the private-key hits were all browser→server contract keys written in `app.js`, which is the discrimination that isolated `_magic_level` below as the only key with no writer anywhere.
- [CLAUDE] The magic setting now decides whether magic shows up in a rolled character. `pick_seed_skill_domain` had no `magic_level` parameter at all, so the four supernatural lanes — arcane, summon, necro, hybrid, 66 of the 189 seed domains — were on the table in every world, and none of them were favoured in a world built around magic. Over 400 offline rolls per setup: magic switched **off** produced at least one supernatural ability **66%** of the time, `rare` 68%, and `cultivation` 69%. The dropdown was inert in both directions, and a gritty siege with magic off was as likely to hand out Grave Chill as a cultivation sect. The same 400-roll measurement now reads **0% / 68% / 80%** - `app/setup_composer.py`, `app/llm.py`, `app/setup_crosscheck.py`, `tests/test_magic_coherence.py`
  - Three layers, because one filter is easy to route around later: `magic_lane_stance()` maps the setting to `off` / `rare` / `common`; `off` removes the supernatural lanes from the pool outright; the lane die is reweighted per stance, ×3 toward magic in a high-magic world. An unset `magic_level` resolves to `rare`, so every caller that does not know the setup behaves exactly as before.
  - `race_magic_enabled` reopens the lanes when ambient magic is `none`, since an ancestry can carry magic a world otherwise lacks.
  - A style branch could still hand back an all-mundane shortlist and mute a high-magic world — "grim industrial city" steers to mundane/support/tool. High magic now tops such a shortlist back up, taking that case from 0.278 to 0.463.
  - Reported as "why is there no magic abilities if magic is on?". The first measurement of this said the opposite — that magic almost never appeared — because it matched ability text against words like "mana" and "rune" and so missed Candle Whisper, Shadow Pup and Grave Chill. The pool's own `lane` field is ground truth and was there the whole time; the corrected reading is that magic appeared constantly and the setting simply never entered the calculation.
- [CLAUDE] An ability can no longer cost mana in a world that grants none. `diversify_ability_costs` decided this from a per-ability `_magic_level` key that nothing in the codebase has ever written — its only appearance was the read. `magic_allows_mana("")` is False, so the computed answer was False in every world, and the call worked around its own broken input by passing a hardcoded `True`. It now takes `magic_ok` from the caller's setup, which `app/setup_crosscheck.py:291` was already doing correctly two files over. The predicate is the same one that sets `max_mana`, so the fix also closes the gap where an ability could charge a resource the player's pool could not hold - `app/llm.py`, `app/setup_crosscheck.py`
- [CLAUDE] The `hidden` attribute now actually hides. The browser's rule for it is `[hidden] { display: none }` in the UA stylesheet, which loses to any author rule — so `.saveBrowserList { display: grid }` and `.saveBrowserFooter { display: flex }` kept both on screen with `el.hidden === true`, and the save row and the "Save here" footer showed through the save editor. Four elements across the document were in that state; a third is the setup wizard's system-style field, which `updateSimpleRulesVisibility()` hides with `wrap.hidden = !gameOn` and which has been showing with the game system switched off - `static/styles.css`
  - The probe that should have caught it asserted `listEl.hidden === true`, the property, which was always true. Checks here now read `getComputedStyle().display` and the bounding box, which is the question that was actually being asked.
- [CLAUDE] Dragging a save editor column border now moves it by exactly as much as the cursor. The table carried `min-width: 100%`, so a table narrower than the panel was stretched to fill it and the extra was shared across the real columns — a header recorded as 173px could be drawn at 320px, and the first pixel of a drag snapped it to whatever it had been stretched to. The slack now goes to a spacer column with no width of its own, so what a column is drawn at is what it is recorded as. Measured drift over a 1px nudge, a 150px drag, an 80px drag left and a drag on an already-overflowing table: zero in all four - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] The table list's scrollbar no longer sits on top of the buttons. `scrollbar-gutter: stable` reserves its lane, and the buttons shrink to fit it — they did not before, because a wrapping flex column sizes its line to the widest item, so all twenty-two stretched to fit `inventory_capacity_modifiers` and hung past the list. Long names truncate with the full name on hover - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] The save editor has a search box above the table list, and it searches every table. It filtered only the table you happened to have open, which answers "is it here" when the question is "where is it" — searching `seed` from `abilities` found nothing while nine tables held it. The box sits at the top of the side column so the list of tables directly beneath it is the result: each table that holds a match carries its count, tables without one are dimmed, and a line between them reads `13 rows in 9 tables · 1 here`. Clicking one goes there with the term still in hand. The box stays put while the list scrolls - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`, `tools/test_save_editor_ui.js`
  - Matches are marked in the grid too: the matched cells, the row behind them, and the column that identifies the row — so a hit on `seed` is read against the `id` that says which row it is in rather than floating on its own.
  - Row values are indexed once per read into one lowercase string per row, joined on NUL. A keystroke over a synthetic save of 22,000 rows costs 0.57ms; a real one, 0.0095ms. NUL rather than a space because the side list's count and the grid's filter must agree exactly — joining on a space would let `seed in hand` match a row whose one column ends `seed` and whose next begins `in hand`, and the count would send you to a table the grid then shows as empty. The harness checks that agreement over six terms and every table.
- [CLAUDE] Save editor columns can be resized. Drag the grip on a column header, or double-click it to fit the column to its contents; **Reset widths** puts a table back. Widths are remembered per table in `localStorage`, so a `description` widened once stays wide for every character's save. Starting widths come from the content rather than an even split — sampled over the first sixty rows, since `model_logs` runs to thousands and the width is recomputed on every render, including every keystroke in the filter box - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`, `tools/test_save_editor_ui.js`
  - The table also moved from auto layout to `table-layout: fixed` with an explicit `colgroup`. Auto layout divides the panel between however many columns exist, which left nothing to drag and no way to say how wide a column should be.
  - Clicking a table in the side list no longer throws that list back to the top. Every render replaces the whole editor, which resets the scroll of everything inside it; scrolling down to reach `player_skills` and landing back at `abilities` made the list unusable past the first screen. The grid keeps its sideways scroll across a resize for the same reason, and starts fresh only when the table actually changes.
- [CLAUDE] The save editor uses the screen. It opened in the save browser's 640px dialog widened to 1080px, which on a large monitor is a small window with the table squeezed into it: twenty-two columns shared the width until a name read "threadbare sca…", and the grid itself got 197px of height — about four rows — after the wrapped table chips, the notes, the footer and an empty queue box had taken theirs. The panel is now up to 1900px and 94vh, tables are listed down a side column instead of wrapped across the top, columns size to their contents and the table scrolls sideways, and the queue box stays one line until something is in it. At 1920×1080 the grid went from 133px to 652px — eleven rows and nine columns visible at once - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
  - The "Apply changes" button was styled `mainMenuPrimary`, which is `width: 100%` for a main-menu column. It stretched to 947px and wrapped its own toolbar onto three lines. It has the accent without the width now.
- [CLAUDE] Entity names in narration are no longer wrapped twice. `7bc20fc` changed inline references from `<button>` to `<span>` so they would copy with the prose around them, and left one guard behind: `linkifyKnownEntityNames` protects existing links by splitting on `/(<button\b[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/button>|<[^>]+>)/`, which makes a whole button atomic but matches only a span's *tags* — so the label between them came back as plain text and was linked a second time. It reads correctly on screen and the click still lands, which is why nobody reported it - `static/app.js`
  - `tools/test_entity_ref_doubling.js` exists to catch this and did not, because the same commit broke the harness: its extractor decides regex-versus-division from the single preceding character, a reformatted line put a regex behind an indent space, the `'` in the following character class opened a string, and the extraction ran on for 14,646 lines until it swallowed a top-level `document.addEventListener`. It had been exiting 2 with "document is not defined" ever since, and an erroring gate reports nothing. It now looks back past whitespace, and fails on nesting directly rather than inferring it from repeated text.

## [0.9.12] - 2026-08-28

Second hotfix on the 0.9.1 line, cut from the first external playtest. Five fixes, all from things a player actually saw. No save-format change; existing saves carry over. Tag `v0.9.12`.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] A turn no longer says the same sentence twice. A reported caravanserai scene wrote four paragraphs whose last two were built almost entirely from sentences already used above them; 838 of its 1587 characters were repeats. The existing guard caught none of it, because it compares a paragraph only with the one directly above it and scores whole paragraphs — two paragraphs sharing a forty-word clause verbatim still measured 45%, under the 48% bar. The new pass works on sentences, against everything said so far in the turn, and counts eight consecutive shared words as a repeat even when no whole sentence matches (a threshold set from two reported turns, whose repeated clauses ran 8 and 9 words at a token overlap of only 0.34 and 0.54) - `app/narration_pipeline.py`, `app/llm.py`
  - It runs in `_normalize_turn` rather than in the narration pipeline, because `pipeline_enabled()` is `False` unless the launcher sets `AI_RPG_NARRATION_PIPELINE` — anyone running the server directly had no repeat guard at all.
- [CLAUDE] A shortened list is no longer presented as the whole of anything. Two places did it. `mechanics_context.player_inventory_codes` is capped at 40 while the system prompt called it "the only gear the player currently holds" and told the narrator that when the player searches their pockets "only listed items exist" — so past 40 items the player owns something and the narrator is instructed it does not exist. Reachable rather than theoretical: `_inventory_summary` sets `slot_capacity` to `None` once a dimensional container is equipped, so nothing bounds the count. The list still gets capped, but now says when it was cut and names `world_state.inventory` as the complete record - `app/world.py`, `app/prompts.py`
  - The same shape in prose: the deterministic fallback opened with "What you still carry is plain: …" built from `inventory[:6]`, stating a six-item summary as a complete account of the pack. It now claims completeness only when the list is complete, and says "Among what you still carry" otherwise - `app/llm.py`
- [CLAUDE] The narrator is now told that the recorded wardrobe is the whole wardrobe. Reported from play: "the cold metal biting through your gloves", written for a character whose recorded appearance is `torso: patched overshirt; feet: scuffed boots; bag: leather satchel` — nothing on the hands. The data was never missing; `build_user_prompt` sends `playthrough_options` whole. The gap was in the rule beside it, which declares `player_inventory_codes` the only gear the player holds and illustrates it with pocket items and keys — clothing is not inventory here, so worn gear fell outside both the scope and the examples. `check_unowned_gear()` measures how often it still happens and reports into `gear_check` - `app/prompts.py`, `app/world.py`
  - Reports only; no prose is rewritten. Cutting a clause out of a finished sentence breaks more than it fixes, which is the same reason `check_narrative_voice` counts pronoun drift without correcting it.
  - The garment vocabulary is the image pipeline's existing wardrobe table rather than a second list, because a second list is how the theme keyword tables drifted apart.
- [CLAUDE] Entity names copy along with the prose around them. Copying a scene returned the paragraph with holes exactly where the location and NPC names were, those being the only words rendered as `<button>` elements. Inline references are now `<span role="link">`, which selects like the text on either side of it; Enter/Space activation is handed back explicitly - `static/app.js`, `static/styles.css`
- [CLAUDE] The `?v=` cache token on `app.js` and `styles.css` is now computed from those files' contents and substituted into the page when it is served, instead of being a version string copied into `static/index.html` by hand. Nothing moved the hand-written one when the version moved, and for 0.9.11 it did not: browsers that had already loaded the game re-requested the URL they had cached and the release reached them as nothing, while the deploy looked perfect from the server. The overlay clamp in `78e9164` never arrived for anyone who had loaded the game before it - `app/main.py`, `static/index.html`, `tests/test_asset_cache_token.py`
  - An intermediate version of this asserted `token == APP_VERSION`, which is narrower than it looks: it says nothing about whether the token moved when `app.js` moved, so a second fix at the same version would have gone missing the same way with the suite green. Deriving the token from the bytes leaves no rule to follow.
  - `index.html` is served with `Cache-Control: no-cache` and an ETag, because a page held in cache would name an old token and buy nothing. The assets it points at stay freely cacheable — their URLs change with them, which is the whole point.

## [0.9.11] - 2026-08-27

Patch on top of `0.9.1-wip`, cut while the first external playtest was already running. One UI fix; no gameplay change, no save-format change. Tag `v0.9.11`.

### Fixed

- [CLAUDE] The item hover overlay no longer hangs off the bottom of the screen. `positionFloatingOverlay` chose above-or-below and then clamped only the top edge, so a row sitting low in a scrolling inventory could open a ~240px card that ran past the viewport. The function was written for the one-line help tooltip, where a missing bottom clamp never showed. Now clamped on both edges, verified in the browser against a real save with the anchor at the top of the viewport, the middle, six pixels from the bottom, and past the bottom edge - `static/app.js`
